# Tests and public interface

The tests pin the CLI, report and file formats described below. See the development notes for
[unpublished references and recipe paths](../docs/development.md#unpublished-references).

- **Short forms:** `04` is `04-workflow-and-composition.md`, `05` is `05-evaluation-and-lineage.md`, and `VP` is `verification-plan.md`. Line numbers are those of the design revision the test was written against (revision 9 or later) and may have shifted since; sections and rule ids are the stable references.
- **Pinned clones:** NeMo RL `4f39fd8`, NeMo Evaluator `ea35ba3`. Every line cited from them was re-checked.

## 1. Environment and how to run

- **Environment:** `.venv` at the repository root. Use the pip setup in the [repository README](../README.md#try-it) or, on macOS, `uv sync --python 3.13 --python-preference only-managed --locked --extra tier-a`.
  - Python 3.13.13.
  - Pinned in `uv.lock`: numpy 2.5.4, scipy 1.18.1, pyarrow 26.0.1, duckdb 2.0.0, pyyaml 6.0.3, torch 2.15.0.
  - torch is the macOS arm64 PyPI wheel, a CPU build: `torch.version.cuda` is None.
  - The lock covers macOS only; see gap G-11.
- **Package:** installed editable with `pip install -e '.[tier-a]'` (module `gatedtrain` under `src/`). The CLI runs as `python -m gatedtrain`.
- **Full suite** (from the repository root): `.venv/bin/python -m unittest discover -s tests -t tests` (add `-v` for one line per test).
- **Classified run:** `.venv/bin/python tests/classify_failures.py` sorts every failure into MISSING_MODULE, MISSING_FILE or OTHER. OTHER means a test bug.
- **Slow tests:** the classes named `Slow*` in `test_e2e_slow.py` are the end-to-end runs.
  - `GATEDTRAIN_SKIP_SLOW=1` skips them.
  - `GATEDTRAIN_KEEP_TMP=1` keeps the temporary workspace for inspection.
- **Container user:** the launch fixtures run the container as the user running the tests and its primary group
  (`_ext.container_user`, EXT-63); as root, for a uid without a user database entry or a primary group that owns a
  Docker socket those tests skip, and with `GATEDTRAIN_REQUIRE_CONTAINER_USER=1` (set in CI) they fail instead.
- **Design set and pinned clones:** the tests read them only from the environment variables below (`tests/_support.py`); there are no defaults. A test that needs a variable that is unset or empty skips and names the variable (a missing directory: the path).

  | Variable | Root | Expected content |
  |---|---|---|
  | `GATEDTRAIN_DESIGN_DIR` | design set | the design set `gatedtrain-design` |
  | `GATEDTRAIN_NEMO_EVALUATOR_DIR` | NeMo Evaluator clone | a NeMo Evaluator clone at `ea35ba36756abd59de201f60af8a2feba014744d` |
  | `GATEDTRAIN_NEMO_RL_DIR` | NeMo RL clone | a NeMo RL clone at `4f39fd86289a2ffd5aaf5d202702f7fd7185c924` |

  - The variables only change where tests read; nothing is written there.
- **Host data:** tests that read real run records or data of a host read their location from `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_TIERB_ROOT` (`<tierb-root>`, the Tier B host work directory) or `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_MM_ROOT` (`<mm-root>`, the multimodal recipes' runtime and output root) and skip when it is unset. This document cites files there as `<tierb-root>/...` and `<mm-root>/...`, and schema ids written by a recipe runtime as `<runtime>.<name>`.
- **Adapters:** gatedtrain registers no adapter. The external-run tests (`test_ext_*.py`, `test_conformance.py`) run on the synthetic test adapter `gatedtrain.testing.synthetic` (adapter `synthetic`, trainer `rl.synthetic@0.1`, evaluator `synthetic.items@0.1`), which `_support.py` registers in process and whose module runs the CLI subprocesses (`python -m gatedtrain.testing.synthetic <args>`); no recipe needs to be installed. With recipe plugins installed, `test_conformance.py` and `test_ext_registry.TestRegistryRows.test_docs_tables_match` also check them.
- **Recipes:** each recipe's adapter, registry rows and adapter-specific tests live in its own repository (section 11.28); sections 11.18 to 11.27 keep the requirements as they were written, and their test tables name the test files and helpers of that time.
- **Time budget:** MVP-SPEC §1 asks for the whole suite in under 15 minutes on CPU; with the demo's portable CPU kernels (`docs/findings.md`, "The CPU demo across machines") it takes about 25 minutes on a desktop CPU.
  - The slow tests make one warm-up and six full runs, plus a second warm-up for the determinism check.
  - Each `gatedtrain run` of a demo spec takes about 2 minutes on a desktop CPU, and longer on a shared CI runner.
  - Each CLI call has a hard limit of 600 s (`_support.RUN_TIMEOUT_S`).
- **One `uv` command at a time** in this environment (MVP-SPEC §1).

## 2. CLI (`gatedtrain` = `python -m gatedtrain`)

| Command | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `gatedtrain warmup <warmup.yaml> --out <dir>` | Train the baseline checkpoint (skeleton-stub) into `<dir>`. Refuses if `<dir>` exists. Relative paths are resolved against the current directory, and the config's `data` path against the config file's directory. Deterministic: the same config gives the same manifest hash. |
| `gatedtrain validate <spec.yaml>` | Checks the spec and its registration only (§3). Does not need the baseline or the data. Prints `ok` on success. |
| `gatedtrain run <spec.yaml> [--runs-root DIR]` | Validates, then runs the whole path (MVP-SPEC §2). `DIR` defaults to `./runs`. Creates a **new** directory `DIR/<run_id>` and never reuses or overwrites one. Prints the line `run_dir: <absolute path>` on stdout, then the text gate report (§5). |
| `gatedtrain impact <sha256:hex \| shard path> (--run-dir DIR \| --runs-root DIR) [--json]` | Impact query (05 §11.3) over the ledger and lineage of one run, or of all runs under a root. A path is hashed as `sha256:` + SHA-256 of the file bytes. `--json` prints `{"query": {...}, "results": [...]}` (§6). |

Exit codes:

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | Success. For `run`, the gate status is PASS. For `impact`, success includes an empty result. |
| 1 | `run` completed and wrote the report, but the gate status is FAIL, INCONCLUSIVE or MISSING. |
| 2 | Usage error (argparse). Under the top-level `--json` it is the AN-1 object (rule `USAGE`), exit 2. An uncaught exception under `--json` is the AN-1 object with rule `INTERNAL` and exit 1 (the code a traceback gives); the rule tells it from a gate that did not pass. |
| 3 | Refused: a spec or registration error, a hash mismatch, an existing `--out`, or an unknown impact target. For `run`, the refusal happens before the run directory is created. |

Paths named by `file:<path>` URIs are relative to the spec file's directory. Validation behaves the same whatever the current directory is.

## 3. Errors (MVP-SPEC §2.1, §5 "Errors")

Every spec or registration error is printed on stderr as one line:

```
error[<RULE>] <field>: <message> | fix: <fix>
```

- `<field>` is a dotted path with list indices, for example `stages[1].test.power`.
- `<fix>` says what to change.
- Several errors may be printed. The tests look for the expected (rule, field) pair among them.

| Rule | Field in the test | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `SPEC-SUBSET` | the unknown key's path (`notes`, `stages[0].learning_rate`, `stages[1].test.n_boot`, `artifacts.train.shards`) | 04 §3 grammar (04:36-72): no field the 04 §3 grammar lacks |
| `SPEC-SHAPE` | `stages` | one stage and one gate (MVP-SPEC §2.1) |
| `SPEC-REF` | `stages[i].inputs.<role>` | typed artifacts consumed by name (04:80) |
| `GATE-REQUIRED` | `stages[i].test.<param>` | 04 §4.2 table (04:90-96) |
| `GATE-ALPHA` | `stages[i].test.alpha` when there is neither `alpha` nor a family; `stages[i].alpha_family` when both are set | 04 §4.2 table "alpha or a family"; 04 §3 α fields (04:34) |
| `GATE-OUTCOME` | `stages[i].on_fail` | 04 §4.2 rule 3 (04:104): `reject` only inside a loop body |
| `GATE-FIELDS` | `stages[i].owner` (also `escalation`) | 04 §4.2 rule 4 (04:105) |
| `REG-MISSING` | `stages[i].inputs.registration` when the gate names no registration; `artifacts.registration.uri` when the file is missing | MVP-SPEC §2.2; 05 §3.7 (05:142) |
| `REG-HASH` | `artifacts.registration.uri`. The message shows both digests. | 05 §3.7 "a mismatching registration hash fails validation" (05:142) |
| `REG-MARGIN` | `registration.clause.margin` | 05 §3.1 "blocking: true and no declared margin is rejected" (05:82) |
| `REG-FIELD` | `registration.<key>` | 05 §3.7 registration contents (05:142) |
| `REG-SCORER` | `registration.evaluators[0].scorer` | G-21 fix B: the scorer is not on the prototype allowlist (`numeric_match` only at NeMo Evaluator ea35ba3); section 11.14 |

The stand-in gates are `gate.superiority`, `gate.noninferiority` and `gate.equivalence` (MVP-SPEC §2.7). They compile to 05 clauses as 04:98 says.

## 4. Spec, registration and demo files

### 4.1 Workflow spec

The spec is a strict subset of the 04 §3 YAML (04:36-72). The L2 fixture reads the allowed keys from the design file itself.

- **Top level:** `workflow`, `version: 1`, `budget`, `artifacts`, `stages`.
- **Artifact entry:** `{type, uri, disjoint_from}`. The URI scheme `file:<relative path>` is a naming choice. A pinned artifact appends `@sha256:<64 hex>`, the 04 §3 notation.
- **Training stage:** `{id, uses, algorithm, model_family, inputs, outputs}`.
  - `uses: sft.cpu_stub@0.1` selects the stand-in trainer backend `cpu_stub`. Tier B uses another `uses`, for example `rl.nemo_rl@0.1`.
  - Inputs: `model` (baseline checkpoint), `data` (training dataset), `config` (trainer config). Output: `model`.
- **Gate:** `{id, uses: gate.<type>@1.0, evaluator, sampler, inputs, test, on_fail, owner, escalation}`.
  - Inputs: `model` (the stage output), `baseline` (the stage's input model), `prompts`, `registration`.
  - `test` uses the 04 fields: `metric, alpha, power, mde | margin, cluster_by, seeds`.
- **Registration artifact:** `{type: registration, uri: "file:<name>@sha256:<SHA-256 of the file bytes>"}`. See gap G-2.
- **Mapping to the 05 clause** (04:98): `mde` → `planning_effect`, `cluster_by` → `unit`, `seeds` → `len(eval_seeds)`.
  - The superiority margin δ_sup comes from the registration, because 04 has no superiority margin field.

### 4.2 Registration (05 §3.7, 05:142)

The registration is a YAML file with these keys:

- `registration_version`, `registration_id`, `signed_by` (`prototype-unsigned`), `workflow`, `gate`;
- `clause`: exactly the 05 §3.1 fields (05:66-80): `type, metric, margin, alpha, power, planning_effect, sigma_plan, unit, samples_per_prompt, eval_seeds, interval, blocking`;
- `margin_justification`;
- `sigma_pilot`: `{s, df, source}` for `ucb80`, or `{sigma, source}` for `fixed`;
- `prompt_set_hash`: the manifest hash of the prompt-set directory (§4.4);
- `sampler`: `{name, temperature, max_new_tokens}`;
- `evaluators`: `[{id, manifest_hash}]`;
- `multiplicity`, `analysis_code_version` (`gatedtrain 0.3.0`, `gatedtrain.ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION`: the analysis version, not the package release; section 11.36), `bootstrap: {replicates, seed}`.

The run records the registration's SHA-256, `sha256:` + hex of the file bytes, and refuses on a mismatch.

Evaluation seeds work as common random numbers (05:97). K = `samples_per_prompt` = 4, and repeat r of every prompt uses seed `eval_seeds[r]` in both arms. Each result record carries the seed in `metadata.eval_seed`.

### 4.3 Demo files (`demo/`)

All demo files are test fixtures.

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `spec.yaml` + `registration.yaml` | E2E-pass. Superiority, δ_sup = 0, α = 0.025, power 0.8, MDE 0.10, σ plan `ucb80` with pilot s = 0.30 and df = 4, giving n_required = 172 (`test_stats_plan`). 500 prompts, K = 4. |
| `spec-no-train.yaml` | The same, with `trainer-lr0.yaml` (lr = 0). |
| `spec-few-prompts.yaml` + `registration-few-prompts.yaml` | 30 held-out prompts, below n = 172. |
| `spec-bad-train.yaml` | Trains on `data/train-shuffled` (permuted answers). Superiority. |
| `spec-bad-train-ni.yaml` + `registration-ni.yaml` | Same training. Non-inferiority, δ = 0.10, planning effect 0, n = 172. |
| `trainer.yaml`, `trainer-lr0.yaml`, `warmup.yaml` | `cpu_stub` configs. The numbers may be tuned, except that lr stays 0 in `trainer-lr0.yaml`. |
| `make_data.py`, `data/` | Deterministic 2-digit addition data (stdlib). |

The data under `data/` is laid out as follows:

- `train/` holds 5 shards of 1000 rows each.
- `train-shuffled/` holds 2 shards.
- `unused/` holds 1 shard that no stage reads.
- `warmup/` holds 1 shard.
- `heldout/` holds 500 prompts; `heldout-small/` holds the first 30.
- The held-out prompts are disjoint from all training data.
- The problem index is the 0-based line number.

If you edit a registration, put its new SHA-256 in every spec that names it. The L2 fixture checks this. Keep `.DS_Store` files out of the data directories, because they change manifest hashes.

### 4.4 Manifests and hashes (format conventions: canonical JSON per RFC 8785, content-addressed manifests)

- **Canonical JSON:** RFC 8785, canonical-JSON rules C1-C4.
- **Entries:** `{path, sha256, size}` for every regular file under the artifact root. Paths are relative, joined with `/`, and sorted by UTF-8 bytes (H1-H6). Symlinks are rejected with `ValueError`.
- **Manifest hash:** `manifest_hash = "sha256:" + hex(SHA-256(JCS(entries)))` (manifest rule H7).
- **Library:** `gatedtrain.canonical` provides `canonical_json(obj) -> bytes`, which raises `ValueError` on NaN or Inf and on integers outside ±(2⁵³−1), plus `manifest_entries(root)` and `manifest_hash(root)`.
- **Digest strings:** always `sha256:<64 lowercase hex>`.

## 5. Run directory layout and report (MVP-SPEC §2.8-§2.11)

```
<runs-root>/<run_id>/            run_id == directory name; never overwritten
  lockfile.json                  canonical JSON (below)
  plan.json                      plan() output
  registration/registration.yaml byte copy of the registration
  manifests/<name>.json          one per artifact, names below
  stage/<stage_id>/logs/train_data_step<N>.jsonl      N = 1..steps, contiguous
  stage/<stage_id>/logs/SKELETON_STUB
  stage/<stage_id>/checkpoints/step_<N>/{training_info.json, train_dataloader.pt, SKELETON_STUB, ...}
  eval/baseline/{results.jsonl, SKELETON_STUB}
  eval/candidate/{results.jsonl, SKELETON_STUB}
  ledger/consumption.parquet
  lineage/...                    edges used by the impact query (format free)
  report/gate_report.json
  report/gate_report.txt
```

### 5.1 Manifests

There are nine manifests, written to `manifests/<name>.json`. Each one has a root directory:

| Name | Root |
|---|---|
| `baseline_checkpoint` | the spec's `base` directory |
| `trained_checkpoint` | the last `step_<N>` |
| `training_shards` | the stage's `data` directory |
| `prompt_set` | the gate's `prompts` directory |
| `registration` | `registration/` |
| `eval_baseline` | `eval/baseline` |
| `eval_candidate` | `eval/candidate` |
| `gate_report` | `report/` |
| `ledger` | `ledger/` |

Each manifest document is canonical JSON with these keys:

- `artifact` (the name);
- `type`;
- `root` (absolute path, recorded but not hashed);
- `entries`;
- `manifest_hash`;
- `manifest_hash_kind: "p2_filelist_sha256"` (manifest rule H8);
- `schema_version`;
- `tags`, which includes `prototype` and `unpromotable`.

### 5.2 Lockfile

`lockfile.json` is canonical JSON (no allow-list checks). Its keys:

- `schema_version`, `tags`;
- `spec_sha256`, `registration_sha256`;
- `config_hash`: `sha256:` of the trainer config file bytes;
- `allow_list_checked: false`;
- `backend: {trainer: {...}, evaluator: {...}}`, each with `{name: "cpu_stub", package: "gatedtrain", package_version: "0.5.0", torch_version}` (`package_version` is the package release that ran, `gatedtrain.__version__`);
- `artifacts`: `{<manifest name>: manifest_hash}` for every manifest except `gate_report`.

Tier B records the container digest and the NeMo RL commit instead.

### 5.3 Gate report

`report/gate_report.json` has these keys:

- **Identity and tags:**
  - `schema_version`;
  - `tags` = `["prototype", "unpromotable", "registration: unsigned"]`;
  - `stubs`: one string per stand-in, each containing `skeleton-stub`;
  - `workflow`, `gate_id`, `metric`;
  - `hypothesis_type`: `superiority`, `non_inferiority` or `equivalence`.
- **Statistics:**
  - `status`: PASS, FAIL, INCONCLUSIVE or MISSING;
  - `delta_hat`;
  - `interval: {lower, upper, level, method}`, where `level` = 1 − 2α and `method` = `cluster_bootstrap`;
  - `margin`, `alpha_used`, `power_target`, `achieved_power`, `mde`, `mde_declared`, `underpowered`;
  - `n_prompts`, `samples_per_prompt`, `eval_seeds`, `sd_diff`, `design_effect`;
  - `plan: {n_required, mde, design_effect, sigma_plan, sigma_used, n_available, underpowered}`;
  - `baseline_score`, `candidate_score`: mean exact match over all samples.
- **Provenance:**
  - `registration_hash`, `registration_signed_by`, `analysis_code_version`;
  - `prompt_set_hash`, `evaluator_hash`, `sampler_hash`;
  - `baseline_checkpoint_hash`, `candidate_checkpoint_hash`, `lockfile_hash`.
- **Not filled by the skeleton:** `independence_tier`, `transfer_test`, `contamination` and `family_alpha`. Each has the value `not-assessed (skeleton)` (MVP-SPEC §2.11; 05:142).
- **Wall clock:** `created_at` is the only wall-clock field. Wall-clock values must not enter any manifest hash compared by the determinism test.

The text report `report/gate_report.txt`, which is also printed by `run`, has one `key: value` line per item:

- at least `status`, `delta_hat`, `interval`, `achieved_power`, `mde` and `underpowered`;
- booleans written `true` or `false`;
- one line for each of the four not-assessed keys;
- a line that contains the tags and `skeleton-stub`.

## 6. Stand-in outputs, ledger and impact query

### 6.1 Trainer `cpu_stub`

- Deterministic under its seed.
- Reads the shards in file-name order and the rows in file order, without shuffling, and wraps to the first shard after the last one.
- Writes `train_data_step<N>.jsonl` with one line per consumed sample.
  - Keys must be within the NeMo RL `log_data` set (grpo_sync.py:1429-1456) plus `idx` (logger.py:1436).
  - Keys must include `token_ids`, `rewards`, `token_loss_mask`, `sample_loss_mask` and `idx`.
  - No row or shard field: NeMo RL's file has neither, and MVP-SPEC §3 allows a shard field only if NeMo RL's loader state carries it, which it does not.
- Saves `step_<N>/training_info.json` with `consumed_samples` and `total_steps` (grpo_sync.py:1323, :1353; checkpoint.py:406-412) and `step_<N>/train_dataloader.pt` (grpo_sync.py:1407-1414; data/utils.py:69-81).
- Always checkpoints the last step.

### 6.2 Evaluator `cpu_stub`

- Writes `results.jsonl` with exactly NeMo Evaluator's per-sample keys (eval_loop.py:790-803; artifacts.py:98-102).
- `reward` is 1.0 when `model_response == expected_answer`, otherwise 0.0.
- `metadata` holds `{"eval_seed": <int>, "skeleton_stub": true}`.
- `latency_ms` must not make results differ between runs.

### 6.3 Ledger writer

`gatedtrain.ledger.write_ledger(log_dir, checkpoints_dir, dataset_dir, out_path, *, run_id, attempt_id="0") -> Path` writes Parquet rows:

- **Columns:** the 05 §12.2 fields (05:372-382).
  - `data_ref` is a struct `{snapshot, shard, row, mixture, component}` (05:361).
  - `snapshot` is the dataset `manifest_hash`; `shard` is the shard file name; `row` is null.
- **Values:** `precision = "iteration"` (see gap G-1) and `outcome = "consumed"`.
- **Granularity:** at least one row per (step, shard) that the step drew from. The step-to-shard map comes from the per-step sample counts and `consumed_samples` (tests: `_support.expected_step_shards`).

### 6.4 Impact query

`gatedtrain.impact.impact_query(shard, *, run_dir=None, runs_root=None) -> list[dict]` uses DuckDB over the ledger and the lineage edges (05 §11.3). Each result has:

- `checkpoint_hash`;
- `run_id`, `stage_id`;
- `last_step_included`;
- `precision` = `iteration`;
- `answer` = `possibly consumed` (05:350).

A checkpoint is returned exactly when `last_step_included >= first_step(shard)` (05 §12.4, 05:406-408). The baseline is never returned for a stage shard. An unused shard gives `[]`.

## 7. Library functions (`gatedtrain.gates`; 05 §3.8, 05:146-155)

| Function | Contract (tests) |
|---|---|
| `sigma_ucb80_factor(df)` | √(df / χ²₀.₂₀,df). df = 4 gives 1.5576 and df = 1 gives 3.9472 (05:130). |
| `design_effect(m, rho)` | 1 + (m − 1)ρ (05:124). |
| `mde(*, alpha, power, sigma, n, design_effect=1.0)` | (z₁₋α + z_power)·σ·√(DE/n) (05:124). |
| `plan(clause, pilot, *, n_available, cluster_size=1.0, icc=0.0)` | Returns a dict with the PLAN keys of §5.3. See the rules below the table. |
| `evaluate(clause, baseline, candidate, *, plan=None, seed, n_boot=10000)` | `baseline` and `candidate` are `{(problem_idx, repeat): record}`. Returns `hypothesis_type, delta_hat, ci_lower, ci_upper, ci_level, alpha, margin, achieved_power, power_target, mde, underpowered, status, n_prompts, sd_diff, design_effect`. See the rules below the table. |
| `combine(results, "and")` | All PASS gives PASS. Any FAIL gives FAIL. Otherwise any MISSING gives FAIL or MISSING. Otherwise any INCONCLUSIVE gives INCONCLUSIVE (05:114; NeMo Evaluator gate.py:537). |
| `load_paired_records(task_dir)` | Same result as NeMo Evaluator comparison.py:360. The test loads the upstream function from the pinned clone with `ast`. |

**`plan()`:**

- The σ used is `s·sigma_ucb80_factor(df)` for `ucb80`, or `sigma` for `fixed`.
- `n_required` = ⌈DE·((z₁₋α + z_power)·σ/d)²⌉, where d = planning_effect − margin for superiority and planning_effect + margin for non-inferiority.
- `mde` is computed at `n_available`.
- `underpowered` = `n_available < n_required`.

**`evaluate()`:**

- **Pairing:** pairs are the key intersection.
- **Paired differences:** per-prompt means over K, then candidate − baseline, on raw scores (05:97-99).
- **Interval:** prompt-resampling bootstrap, seeded by `seed` (05:105). The interval is two-sided at level 1 − 2α; its lower end is the one-sided (1 − α) bound (05:58-62).
- **Achieved power and MDE:** computed from the observed SD (gap G-3).
- **`underpowered`:** `achieved_power < power` or the plan is underpowered (`n_prompts < plan["n_required"]` or `plan["underpowered"]`).
- **Status:**
  - PASS if the pass rule holds;
  - otherwise INCONCLUSIVE if underpowered;
  - otherwise FAIL;
  - MISSING if there are no pairs (05:82, 05:155; 04:102).

### 7.1 Other modules

The modules below exist (`test_stubs_docs_tierb`):

- `gatedtrain/{__init__ (__version__ = "0.5.0", ANALYSIS_VERSION = "0.3.0"), __main__, cli, spec, registration, canonical, gates, ledger, impact, report}.py`;
- `gatedtrain/stubs/` (task, model, trainer and evaluator stand-ins, each containing `skeleton-stub`);
- `gatedtrain/backends/{nemo_rl, nemo_evaluator}.py`.

Interfaces:

- `gatedtrain.stubs.model.count_parameters(checkpoint_dir) -> int`, which must fall in [100 000, 1 000 000].
- `gatedtrain.backends.nemo_rl.build_command(stage, *, nemo_rl_dir, launcher)`:
  - `launcher="sbatch"` returns an argv that starts with `sbatch` and contains `--no-requeue`;
  - `launcher="local"` returns a command without `sbatch`;
  - any other launcher raises `ValueError`.
- `gatedtrain.backends.nemo_evaluator.read_results(task_dir)` returns the same as `load_paired_records`.
- Docs: `docs/quickstart.md`, `docs/tier-b-runbook.md`, `docs/findings.md`.

## 8. Test files

| File | Covers | Needs the implementation |
|---|---|---|
| `test_l2_skel_compat.py` | L2-E-skel-compat fixture (VP:257) | no |
| `test_demo_data.py` | Task data: ≥ 200 prompts, held-out set disjoint, reproducible | no |
| `test_stats_plan.py` | σ_U factors, design effect, MDE, `plan()` per gate type | yes |
| `test_stats_evaluate.py` | Δ̂ on raw paired differences, interval, achieved power, status rules, MISSING, `combine` | yes |
| `test_stats_bootstrap_coverage.py` | Bootstrap coverage on 400 seeded simulations | yes |
| `test_pairing.py` | `load_paired_records` matches the upstream function | yes |
| `test_canonical_manifest.py` | Canonical JSON and manifest hash (format conventions) | yes |
| `test_spec_errors.py` | Errors name the rule, the field and the fix; the demo specs validate | yes (CLI) |
| `test_ledger_unit.py` | Ledger writer on NeMo RL-shaped fixture outputs | yes |
| `test_e2e_slow.py` | E2E-pass, the three negative controls plus the NI variant, registration integrity, impact query, determinism, no-overwrite, warm-up, stub markers | yes (slow) |
| `test_stubs_docs_tierb.py` | Stub marking in code, docs, Tier B command construction and parsing | yes |
| `test_ext_sidecar.py` | EXT-190, EXT-249: the paired sidecar in process (synthetic adapter, sidecar row through `HostConfig.sidecar`): the accepted record and printed start, trainer and stop lines, each isolation refusal (image, lock, flags, network, user, limits, tmpfs, mounts, socket, credential file, spool, environment, argv), `--recheck` | yes |
| `test_ext_fill.py` | EXT-110, EXT-126, EXT-154, EXT-241: `ext-arm-fill` fill, verify, convert and refuse, the LoRA base, normalizer and environment version paths (a patched subclass of the synthetic adapter), the CLI lines | yes |
| `test_ext_rederive.py` | EXT-52: `arm.provenance_problems` re-derives merged (`ext-merge-eval`) and converted (`ext-from-nel`) arms in process | yes |
| `test_ext_sdk.py` | The adapter SDK readers, safetensors check, option parsing and `runtime_argv_problems`, `runtime_script_problems`; `rowkit`; the B18 executor limits; the NeMo RL family pins | yes |
| `test_family_flow_grpo.py` | The Flow-GRPO and diffusers LoRA family bases on a synthetic run (`MiniFlowGrpo`): cuts, identity, the observed ledger and each samples, plan, steps and cut-count refusal, target overlap, pins, the family's run checks | yes |

### 8.1 Mutants (MVP-SPEC §7) and the tests that kill them

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| Gate ignores pairing | `TestPairingNarrowsTheInterval`, `test_record_order_does_not_matter`, `test_evaluate_uses_the_paired_keys` |
| Gate uses group-normalized scores | `test_delta_hat_and_sd`, `SlowE2EPass.test_delta_matches_independent_computation` |
| Underpowered rule inverted | `TestStatusRules`, `SlowNegativeTooFewPrompts` |
| Registration hash check removed | `test_registration_changed_after_hash_recorded`, `SlowRegistrationIntegrity` |
| Ledger omits a step | `test_every_step_maps_to_its_shards`, `SlowE2EPass.test_ledger` |
| Impact query drops the precision filter or the precision | `SlowE2EPass.test_impact_used_shards`, `test_impact_unused_shards` |

## 9. Design gaps found while writing the tests

These are reported to the maintainers, not decided here. The tests take the conservative reading named in each entry.

| ID | Question | Sources | Reading the tests take | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G-1 | `ConsumptionRecord.precision` allows only `row` or `step_range` (05:381), and `data_ref.row` is a required opaque key (05:361). MVP-SPEC §2.9 asks for rows at `iteration` with no row IDs. | 05:341-350, 05:361, 05:381; MVP-SPEC §2.9 | Rows carry `precision = "iteration"` and `data_ref.row = null`. | Allow `iteration` (and a null `row`) in the record schema for trainers that report shards per iteration (05:347), or keep `iteration` on edges only. Design-owner decision. |
| G-2 | 04 §3 has no field that names a registration. Input role names such as `data`, `config` and `registration` are not in the 04 §3 example. | 04:36-72; 05:142; MVP-SPEC §2.2 | The registration is an artifact `type: registration` with `@sha256:` in its URI, used as a gate input role. Input and output role names are treated as data keys whose vocabulary belongs to the stage type. | Confirm before L2-E-skel-compat is re-run; otherwise add a `registration` field to 04 §3. |
| G-3 | "Achieved power" (04:102, 05:82) does not say whether σ is the observed SD or the planning σ. 05 §3.6 gives no power, MDE or n formula for equivalence (TOST). | 04:102; 05:82, 05:120-132 | Observed SD (ddof = 1), normal approximation, at the planned distance d. MVP-SPEC adds the plan-level condition (n < n_required means underpowered). For equivalence only the literal MDE formula is pinned; its power is left open. | Fix both in the E-stats work package. |
| G-4 | Spec and registration both state α, power, MDE or planning effect, margin and seeds. Which wins on a mismatch is unstated. | 04:98; 05:142 | No mismatch rule is tested. The L2 fixture checks that the demo files agree. | Add a validation rule: a mismatch is an error. |
| G-5 | MVP-SPEC §5 wants identical ledger hashes across two runs, but 05 §12.2 rows carry `run_id` and a `sample_id` derived from the run, and runs never overwrite each other, so the bytes cannot match. | MVP-SPEC §5; 05:374 | Ledger content is compared with `run_id`, `attempt_id`, `sample_id` and `writer_id` dropped. Checkpoint and results hashes must match exactly. | Confirm, or define a run-independent ledger content hash. |
| G-6 | The registration must hold evaluator manifest hashes (05:142), but the stand-in evaluator does not exist when the registration is written. | 05:142 | The demo registration has `manifest_hash: null` and the report records `evaluator_hash`. | Accept for the prototype; Tier B registers the NeMo Evaluator container digest. |
| G-7 | Tier B: NeMo RL's `consumed_samples` counts prompts (grpo_sync.py:1323), while `train_data_step` lines count prompts × generations. The file is not written on the nemo_gym-responses path (:1428). | grpo_sync.py:1323, :1428 | Tier A only: the stand-in writes one line per consumed sample. | The Tier B ledger writer reads `num_prompts_per_step` from the recipe config. Record this in docs/findings.md. |
| G-8 | 04 §4.2 requires `alpha` or a family on every gate, while 05 §3.2 gives a default of 0.025. | 04:92-95; 05:88 | A 04 gate without `alpha` and without a family is an error (GATE-ALPHA). The 05 default applies to clauses only. | Confirm. |
| G-9 | `combine("and")` with MISSING: NeMo Evaluator maps it to NO-GO, and E-stats has no NO-GO status. | 05:151, 05:155; gate.py:537 | FAIL or MISSING are both accepted. | To be fixed in the gate library. |
| G-10 | Budget validation (04:83) needs a cost estimate that the skeleton does not have. | 04:83 | The demo has `budget: {gpu_hours: 0}` and no estimate is made. | None for Tier A. |
| G-11 | A CPU-only torch lock for Linux needs the PyTorch CPU index, which is not PyPI. | MVP-SPEC §1 | The lock is restricted to macOS. | Maintainers' decision before a Linux CI run. |

## 10. Review round 2 additions (2026-11-09)

These follow the prototype gap decisions (G-3 revised; G-12 to G-14) and maintainers' decisions D-1 to D-5. They supersede earlier text where the two differ.

### 10.1 Loader semantics (D-1, D-2)

Section 6.1's "wraps to the first shard" is replaced by NeMo RL loader semantics: `StatefulDataLoader(..., drop_last=True)` (`nemo_rl/algorithms/grpo.py:883-890`, `drop_last=True` at `:888`, at 4f39fd8).

- When rows % batch ≠ 0, the tail rows of each epoch are skipped.
- The next epoch restarts at row 0.
- No batch straddles an epoch boundary.
- A step with zero samples maps to no shard.

The reference implementation is `_support.expected_step_shards`. It also takes `samples_per_position` (G-7), and `write_ledger(..., samples_per_position=N)` must agree with it.

### 10.2 `evaluate()` and report fields

| Field | Where | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `sd_zero` | `evaluate()` result; JSON report; text line `sd_zero: true\|false` | G-13. True when the SD of the paired differences is 0; achieved power keeps the limit 1.0. |
| `achieved_power` = null, `underpowered` = true | equivalence | G-12. A non-rejection is INCONCLUSIVE, never FAIL. |
| `unpaired_baseline`, `unpaired_candidate` | `evaluate()` result; JSON report; text lines `unpaired_baseline: N` and `unpaired_candidate: N` | D-3. Counts of (problem_idx, repeat) items present in one arm only. |
| `baseline_score`, `candidate_score` | `evaluate()` result and report | D-3. Mean reward over the paired items only. |
| warning line | text report | D-3. A line starting `warning:` that mentions `unpaired`, when either count is above 0 (`report.render_text`). |
| `disjoint_from` | JSON report; text line `disjoint_from: not-assessed (skeleton)` | D-5. Present when the spec declares `disjoint_from`. |

### 10.3 Refusals

- **G-4:** the registration is authoritative. `run` refuses with exit 3, before creating a run directory, when the spec differs from the registration. The test fields are:
  - `registration.clause.alpha`, `.power`, `.planning_effect` (spec `mde`), `.margin`, `.type` (spec `uses`) and `.eval_seeds` (spec `seeds`);
  - `registration.sampler.name`;
  - `registration.evaluators`.
- **D-4:** a registration that changes while it is copied gives exit 3 and REG-HASH, and no run directory is left behind.

### 10.4 Impact query layout

`test_impact_unit.py` writes `lineage/shards.parquet` `(run_id, stage_id, snapshot, shard, shard_sha256)` and `lineage/checkpoints.parquet` `(run_id, stage_id, checkpoint_hash, last_step_included, path)`, the layout documented in the `gatedtrain.impact` module docstring. This layout is now part of the tested interface.

### 10.5 New demo file

`demo/spec-no-train-few-prompts.yaml` combines `trainer-lr0.yaml` with `heldout-small` and `registration-few-prompts.yaml`. It is the end-to-end INCONCLUSIVE fixture.

### 10.6 Docs

G-14: `docs/tier-b-runbook.md` must:

- describe exporting the training split to local shard files under a manifest;
- describe the fallback to precision `stage` (05:348);
- no longer say "not designed yet".

## 11. Tier B helpers H-1 to H-8 (2026-11-09)

- **Sources:**
  - `TIER-B-SPEC.md` at the repository root;
  - an earlier Tier B research draft for a single-GPU host;
  - interim rules G-7 and G-14 to G-17.
- **Verification:** every pinned fact a test depends on was re-checked in the clones at NeMo RL 4f39fd8 and NeMo Evaluator ea35ba3.
- **Tests:** they need no network and no GPU.
- See section 2 for the exit codes. Only `tierb-collect` can return 1 for a gate that did not pass.
- **Errors:** every refusal prints `error[<RULE>] <field>: <message> | fix: <fix>` lines on stderr and writes nothing.

### 11.1 H-1 `gatedtrain export-gsm8k --out DIR [--source SRC --revision REV] [--rows-per-shard N]`

- **Source:**
  - `--source SRC` reads a local copy, `SRC/train.jsonl` and `SRC/test.jsonl`, with rows shaped like Hugging Face `openai/gsm8k` config `main`: `{question, answer}`, where the answer ends in `#### <final>`.
  - `--revision` (the HF dataset commit) is required with `--source`. If it is missing, the command refuses with `error[EXPORT-REVISION]` (G-17).
  - Without `--source` the command downloads from HF at `--revision`, a full 40-hex commit (default `tierb.DATASET_REVISION`, `740312add88f781978c0658806c59bc2815b9866`, the reference export's); a branch name refuses with `error[EXPORT-REVISION]`. At a revision listed in `tierb.DATASET_PARQUET_SHA256` (the default one) each downloaded parquet file must have the recorded SHA-256, otherwise `error[EXPORT-SHA256]`; another revision has no expected value. The download is tested with `urllib.request.urlopen` replaced (no network): the hash check only.
- **`--rows-per-shard`:** defaults to 500 (draft §3). The tests use 10.
- **Refusal:** if `DIR` already exists, exit 3.
- **Row format:** each row is `{"answer": A, "question": Q}`, with `A = answer.split("####")[1].strip()`, exactly as `nemo_rl/data/datasets/response_datasets/gsm8k.py:22-25`. Thousands commas are kept.
- **Bytes:** each row is written as `json.dumps(row, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False) + "\n"`.

The command writes:

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `DIR/train/shard-<NNNNN>.jsonl` | Training split, in split order. Nothing else is in `train/`. |
| `DIR/train.jsonl` | Byte concatenation of the shards in file-name order. It sits outside `train/` (L-5, G-14). |
| `DIR/heldout/prompts.jsonl` | Test split, same row format (it is the `prompt_set_hash` input). |
| `DIR/nel_gsm8k_prompt.txt` | NeMo Evaluator's GSM8K prompt (`benchmarks/gsm8k.py:22-26`) with `{question}` replaced by `{}` (`processors.py:574-576`). No trailing newline (G-15). |
| `DIR/manifests/{training_shards,prompt_set}.json` | manifest documents (format conventions) for `train/` and `heldout/`, same keys as the run manifests (section 5.1). |
| `DIR/export.json` | Canonical JSON with keys `schema, schema_version, tags, dataset ("openai/gsm8k"), config ("main"), revision, rows {train, test}, rows_per_shard, train_manifest_hash, prompt_set_hash, concat_sha256, prompt_file_sha256` (G-17). |

### 11.2 H-2 `gatedtrain verify-export DIR`

- **Success:** prints `ok`.
- **`error[EXPORT-HASH]`:** the shards concatenated in file-name order, `train.jsonl`, the `train/` manifest hash or the prompt-set hash disagree with `export.json`.
- **`error[EXPORT-LAYOUT]`:** a `train.jsonl` sits inside `train/`.

### 11.3 H-3 `gatedtrain merge-eval --registration REG --out OUT RUN_0 ... RUN_{K-1}`

**Inputs.** Each `RUN_r` is one NeMo Evaluator config-mode output directory:

- `RUN_r/gsm8k/results.jsonl` (orchestrator.py:1046-1047, :1081);
- `RUN_r/full_config.yaml`, the composed-config snapshot (config/snapshot.py:39, :72-81; executors/local_executor.py:69). The run's seed is read from `services.<solver service>.generation.seed`, or from the solver's own `generation.seed` if set. `repeats` is read from the benchmark entry.

**Refusal.** `error[MERGE-EVAL]`, exit 3, and `OUT` is not created, with these fields:

- `runs` when the number of runs differs from K = `samples_per_prompt`;
- `runs[r]` when that run is missing or empty, has `repeats` ≠ 1, has a seed other than `eval_seeds[r]`, or has a problem set that differs from run 0.

An existing `OUT` is also refused.

**Output.**

- `OUT/results.jsonl` holds every record sorted by `(problem_idx, repeat)`. Each record is unchanged except `repeat = r` and `metadata.eval_seed = eval_seeds[r]` (G-16).
- `OUT/merge.json` holds:
  - `sources`: `[{repeat, seed, run_dir, results_sha256}]`;
  - `notes`: a list that includes a sentence saying vLLM seeded sampling is reproducible only up to batch-composition numerics (G-16).

### 11.4 H-4 `gatedtrain nel-config --registration REG --model-url URL --model-id ID --eval-root ROOT --out DIR`

**Output.** One file per repeat, `DIR/nel-gsm8k-rep<r>.yaml` for r = 0..K-1, equal as parsed YAML to `fixtures/tierb/golden/nel-gsm8k-rep<r>.yaml`:

- `services.model`: `{type: api, url: URL + "/chat/completions", protocol: chat_completions, model: ID}`;
- `generation`: `{temperature, top_p, max_tokens = max_new_tokens, seed = eval_seeds[r]}`, from the registration sampler;
- `proxy.extra_body.chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false`;
- `benchmarks: [{name: gsm8k, repeats: 1, solver: {type: simple, service: model}}]`;
- `output: {dir: ROOT/rep<r>, timestamped: false}`.

**No revision key.** NeMo Evaluator's `hf://` loader has no revision parameter (environments/custom.py:126-148), so the config carries no revision; the export and the report record it (G-17).

**Refusal.** A registration with `sampler.enable_thinking` other than false is refused with the field `registration.sampler.enable_thinking` (G-15).

### 11.5 Tier B trainer config

The trainer config is the spec's `config` input. It is a small YAML file: `{backend: nemo_rl, recipe: examples/configs/grpo_smoke.yaml, profile: smoke|long}`. An example is `fixtures/tierb/trainer.yaml`.

### 11.6 H-5 `gatedtrain.backends.nemo_rl`

**`launch_spec(stage, *, nemo_rl_dir, launcher, out_dir, export_dir, profile, image, work_dir, hf_cache)`** returns `{"argv": [...], "cwd": str, "samples_per_position": int}`.

- `build_command(...)` takes the same keywords and returns `launch_spec(...)["argv"]`. Existing calls without the new keywords must keep working, with documented defaults.
- Launchers:
  - `local`: absolute script and recipe, cwd `nemo_rl_dir`;
  - `docker`: `docker run` with the draft §4 step 3 flags (golden `docker_flag_groups`, plus any `--shm-size`), the image's own source with no mount over `/opt/nemo-rl`, `-w /opt/nemo-rl`, and the program `uv run python examples/run_grpo.py --config examples/configs/grpo_smoke.yaml`;
  - `sbatch`: `sbatch --no-requeue`, with `COMMAND` equal to the local argv.
- Unknown launchers and profiles raise `ValueError`.

**Overrides.** They are the golden `common_overrides` plus the profile's overrides. `samples_per_position` = `grpo.num_generations_per_prompt` of the profile.

| Profile | Steps | Prompts × generations | Save period | Other |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `smoke` | 10 | 2 × 4 | 5 | Default batch sizes |
| `long` | 150 | 32 × 8 | 25 | `policy.train_global_batch_size=256`, `policy.train_micro_batch_size=8` |

**`samples_per_position_from_checkpoint(step_dir)`** reads `grpo.num_generations_per_prompt` from `step_<N>/config.yaml` (utils/checkpoint.py:413-418). The constant `NUM_GENERATIONS_PER_PROMPT` is removed (draft §2 item 6).

### 11.7 H-6 `gatedtrain tierb-collect SPEC --stage-out DIR --eval-baseline A --eval-candidate B --image-id ID --nemo-rl-commit SHA [--runs-root R]`

**Inputs.**

- `--image-id` and `--nemo-rl-commit` are interface additions; TIER-B-SPEC names only the lockfile content.
- `--stage-out` is the directory `build_command` pointed at: `DIR/checkpoints/step_<N>/` and `DIR/logs/exp_<NNN>/train_data_step<N>.jsonl` (examples/run_grpo.py:106; utils/logger.py:1993-2025).
- `A` and `B` are merged arms (H-3 output), so `results.jsonl` sits directly inside each.
- The spec's training dataset is `<export>/train`. Collect runs the H-2 checks on `<export>`.

**Refusals.** All are exit 3, before any run directory is created:

- `error[TIERB-RESULTS]` when an arm's `results.jsonl` is missing or empty. This covers the parent directory and an unmerged `rep<r>` run dir, and fixes the silent MISSING of draft §2 item 1.
- `error[TIERB-PAIRING]` when `unpaired_baseline + unpaired_candidate` exceeds the registered `pairing.max_unpaired`. The field is new; absent means 0.
- A refusal when there is more than one `exp_*` log directory (draft risk R9).
- A refusal when the `consumed_samples` check fails, with `samples_per_position` from the checkpoint config (G-7).

**Output.** A run directory with the layout of section 5:

- the manifests; `baseline_checkpoint` is the spec's `base` directory, which must be a plain directory with no symlinks (manifest rule H2), not the HF cache;
- `lockfile.json` with:
  - `backend.trainer: {name: nemo_rl, image_id, nemo_rl_commit}`;
  - `backend.evaluator: {name: nemo_evaluator, nemo_evaluator_commit: "ea35ba3"}`;
  - `dataset_revision`;
  - `config_hash`: SHA-256 of the spec's trainer config;
  - `nemo_rl_config_sha256`: SHA-256 of the final `step_<N>/config.yaml`;
  - `allow_list_checked: false`;
- the ledger, the lineage and the report. The report adds `dataset_revision` and `notes` (with the G-16 sentence) and shows `dataset_revision: <rev>` in the text.

**Exit code.** 0 on PASS, otherwise 1.

**Afterwards.** `gatedtrain impact` works on the run directory.

### 11.8 H-7 spec and registration support

| Item | Accepted value |
|---|---|
| Gate evaluator | `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator@0.1` |
| Trainer | `rl.nemo_rl@0.1` |
| Budget | `budget.gpu_hours > 0` |
| Sampler fields | `top_p`, `enable_thinking`; `enable_thinking: true` is refused with the field `registration.sampler.enable_thinking` (G-15) |
| Registration field | `pairing: {max_unpaired: int}` |

`gatedtrain run` still refuses a Tier B stage, with the field `stages[0].uses`.

### 11.9 H-8 `demo/tierb/spec.yaml` and `demo/tierb/registration.yaml`

The templates must:

- validate as they are;
- set `budget.gpu_hours: 8`;
- use `rl.nemo_rl@0.1` and `gate.superiority@1.0` with `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator@0.1`;
- set K = 4 with 4 distinct seeds;
- set a margin and a `fixed` or `ucb80` σ plan, with comments that name them `internal_pilot` placeholders;
- set `enable_thinking: false` and `top_p`;
- set `signed_by: prototype-unsigned`;
- pin the template registration's SHA-256 in the spec;
- stay a subset of the 04 grammar.

### 11.10 Tier B test files

All run with no network and no GPU.

| File | Covers |
|---|---|
| `test_tierb_export.py` | H-1 and H-2, including tamper, reorder, stray shard and the concatenation inside `train/` |
| `test_tierb_merge.py` | H-3 and its refusals |
| `test_tierb_nel_config.py` | H-4 golden and the G-15 refusal |
| `test_tierb_build_command.py` | H-5 goldens for local, docker and sbatch and both profiles; generations from the config |
| `test_tierb_collect.py` | H-6 end to end on recorded outputs, PASS; ledger with 8 generations; impact after collect; refusals including the silent-MISSING regression |
| `test_tierb_spec.py` | H-7 and H-8 |
| `test_tierb_citations.py` | Every emitted key and flag is checked against the pinned clones. It skips when a clone is absent. The `TestReference*` classes check the goldens and pass without `gatedtrain`. |
| `test_tierb_scorer.py` | G-21 fix B: the registered `numeric_match` scorer through validate, nel-config, merge-eval and tierb-collect, with its refusals and clone citations (section 11.14) |

**Helpers and fixtures:**

- `_tierb.py` holds the independent reference export and the builders for recorded outputs;
- the fixtures are in `fixtures/tierb/`, and `make_fixtures.py` regenerates the GSM8K-shaped source.

### 11.11 F-17 addition to H-5

- **Parameter:** `launch_spec(..., base_model=PATH)` and `build_command(..., base_model=PATH)` take the base-model directory as it is seen by the training process. Under docker that is a path inside the container, for example `/work/tierb/base-model`.
- **Override:** they emit `policy.model_name=PATH` exactly once, for every launcher and profile (golden `common_overrides`).
- **Why:** PATH must be the same directory as the spec's `base` artifact, whose manifest the gate records, so that lineage names the right parent.
- **Pins:** `policy.model_name` is loaded with `from_pretrained` (`nemo_rl/models/automodel/setup.py:374-375`, `:941-942`) and passed to vLLM (`grpo.py:1426`).

### 11.12 Tier B review round: collect and merge checks (maintainers' decisions)

**`tierb-collect`.** It refuses before creating anything (exit 3) in each of these cases:

| Decision | Rule | Field | Refused when |
|---|---|---|---|
| HIGH-2 | `TIERB-PROMPTS` | `--eval-baseline` | (a) the paired `problem_idx` set differs from `range(len(heldout))`; (b) a record's `expected_answer` differs from the heldout answer with thousands commas removed (NeMo Evaluator `_prepare`, `benchmarks/gsm8k.py:29-31`); (c) the heldout question is not contained in the record's `prompt` |
| LOW-2 | `ARTIFACT-HASH` | `artifacts.<name>.uri` | a non-registration `@sha256:` pin in the spec does not match, as in Tier A preflight |
| LOW-3 | `TIERB-PROFILE` | `--stage-out` | `grpo.max_num_steps`, `grpo.num_prompts_per_step` or `grpo.num_generations_per_prompt` in `step_<last>/config.yaml` differs from the trainer.yaml `profile` (section 11.6 table) |
| LOW-4 | `TIERB-BASELINE` | `artifacts.<model>.uri` | the base-model directory contains `.cache/`. The fix line says to delete `base-model/.cache`. |
| LOW-6 | `TIERB-RESULTS` | `--eval-baseline` or `--eval-candidate` | a results record has no `reward` key |

**`merge-eval`.** It refuses with `MERGE-EVAL`, field `runs[r]` (LOW-1), when `full_config.yaml` differs from the registration or from the other runs in any of:

- `temperature`;
- `top_p`;
- `max_tokens` (the registration's `max_new_tokens`);
- `model`;
- `proxy.extra_body.chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`.

**Fixtures.** The recorded evaluator records now look like NeMo Evaluator's: `prompt` is the GSM8K template filled with the question, and `expected_answer` has no thousands commas. The collect scenarios follow the trainer.yaml profile, with smoke 2 × 4 and a separate long-profile scenario, 32 × 8 over 150 steps.

### 11.13 F-27: the Tier B host path without torch

The `uv.lock` covers macOS only (G-11), so a Linux host cannot run `uv sync --locked`.

**Host install.**

```
uv venv --python 3.13 <dir>
uv pip install --python <dir>/bin/python -r requirements-tierb-host.txt --only-binary :all: --require-hashes
```

- `requirements-tierb-host.txt` sits at the repository root.
- It holds one `name==version` pin per package, each followed by at least one `--hash=sha256:` entry. Each version equals the `uv.lock` entry.
- It covers numpy, scipy, pyarrow, duckdb and pyyaml, plus any other third-party module the host path imports, and the lock dependency closure of all of them. It never includes torch.
- `export-gsm8k` without `--source` uses `urllib` and pyarrow only, so no huggingface_hub pin is needed.
- In the runbook, `gatedtrain` means `<dir>/bin/python -m gatedtrain`.
- The Tier A lock is unchanged.

**Tests.** `test_tierb_host.py` runs the host commands with torch blocked by a `sys.meta_path` finder:

- `validate` (demo/tierb);
- `export-gsm8k`, `verify-export`;
- `nel-config`, `merge-eval`;
- `tierb-collect`;
- `impact`.

It also checks the pins against `uv.lock`, the completeness of the pins against an import scan of `gatedtrain/` (excluding `stubs/` and the Tier A-only functions `run` and `preflight` in `runner.py`), and the runbook's host install section.

### 11.14 G-21 fix B: the registered `numeric_match` scorer (2026-11-11)

**Why.** At NeMo Evaluator ea35ba3 the GSM8K default scorer (`answer_line`, strict) gives Qwen3-0.6B a mean reward of about 0 because of answer formatting, so the gate measures format, not training (gap G-21). Fix B, chosen by the maintainers: the registration selects NeMo Evaluator's built-in `numeric_match` scorer, and the gate reads that scorer's reward.

**Pinned facts** (a NeMo Evaluator clone at `ea35ba36756abd59de201f60af8a2feba014744d` (`GATEDTRAIN_NEMO_EVALUATOR_DIR`); `TestScorerCitations` re-reads each one and skips if the clone is absent):

- `BenchmarkConfig.scoring: ScoringConfig` (`src/nemo_evaluator/config/benchmarks.py:63`). `ScoringConfig.metrics` is a list. A function scorer is `{type: scorer, name: <registered name>}` (`config/scoring.py:24-30`, `:160-184`). `include_defaults` and `primary` exist in the schema but nothing consumes them at ea35ba3, so they are not emitted or relied on.
- `numeric_match` is in the scorer registry (`scoring/__init__.py:38-44`). It takes the last number in the response with commas removed, then strips trailing zeros and a trailing dot on both sides (`scoring/pattern.py:39-43`). So `10` and `1` match (but `10.` and `1` do not: the dot stops the zero strip).
- Scorer-type metric names become extra scorers (`orchestration/orchestrator.py:1050`, `:1064`). They do not replace the top-level `reward`. Each writes `scoring_details["scorer:<name>"] = {correct, extracted, reward}` (`engine/eval_loop.py:704-721`), and the record gets `scorer_rewards: {"scorer:<name>": 0.0 | 1.0}` (`eval_loop.py:785-794`).
- `<run>/full_config.yaml` dumps the raw config as written (`config/snapshot.py:72-81`), so a run made with the scorer declares it there.

**Interface.**

| # | Item | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Registration `evaluators[0].scorer` | Optional string. Allowlist at ea35ba3: `numeric_match`. Absent or `null` keeps today's behaviour exactly: the default benchmark scorer and the top-level `reward`. Any other value, or a non-string, is refused by `validate` and by every command that loads the registration (`nel-config`, `merge-eval`, `tierb-collect`) with `error[REG-SCORER] registration.evaluators[0].scorer: ... \| fix: ...`, exit 3. The fix names the allowlist. |
| 2 | `nel-config` | With `scorer: numeric_match`, every `nel-gsm8k-rep<r>.yaml` has `scoring: {metrics: [{type: scorer, name: numeric_match}]}` under its single gsm8k benchmark. Nothing else changes. With no scorer (absent or null) the output bytes are today's. |
| 3 | `merge-eval` | Merged records keep `scorer_rewards` and `scoring_details` unchanged (already true; pinned). |
| 4a | `tierb-collect`, metric | With scorer X registered, the per-record metric is `scorer_rewards["scorer:X"]`, not `reward`. Both `baseline_score`/`candidate_score` and the gate use it. The copied `eval/<arm>/results.jsonl` stay byte-identical to the arm's. With no scorer, the gate numbers are unchanged. |
| 4b | `tierb-collect`, missing value | A record without `scorer_rewards["scorer:X"]` is refused with `TIERB-RESULTS`, field `--eval-baseline` or `--eval-candidate`, exit 3, nothing written. It is never read as 0 (mirrors LOW-6). |
| 4c | `tierb-collect`, run configs | New rule `TIERB-SCORER`, field `--eval-baseline` or `--eval-candidate`, exit 3, nothing written: the arm's copied per-run configs `configs/rep<r>.yaml` (decision D-1) do not all declare `{type: scorer, name: X}` under the gsm8k benchmark's `scoring.metrics`, or the arm has no copied configs. Kept as defence in depth behind the `merge-eval` check (D-2). |
| 4d | Text report | A line `metric_source: scorer_rewards[scorer:X]`, or `metric_source: reward` when no scorer is registered. |
| 6 | Report note | With a scorer registered, `notes` (JSON and text) carry one note that names `numeric_match`, says it compares the last number in the response after stripping **trailing zeros** (`pattern.py:39-43`), an effect on integers without a trailing dot (`10` and `1` match; `10.` and `1` do not, D-7), and gives the observed false-positive rate **25/5,276 = 0.47%** on the smoke baseline (**G-21**). The test looks for `numeric_match`, `trailing zero`, `25/5,276`, `0.47%` and `G-21` in one note. No such note without a scorer. The G-16 note stays. |

**Tests** (`test_tierb_scorer.py`; fixtures from `_tierb.py`, which gained optional `scorer`/`scorer_fn`/`declared_scorers`/`scorer_missing` arguments and `with_scorer()`, all defaulting to today's bytes):

| Requirement | Test |
|---|---|
| 1 | `TestRegistrationScorer.test_numeric_match_accepted`, `test_null_accepted`, `test_other_scorer_names_refused` (answer_line, exact_match, fuzzy_match, Numeric_Match, not_a_scorer, empty), `test_non_string_refused`, `test_refusal_names_the_allowlist`, `test_nel_config_refuses_unknown_scorer`, `test_merge_eval_refuses_unknown_scorer` |
| 2 | `TestNelConfigScorer.test_scoring_block_emitted`, `test_everything_else_unchanged`, `test_null_scorer_is_byte_identical_to_no_scorer`; plus the existing `test_tierb_nel_config.TestNelConfig.test_golden` |
| 3 | `TestMergeKeepsScorerRewards.test_scorer_fields_unchanged` |
| 4a, 5 | `TestCollectWithScorer.test_fixture_reward_is_zero_everywhere`, `test_gate_uses_scorer_rewards` (reward all 0, scorer values give Δ̂ = 1/3, PASS, scores 1/3 and 2/3), `test_results_copied_unchanged`; control `TestCollectWithoutScorerControl.test_gate_reads_reward` (same records, no scorer: Δ̂ = 0, not PASS); existing `test_tierb_collect.TestCollectPass` for unchanged numbers |
| 4b | `TestCollectScorerRefusals.test_record_without_scorer_reward` |
| 4c | `TestCollectScorerRefusals.test_runs_without_scorer`, `test_runs_with_another_scorer` (arms merged under a no-scorer registration, so the copied configs lack the scorer), `test_hand_built_arm_configs_lack_scorer`, `test_arm_without_run_configs`; `test_good_arms_accepted` shows the check is not a blanket refusal |
| 4d | `TestCollectWithScorer.test_metric_source_line`, `TestCollectWithoutScorerControl.test_metric_source_reward` |
| 6 | `TestCollectWithScorer.test_numeric_match_note`, `TestCollectWithoutScorerControl.test_no_numeric_match_note` |
| Citations | `TestScorerCitations` (clone commit, `benchmarks.py:63`, `scoring.py:24-30` and `:160-184`, `__init__.py:38-44`, `pattern.py:39-43` executed on 10/1, 1/10, `1,234`, `orchestrator.py:1050`/`:1064`, `eval_loop.py:704-721`/`:785-794`, `snapshot.py:72-81`) |

The collect scenarios build each arm the way the runbook does: K recorded raw runs merged by the real `gatedtrain merge-eval`. The arm for `test_arm_without_run_configs` is written directly (no `configs/`, no `merge.json`); the arm for `test_hand_built_arm_configs_lack_scorer` is written directly in the D-1 layout.

**Maintainers' decisions (2026-11-11)** on the design questions, with their tests:

| # | Decision | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | `merge-eval` copies each input run's `full_config.yaml` byte for byte to `OUT/configs/rep<r>.yaml` (r = registered seed order) and records it in `merge.json` as `sources[r].config = "configs/rep<r>.yaml"` and `sources[r].config_sha256` (the two key names are a naming choice). `tierb-collect` reads the per-run configs from `configs/`, not from the absolute `sources[].run_dir` paths. | `TestMergeCopiesConfigs.test_configs_copied`, `test_configs_copied_with_scorer`; `TestCollectWithScorer.test_arm_holds_copied_configs`, `test_collect_after_raw_runs_moved` |
| D-2 | With scorer X registered, `merge-eval` refuses any run whose `full_config.yaml` does not declare `{type: scorer, name: X}` under the gsm8k benchmark: `MERGE-EVAL`, field `runs[r]`, exit 3, `OUT` not created. Without a registered scorer there is no check. `tierb-collect` keeps `TIERB-SCORER` (row 4c). | `TestMergeScorerRefusals.test_run_without_scorer`, `test_run_with_another_scorer`, `test_no_run_declares_scorer`, `test_no_scorer_registered_no_check`; `TestCollectScorerRefusals.test_one_run_without_scorer`, `test_hand_built_arm_configs_lack_scorer` |
| D-3 | `tierb-collect` refuses (`TIERB-RESULTS`, exit 3, nothing written) any record whose `scoring_details["scorer:X"]` has an `error` key (`eval_loop.py:719-721`), although `scorer_rewards` holds 0.0 there. | `TestCollectScorerRefusals.test_scorer_error_record` |
| D-4 | The scorer is part of the evaluator identity. With a scorer registered, `evaluator_hash` covers it (differs from the no-scorer value) and `lockfile.backend.evaluator` gains `scorer: X`. With no scorer, `evaluator_hash` is today's `sha256:032ad6d4e93ce7d39a33641679b6bec9d534df21ba7af4b5738858531648739e` (SHA-256 of the canonical JSON `{benchmark, evaluator, nemo_evaluator_commit}`), and the lockfile keeps today's keys and `backend.evaluator = {name, nemo_evaluator_commit, uses}`. | `TestCollectWithScorer.test_evaluator_identity_covers_scorer`; `TestCollectWithoutScorerControl.test_evaluator_hash_unchanged`, `test_lockfile_unchanged` |
| D-5 | `metric: reward` stays in the report next to the `metric_source` line. | no change |
| D-6 | `demo/tierb/registration.yaml` becomes the amendment (05:142): `registration_version: 2`, `registration_id: tierb-4090-gsm8k-superiority-v2`, `evaluators[0].scorer: numeric_match` with a comment citing G-21 (on the line or just above it); `demo/tierb/spec.yaml` re-pins its SHA-256. The templates are edited accordingly (section 11.9). | `TestDemoTemplateAmendment.test_amendment_fields`, `test_scorer_comment_cites_g21`, `test_spec_pins_template`, `test_template_validates`; existing `test_tierb_spec.TestTierBTemplates` and `test_tierb_host` validate the template unchanged |
| D-7 | The note says the trailing-zero effect applies to integers without a trailing dot: `10` and `1` match, `10.` and `1` do not (the regex keeps the dot, so no zero is stripped). | `TestCollectWithScorer.test_numeric_match_note` (row 6); `TestScorerCitations.test_numeric_match_strips_trailing_zeros` executes `pattern.py:39-43` and pins both behaviours (`10`/`1` correct, `10.`/`1` not correct) |
| D-8 | Byte identity of `nel-config` without a scorer is checked as in row 2. | no change |
| D-9 | The G-21 gap record is updated by the maintainers. | Documentation |
| D-10 | Review MEDIUM-1. `evaluators[0].scorer` (non-null) is allowed only when `evaluators[0].id` is the Tier B evaluator `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator@0.1`. Any other evaluator with a scorer is refused by `validate` (and every command that loads the registration) with `REG-SCORER`, field `registration.evaluators[0].scorer`, exit 3, nothing written; the message or fix names `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator@0.1`. `scorer: null` stays accepted for every evaluator. | `TestScorerOnlyForTierBEvaluator.test_tier_a_evaluator_with_scorer_refused` (Tier A demo `addition_em.cpu_stub@0.1` + `scorer: numeric_match`, spec re-pinned), control `test_tier_a_null_scorer_accepted` |
| D-11 | Review LOW-1. Every per-record score the gate reads, `scorer_rewards["scorer:X"]` with a scorer registered or the top-level `reward` without one, must be a finite number in [0, 1]. NaN, +inf, -inf, -0.1 and 1.5 (Python's `json` reads the `NaN`/`Infinity` literals) are refused with `TIERB-RESULTS`, field `--eval-baseline` or `--eval-candidate`, exit 3, no run directory, no traceback. | `TestCollectScoreRange.test_scorer_reward_out_of_range`, `test_scorer_reward_out_of_range_candidate`, `test_plain_reward_out_of_range`, `test_plain_reward_out_of_range_candidate`; control `test_good_arms_accepted` |
| D-12 | Review LOW-2, with a scorer registered. A record whose `scorer_rewards` or `scoring_details` is not a JSON object (a list or a string) is refused with `TIERB-RESULTS`, field of the arm. An arm whose `configs/rep<r>.yaml` is not valid UTF-8 is refused with `TIERB-SCORER`, field of the arm. Both exit 3, no run directory, no traceback. | `TestCollectMalformedScorerFields.test_non_dict_fields`, `test_non_utf8_run_config` |


### 11.15 Design revision 10 alignment (2026-11-11)

**Sources.** `design.md` "Revision 10 changes" (R10-1 to R10-12) and the edited sections of 04 and 05 (current line numbers: 05:116 missingness check, 05:138-143 achieved power, 05:145 minimum paired sample, 05:151 registration, 05:160 statuses and `combine`, 05:217 scorer validity, 05:424 and 05:463 superseded and orphaned counts; 04:34 α fields, 04:100 registration authoritative). Gap dispositions: the prototype gap record. This section supersedes sections 3, 4.2, 7, 10.2, 11.7, 11.8 and 11.12 where they differ.

**Decision IDs.** Decisions in this section use the prefix `R10A-` and never a bare `D-<n>`. Sections 10 (2026-11-09, D-1 to D-5) and 11.14 (2026-11-11, D-1 to D-12) both used `D-1`, `D-2`, ...; that collision is resolved by this rule: from now on every section that records decisions uses its own prefix, and earlier `D-<n>` references are read with their section (for example "11.14 D-4").

**Superseded interim rules.** G-3 (equivalence part) and G-12 by R10-3; G-6 by R10-5; G-8 by R10-7; G-18's refusal (TIERB-PAIRING) by R10-8.

#### Requirements

| ID | Source | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| R10A-1 | R10-3; 05 §3.6 | **Equivalence power and n.** `plan()` returns, for equivalence, `n_required` = ⌈DE·((z₁₋α + z₁₋β/2)·σ/δ)²⌉ at θ = 0 and ⌈DE·((z₁₋α + z₁₋β)·σ/(δ − \|θ\|))²⌉ for θ ≠ 0 (β = 1 − power, θ = `planning_effect`, σ from the σ plan), and `underpowered = n_available < n_required`. `evaluate()` returns achieved power max(0, Φ((δ − θ)/SE − z₁₋α) + Φ((δ + θ)/SE − z₁₋α) − 1), SE = σ̂_d·√(DE/n), σ̂_d the observed SD (ddof = 1). An equivalence non-rejection is FAIL when powered and INCONCLUSIVE when underpowered. Superiority and non-inferiority are unchanged. `underpowered` is true on low power, on n below the plan's `n_required`, or when power cannot be computed. |
| R10A-2 | R10-5; 05 §3.7, §6.1 | **Evaluator hash.** `evaluators[0].manifest_hash` null or absent is refused by `validate` and every command that loads the registration: `REG-FIELD`, field `registration.evaluators[0].manifest_hash`, exit 3. The fix line names the hash the prototype resolves for that evaluator: Tier B, the evaluator identity hash of 11.14 D-4 (`sha256:032ad6d4…` without a scorer, `sha256:2c196d9d…` with `numeric_match`); Tier A, the stand-in identity hash the report already records as `evaluator_hash`. At run time (`run`, `tierb-collect`) a registered hash that differs from the resolved one makes the gate MISSING with reason `evaluator_hash_mismatch` (05 §6.1); the report gives `evaluator_hash` (resolved) and `evaluator_hash_registered`. `validate` does not compare values. `manifest_hash: null` is replaced in every demo registration (`demo/registration*.yaml`, `demo/tierb/registration.yaml`) and the specs are re-pinned. |
| R10A-3 | R10-8; 05 §3.4 | **Missingness.** Registration `pairing: {max_unpaired: B}`; absent means 0. B is an integer ≥ 0 (a count) or a float in [0, 1) (a fraction); `true`, strings, null, negatives and floats ≥ 1 are refused (`REG-FIELD`, field under `registration.pairing`). Expected items E = {(p, r): p in either arm, r in range(K)}; `missing_<arm>` = \|E − arm\|. The clause is MISSING, reason `missing_above_bound`, when `missing_baseline + missing_candidate` > B (count) or > B·2\|E\| (fraction). Complete pairs are still analysed and reported (Δ̂, interval). `evaluate(..., max_unpaired=0)`; Tier A and Tier B pass the registration's value. `tierb-collect` no longer refuses with `TIERB-PAIRING`: it writes the run and the MISSING report (exit 1). `TIERB-PROMPTS` (a) compares the **union** of both arms' problems with the held-out set. Report and text: `missing_baseline`, `missing_candidate`, `max_unpaired`; `unpaired_*` (10.2) stay. |
| R10A-4 | R10-9; 05 §3.7 | **Amendments.** Optional registration fields `amends: "sha256:<64 hex>"` (the prior registration's hash) and `post_hoc: <bool>` (default false); other values are refused (`REG-FIELD registration.amends`, `registration.post_hoc`). The report and text give `registration_amends` (null when absent) next to `registration_hash`, and `post_hoc`. With `post_hoc: true` a clause whose pass rule holds is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `post_hoc`; the statistics are still reported. **How the prototype learns "after candidate results":** only from `post_hoc`, which the author must set truthfully; the prototype has no record of when results were first seen. |
| R10A-5 | R10-10; 05 §3.6 | **Minimum paired sample.** Registration `min_paired` (integer ≥ 1; default 10; others refused `REG-FIELD registration.min_paired`). `evaluate(..., min_paired=10)`. A clause with n < `min_paired` is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `min_paired`; a zero-width interval (n < 2, or σ̂_d = 0) is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `zero_width_interval`, whatever its limiting power (`sd_zero` and the 1.0 limit of G-13 stay). Report and text: `min_paired`. |
| R10A-6 | R10-7; 04 §3, §4.2; 05 §3.2 | **Registration authoritative.** The spec's `test.alpha`, `power`, `mde` and `margin` are optional: their absence is no longer refused (`GATE-ALPHA` on `stages[i].test.alpha` and `GATE-REQUIRED` on those fields are retired). A registration without `clause.alpha` (no family) stays refused, `REG-FIELD registration.clause.alpha`, whatever the spec says. A differing spec value stays refused (G-4, 10.3). `GATE-ALPHA stages[i].alpha_family` (alpha inside a family) stays. |
| R10A-7 | review finding 7 | **Data config.** New rule `TIERB-DATA`, field `--stage-out`, exit 3, nothing written, when `step_<last>/config.yaml` has `data.shuffle` other than `false` (absent included); `data.train` that is not one dataset (a mapping or a one-element list: `nemo_rl/data/utils.py:215-216` at 4f39fd8 saves the list form); a `data_path` whose file name is not `train.jsonl` or whose parent directory name differs from the spec's export directory name (the container path differs from the host path, as for F-17); or `split_validation_size` other than 0 (`response_dataset.py:76` splits and reorders rows; an addition here). The fixture stage config (`_tierb.nemo_rl_data_config`) now has the real long run's saved `data` section. |
| R10A-8 | review finding 8; 05 §3.3 | **K and seeds.** `k_actual = {baseline: [min, max], candidate: [min, max]}` repeats per prompt in each arm (JSON and a text line `k_actual: …`). The clause is MISSING with reason `k_mismatch` when either arm's maximum differs from the registered K or a record has a repeat outside range(K); with reason `seed_mismatch` when a record's `metadata.eval_seed` is present and differs from `eval_seeds[repeat]` (records without it are not checked). Per-prompt shortfalls below K are missing items (R10A-3). Tier B keeps `metadata` when it reads `scorer_rewards`. |
| R10A-9 | review finding 9; 05 §12.4, §11.3 | **Impact counts.** `gatedtrain impact … --json` prints a top-level `counts: {orphaned, superseded}` for the queried shard over the selected runs; the text output adds the line `orphaned: <n>, superseded: <m>`. Counts are ConsumptionRecords (at precision `iteration`, one per (step, shard)). Orphaned: records after the attempt's last committed cut; superseded: 0 in the prototype (one attempt, one segment). Orphaned records still enter no rollup (`test_ledger_unit`); where they are kept is an implementation choice. |
| R10A-10 | review finding 10; 05 §3.8 table | **p-values.** `evaluate()` and the report give `p_value` and `p_value_method = "bca_inversion"`: the one-sided p for H₀: Δ ≤ δ_sup (superiority) or Δ ≤ −δ (non-inferiority) is the α′ at which the BCa lower bound equals the null value, p = Φ(w/(1 + a·w) − z₀) with w = Φ⁻¹(G(null)) − z₀, G the bootstrap CDF (ties counted half, clipped to [1/(B+1), B/(B+1)] as z₀ is), and z₀, a from the same bootstrap. Equivalence: TOST p = max(p for H₀: Δ ≤ −δ, p for H₀: Δ ≥ δ), the upper one 1 − Φ(w/(1 + a·w) − z₀) at G(δ). Null for a zero-width interval or no pairs. Text line `p_value: …`. The decision still comes from the interval; p < α agrees with it up to the bootstrap quantile interpolation. |
| R10A-11 | review finding 11 | **Image modification.** `tierb-collect` takes `--nemo-rl-modified {yes,no}` and `--image-build-notes TEXT`. The lockfile's `backend.trainer` gains `nemo_rl_modified` (true, false, or the string `"unknown"` when the flag is absent; never false by default) and `image_build_notes` (TEXT or null). Another value of `--nemo-rl-modified` is a usage error (exit 2). |
| R10A-12 | regression | A Tier B collect shaped like the real long run (long profile, K = 4, seeds 11-14 merged by `merge-eval`, `numeric_match`, fixed σ, the real saved `data` config, `min_paired: 10`, `pairing.max_unpaired: 0`, resolved evaluator hash, `amends`) is PASS with no status reasons, with and without the new flags. |
| R10A-13 | maintainers' decision; 05 §3.7 | **Post hoc does not soften a FAIL.** `post_hoc: true` changes only a PASS (to INCONCLUSIVE, reason `post_hoc`, R10A-4). A FAIL stays FAIL; INCONCLUSIVE and MISSING keep their status and reasons, and `post_hoc` is not added to their `status_reasons`. Implemented by `gates.apply_post_hoc`. |
| R10A-14 | review follow-up MEDIUM-1 (2026-11-11); 05 §3.6 (05:156), R10A-5, R10A-10 | **Zero width up to float noise.** The per-prompt differences d are degenerate when max(d) − min(d) ≤ 1e-12 · max(1, \|mean(d)\|), and n < 2 is degenerate as before. One shared criterion decides all four outputs: the interval has zero width (`ci_lower == ci_upper`), `p_value` is null (`bca_p_value` returns None), `sd_zero` is true (n ≥ 2; for n = 1 `sd_diff` and `sd_zero` stay null/false), and the clause is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `zero_width_interval`; `achieved_power` keeps its G-13 limit. Exact `std() == 0.0` (today `bca_interval`, `bca_p_value` and `evaluate`) is not enough: K = 3 binary rewards with exactly one more correct candidate repeat per prompt give differences 0.3333333333333333 and 0.33333333333333337 (sd 9.8e-17), which today PASS with `ci_lower == ci_upper` and a p-value (review probe p1). Spread above the tolerance (for example 1e-11 at mean 0.2) is a real interval: `sd_zero` false, p-value present. |
| R10A-15 | review follow-up MEDIUM-2 (2026-11-11); R10A-10 | **p-value pinned to the BCa inversion.** On skewed data (nonzero a and z₀) the reported `p_value` equals, within 3/B, the α′ at which the real `bca_interval` (same seed, so the same bootstrap draws) has its lower bound at the null value (for the upper TOST side, its upper bound at δ); equivalence takes the larger side. Test-quality gap: no code change is required, but the tests must fail when `w/(1 + a·w)` becomes `w/(1 − a·w)`, when either `− z₀` becomes `+ z₀`, or when the BCa p is replaced by the percentile Φ(w). |
| R10A-16 | review follow-up LOW-1 (2026-11-11); R10A-10 | **p-value at the bootstrap floor.** When the bootstrap tail behind the reported one-sided p is empty (G(null) clipped to 1/(B+1) for H₀: Δ ≤ null; G(δ) clipped to B/(B+1) for the upper TOST side; for TOST, the side that gives the reported maximum), the p-value is a bound, not an estimate (the real long run reported 8.54e-05 this way). `evaluate()` and the report give `p_value_at_floor: true`; it is false otherwise, including a p near 1 from the opposite clip (a ceiling, not a floor) and a null p-value. The text line reads `p_value: <= <p> (bca_inversion)` at the floor and `p_value: <p> (bca_inversion)` otherwise. |

**Status reasons.** `evaluate()` and the report carry `status_reasons`, a list of strings, empty for an unqualified result: `no_pairs`, `missing_above_bound`, `k_mismatch`, `seed_mismatch`, `evaluator_hash_mismatch` (MISSING); `min_paired`, `zero_width_interval`, `post_hoc`, `underpowered` (INCONCLUSIVE; an underpowered PASS keeps `underpowered: true` and gets no reason). Text line `status_reasons: <comma-separated>` (empty after the colon when none). MISSING takes precedence over INCONCLUSIVE within a clause; `combine` follows 05 §3.8 (pinned by `test_r10_stats.TestCombinePrecedence`).

**`evaluate()` signature.** `evaluate(clause, baseline, candidate, *, plan=None, seed, n_boot=10000, min_paired=10, max_unpaired=0)`. New result keys: `status_reasons`, `p_value`, `p_value_method`, `k_actual`, `missing_baseline`, `missing_candidate`, `min_paired`, `max_unpaired`. The report adds those plus `post_hoc`, `registration_amends`, `evaluator_hash_registered`. R10A-16 adds `p_value_at_floor` to the result and the report.
