**Older tests revised for revision 10.** `test_spec_errors.TestRegistrationRules.test_registration_changed_after_hash_recorded` and `test_e2e_slow.DEMO_REG_SHA` no longer pin the demo registration's hash as a literal: R10A-2 changed `demo/registration.yaml` (`755b0d33…` → `45332251…`), and the requirement is that the report, lockfile and manifest record the hash of the registration that was run and that the spec pins that file. Both now compute the hash from the demo file; `SlowE2EPass.test_report_values` adds the check that `demo/spec.yaml` pins it. `test_e2e_slow.SlowNegativeNoTraining.test_not_pass`: lr = 0 gives sd = 0 and a zero-width interval, so the gate is INCONCLUSIVE with reason `zero_width_interval` and `sd_zero: true` (R10A-5), replacing the rev-9 rule "INCONCLUSIVE iff underpowered" (the G-13 power limit 1.0 leaves `underpowered` false).

**Fixture changes.** `fixtures/tierb/registration.template.yaml` carries the resolved evaluator hash; `_tierb.with_scorer` swaps in the scorer's identity hash; `_tierb.evaluator_identity_hash` and `_tierb.nemo_rl_data_config` are new; `build_stage_out` takes `data=` and writes the real long run's `data` section by default; `test_tierb_scorer.TestScorerOnlyForTierBEvaluator` accepts any `manifest_hash` value on the Tier A anchor line.

### 11.16 Design revision 11 alignment (2026-11-11)

**Sources.** `design.md` change table R11-1 to R11-22 (current files are revision 12; revision 12 changes nothing below except the cross-references R12-6 and R12-7). Current line numbers: 05:56 orientation, 05:58 ratio metrics, 05:71-86 clause YAML, 05:105-110 item and evaluated model, 05:118 ratio interval, 05:124 missingness, 05:152 ratio power, 05:156 minimum paired sample, 05:166 registration and `post_hoc`, 05:179 statuses, 05:181 per-item record, 05:183 p-values, 05:267 score range and scorer validity, 05:369 `Checkpoint`, 05:401 derivation, 05:463 superseded and orphaned; 04:100 gate mapping and `items` role; 08:71 completeness rule; the multimodal decisions (C1 to C7, `post_hoc` rules). This section supersedes sections 4.2, 5.3, 7, 11.7, 11.8 and 11.15 where they differ; in particular it replaces the R10A-8 rule "the maximum repeats per prompt must equal K" and the R10A-3 expected set "the prompts of either arm".

**Decision IDs.** Prefix `R11A-`. Choices still to confirm are marked **(choice)** in the table and listed under "Choices".

**Names.** Revision 11 names: clause `unit: item`, `samples_per_item`, `direction`, optional `estimator`; registration `eval_distribution: {item_set_hash, env_version, env_params_version}`; spec gate input role `items` of artifact type `item_set`, `test.cluster_by: item | training_seed`. Legacy names: `unit: prompt`, `samples_per_prompt`, `prompt_set_hash`, role `prompts` of type `prompt_dataset`, `cluster_by: prompt`. Literal prompts and training prompt datasets keep "prompt" (`prompts.jsonl` in the item-set directory, `training_dataset`, the Tier B export's `export.json` `prompt_set_hash`).

**Interfaces.**
- `gates.evaluate_items(clause, baseline, candidate, *, items, seed, n_boot=10000, min_paired=10, max_unpaired=0, score_range=(None, None), expected_models=None, plan=None)`. An arm is `{"records": [record, ...], "model_manifest_hash": str | None}` (other arm keys, such as section 11.17's `batches`, are ignored). `items` is the registered item set (any hashable IDs). Result: every key of `evaluate` with `n_items` in place of `n_prompts`, plus `direction`, `estimator`, `delta_hat_natural`, `ci_lower_natural`, `ci_upper_natural`, `missing_reasons: {baseline: {reason: count}, candidate: {...}}` (the same key section 11.17 uses). `tierb-collect` and `run` gate through it; the mapping form `evaluate` may stay as the statistics core.
- `gates.is_post_hoc(*, amends, amended_at, judged_checkpoint, observed) -> bool`, `observed = [{checkpoint, arm, created_at}]`, times RFC 3339.
- `items.from_nemo_evaluator(record, *, scorer_key=None) -> record` (new module `gatedtrain/items.py`, a naming choice).
- `registration.normalize(reg) -> dict`: a copy in revision 11 names (legacy clause without `direction` gets `higher_is_better`); the input is unchanged.
- `ledger.check_precision(precision, derivation, *, replay_conformance=False)`: ValueError for a disallowed pair or an unknown value; `ledger.SCHEMA` gains a string field `derivation`.
- `report.build_report(..., hashes=...)`: the `hashes` key `prompt_set_hash` becomes `item_set_hash`; `eval_distribution` comes from the (normalized) registration.

#### Requirements

| ID | Source | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| R11A-1 | R11-19, R11-10, R11-21; 05 §3.1 (05:71-86), §3.7 (05:166) | **Registration in item names.** Clause fields `type, metric, direction, margin, alpha, power, planning_effect, sigma_plan, unit, samples_per_item, eval_seeds, interval, blocking`, optional `estimator` (and section 11.17's `constraint_set`). `direction` ∈ {`higher_is_better`, `lower_is_better`}, required for a clause that uses `samples_per_item`, and equal to the direction the evaluator declares: both prototype evaluators (`gsm8k.nemo_evaluator@0.1`, `addition_em.cpu_stub@0.1`) declare `higher_is_better`, range [0, 1], estimator `mean` **(choice: a prototype table stands in for the evaluator manifest)**. `unit: item` (`training_seed` stays unsupported). `estimator` ∈ {`mean`, `ratio_of_sums`}, absent = `mean`; `ratio_of_sums` is refused for both prototype evaluators **(choice)**. Top-level `eval_distribution` (required unless the legacy alias is given): a mapping with `item_set_hash` (`sha256:<64 hex>`) and optional `env_version`, `env_params_version` (null or a non-empty string; accepted for any evaluator) **(choice of shape)**; other keys refused. Every refusal is `REG-FIELD` on the field (`registration.clause.direction`, `registration.eval_distribution.item_set_hash`, …), and the mutated field is the only error. |
| R11A-2 | R11-19 ("existing artifacts readable") | **Legacy names read.** Each legacy key is an alias of its new key, field by field: `prompt_set_hash` for `eval_distribution.item_set_hash`, `samples_per_prompt` for `samples_per_item`, `unit: prompt` for `item`, gate role `prompts` (type `prompt_dataset`) for `items` (type `item_set`), `cluster_by: prompt` for `item`. A clause with `samples_per_prompt` may omit `direction` (read as `higher_is_better`). A document that gives both forms of one field is refused on the legacy key (`REG-FIELD registration.prompt_set_hash`, `registration.clause.samples_per_prompt`, `SPEC-REF stages[1].inputs.prompts`). Each role keeps its own type (`items` with a `prompt_dataset` artifact, or `prompts` with an `item_set` artifact, is `SPEC-REF` on the role). The registration hash stays the SHA-256 of the file bytes. The demo files and the Tier B fixture template stay in legacy names in this round: they are the legacy-read fixtures (section 11.17's registration builder also mixes a revision 11 clause with the legacy `prompt_set_hash`). |
| R11A-3 | R11-19; 04 §3, §4.2 (04:100) | **Spec.** The gate's evaluation items come through role `items`, artifact type `item_set`; a missing role is `SPEC-REF stages[1].inputs.items`. `cluster_by` maps to `unit`. A training stage's `data` stays `training_dataset`. |
| R11A-4 | R11-19; 05 §3.7 | **New writes use item names** (for legacy and new inputs alike). Report JSON: `n_items`, `samples_per_item`, `unit: "item"`, `direction`, `natural_units: {delta_hat, lower, upper}`, `eval_distribution: {item_set_hash, env_version, env_params_version}`, `missing_reasons`, `interval.bootstrap.resampling_unit: "item"`; no `n_prompts`, `samples_per_prompt`, `prompt_set_hash`. Text lines `n_items: <n>`, `direction: <d>`, `item_set_hash: <h>`, `natural_units: …`; no `n_prompts:` or `prompt_set_hash:` line. Lineage: the gate activity `used` role `items`. Manifests: `manifests/item_set.json` with `type: item_set` (no `prompt_set.json`); lockfile `artifacts.item_set`. |
| R11A-5 | R11-9; 05 §3.8 (05:181) | **Per-item record.** Fields `item, repeat, seed, score, error, sub_metrics, output_ref`, plus `numerator, denominator` for `estimator: ratio_of_sums`. `error` is null or `{class, message}` (MULTIMODAL-SPEC MM-1, as section 11.17). Pairing is by `(item, repeat)`, never by position; item IDs are opaque. `sub_metrics` and `output_ref` never change a result. `from_nemo_evaluator`: `item = problem_idx`, `repeat`, `seed = metadata.eval_seed` (null when absent), `score = reward`, or `scorer_rewards[scorer_key]` with a scorer; a scorer error (`scoring_details[scorer_key].error`, eval_loop.py:719-721 at ea35ba3) becomes score null and an `error`, never 0; `output_ref` names the SHA-256 of `model_response`; exactly the seven fields. |
| R11A-6 | R11-2, R11-11; 05 §3.4 (05:124), §6.1 (05:267) | **Missing items.** Expected items = `items` × range(K) per arm (an item in neither arm is missing in each). Reasons: `absent` (no record), the error class, `out_of_range` (outside `score_range`, either bound may be None; NaN and ±inf always), `no_seed` (seed null or key absent) **(choice: the design lists absent, error class and out_of_range only)**. `missing_<arm>` counts them; `missing_reasons` per arm per reason. Bound: a count over all arms, or a fraction of 2·\|items\|·K. Missing records never enter a pair. `tierb-collect` keeps refusing scorer errors and out-of-range rewards (11.14 D-3, D-11) **(choice; ext-collect follows MM-3/MM-4)**. |
| R11A-7 | R11-7, R11-14; 05 §3.8 (05:179), §3.3 (05:110) | **Clause MISSING from records.** `k_mismatch`: a repeat outside range(K). `seed_mismatch`: a seed other than `eval_seeds[repeat]`. `malformed_record` **(choice)**: an unknown key, both or neither of score and error, a non-numeric or boolean score, a duplicate `(item, repeat)`, a ratio record without numerator or denominator. `unknown_item` **(choice)**: an item outside `items`. `model_hash_mismatch`: with `expected_models` given, an arm whose `model_manifest_hash` differs or is null. **Fewer than K records** for an item are missing items only (supersedes R10A-8's "maximum ≠ K" rule; `k_actual` is still reported). |
| R11A-8 | R11-3, C1; 05 §3.6 (05:156) | **Complete pairs.** An item with at least one repeat present in both arms; its difference is the mean over the repeats present in both. `n_items` counts them and is the n of `min_paired` and of the power formulas. |
| R11A-9 | R11-10; 05 §3.1 (05:56) | **Direction.** Scores of a `lower_is_better` clause are negated before Δ; margins, θ and `bar` are oriented; Δ > 0 favours the candidate. `delta_hat_natural = ±delta_hat`; for `lower_is_better` the natural interval is the oriented one negated with bounds swapped. `baseline_score` and `candidate_score` stay in natural units. Report: R11A-4. |
| R11A-10 | R11-11; 05 §6.1 | **Score range.** Library side in R11A-6 (`score_range`). Floor and ceiling refusal at registration needs baseline data and is deferred (smoke record, MULTIMODAL-SPEC MM-38). |
| R11A-11 | R11-5; 05 §3.8 (05:183) | **p-values by duality.** The mean estimator keeps R10A-10/15/16. A ratio clause's p-value comes from its own bootstrap (p < α where the lower bound clears the null, away from the boundary). No p-value and `p_value_at_floor: false` for no complete pairs or a zero-width interval. |
| R11A-12 | R11-21, C7; 05 §3.1, §3.4 (05:118), §3.6 (05:152) | **Ratio of sums.** `estimator: ratio_of_sums`: R_arm = Σ numerator / Σ denominator over the repeats of complete pairs; Δ = R_candidate − R_baseline, oriented. Interval: item-clustered bootstrap of the ratio difference (resample items with all their repeats, both arms together; variant open, BCa or percentile). `sd_diff` and achieved power from the oriented paired difference of the linearized contributions d_i = (num_i − R·den_i) / mean(den) per arm (num_i, den_i summed over the item's paired repeats), SE = sd·√(DE/n). A missing item leaves both arms' sums. |
| R11A-13 | R11-6; 05 §3.7, 04 §4.2 | **Registration authoritative**, alias-aware: `cluster_by` vs `unit` (`prompt` ≡ `item`), `seeds` vs K, `metric`; each refused as before (`REG-FIELD registration.clause.<field>`). |
| R11A-14 | R11-4; 05 §3.7 | **Evaluator hash** null or absent is refused whatever `signed_by` says (already true; regression). "Unresolved" is not compared at validation (R10A-2 stays): see "Ambiguities". |
| R11A-15 | R11-8; 05 §3.6 | `plan()` refuses TOST with \|θ\| ≥ δ (already true; regression on revision 11 clause names). |
| R11A-16 | R11-1; 05 §3.7 (05:166); decision `post_hoc` rules | **`post_hoc` per judged checkpoint.** `is_post_hoc` is true iff `amends` is set and some observed result has `checkpoint == judged_checkpoint`, `arm == "candidate"` and `created_at` strictly before `amended_at` (instants, not strings). Results of other checkpoints and baseline-arm results do not count. A post-hoc FAIL stays FAIL (R10A-13). Wiring the evidence into `tierb-collect` is deferred (no per-arm checkpoint hash or creation time in NeMo Evaluator arms); the declared `post_hoc` flag keeps working (R10A-4). |
| R11A-17 | R11-2, R11-9; 05 §3.4, §3.8 | **Tier B uses the registered item set.** A held-out problem in neither arm is two missing items per arm (K = 2), reason `absent`: MISSING at bound 0 (exit 1, run written), PASS within the bound; `TIERB-PROMPTS (a)` refuses only problems outside the held-out set. A record without `metadata.eval_seed` is a missing item (`no_seed`). Tier A and Tier B ledger rows are `reconstructed` **(choice: the shard per step is derived from step sample counts and the sequential loader order, decision D-1, not emitted by the trainer)**. Impact results carry `derivation` (`reconstructed` if any contributing record is; null for ledgers written before revision 11) and the text line adds `derivation <value>`. |

R10A-14 now reads "`sd_zero` is true (n ≥ 2; for n = 1 `sd_diff` and `sd_zero` stay null/false)". |
| R11A-23 | rev-11 review HIGH-1 (2026-11-11); 05 §3.1, §3.7, §6.1; R11-10, R11-21 | **The deciding evaluator.** The deciding evaluator is the registration entry of `evaluators` whose `id` equals the spec gate's `evaluator` (`registration.deciding_evaluator(reg, evaluator_id) -> dict | None`), not `evaluators[0]`. Direction, estimator, score range, scorer (G-21) and the run-time hash check (R11A-24) all use it. Validation refuses: `gate.evaluator` not among the ids (`REG-FIELD registration.evaluators`, as before); a `direction` or `estimator` that contradicts the deciding evaluator's declaration (`REG-FIELD registration.clause.direction` / `.estimator`); a deciding evaluator absent from the prototype table `items.EVALUATORS` **(choice: its direction cannot be checked, so it is refused)**: `REG-FIELD registration.evaluators[<i>].id`, i its index; a scorer of the deciding evaluator off the allowlist (`REG-SCORER registration.evaluators[<i>].scorer`). `tierb.registered_scorer(reg, evaluator_id)` returns the deciding evaluator's scorer. The review's repro (decoy id at index 0 with the resolved hash, the real evaluator at index 1 with a null hash, `direction: lower_is_better`, the bad-train negative control) is refused by `validate` and by `run` (exit 3, both errors, no run directory); before the fix it ran and passed. |
| R11A-24 | rev-11 review HIGH-1; 05 §6.1 ("a gate whose evaluator hash differs at run time is MISSING"), R10A-2 | **Run-time hash of the deciding evaluator.** `runner.gate_and_report` compares the run's evaluator hash with the deciding evaluator's `manifest_hash` (by id); a mismatch is MISSING `evaluator_hash_mismatch`, and the report's `evaluator_hash_registered` is the deciding evaluator's hash. Another entry's hash never decides. |
| R11A-25 | rev-11 review MEDIUM-2; 05 §3.7, R13-7 | **Every evaluator hash resolved.** `evaluators[i].manifest_hash` for every i must be `sha256:` plus 64 lower-case hex; null, absent, a tag, a short or upper-case digest is `REG-FIELD registration.evaluators[<i>].manifest_hash` (was: only index 0 null-checked; supersedes the "unresolved is not compared" part of R11A-14). |
| R11A-26 | rev-11 review MEDIUM-3; 05 §3.4, §6.1 | **Ratio parts out of range.** For `estimator: ratio_of_sums`, a scored record whose `numerator` or `denominator` is not finite, or whose `denominator` is ≤ 0, is a missing item with reason `out_of_range` (not `malformed_record`, which stays for absent or non-numeric parts). It leaves both arms' sums (R11A-12); no NaN or ±inf reaches Δ̂, the interval, `sd_diff` or the power, so such data never give PASS or FAIL. |
| R11A-27 | rev-11 review LOW-4 | **Duplicate item IDs.** `evaluate_items(..., items=...)` with a repeated ID raises `ValueError` **(choice: a caller error, the registered item set is a set; not a clause MISSING)**. |

Blank lines are skipped. `gates.load_paired_records` keeps NeMo Evaluator's skip-and-last-wins behaviour (`test_pairing`); the gate path must not use it. **Extended to `merge-eval` (re-review M-1, 2026-11-11).** `gatedtrain merge-eval` reads each single-seed run with the same per-line rule (`runner.results_lines`, shared with `read_arm_records`), not `load_paired_records`: a line that does not parse, is not a JSON object or has no `problem_idx`, a `problem_idx` or `repeat` that is not an integer **(choice: nothing is coerced)**, and a second record for the same `(problem_idx, repeat)` refuse the merge with `MERGE-EVAL runs[<r>]` (line number and fix; at most five lines named, then a count); nothing is written. Blank lines are skipped. So R11A-28 holds end to end: the merged file cannot hide a bad or duplicate line. |
| R11A-29 | rev-11 review LOW-6; 05 §6.1 (R13-9), §3.7 (R13-7) | **Pilot baseline mean; `created_at` with `amends`.** Top-level `baseline_pilot_mean` (05 §6.1: "stated in the registration beside `sigma_pilot`"; name: a naming choice) is optional **(choice: absent = not checked, so existing registrations stay valid)**; when given it must be a finite number inside the deciding evaluator's declared range and not equal to a closed bound (an open bound checks nothing on that side): `REG-FIELD registration.baseline_pilot_mean` (bool, string, null, NaN, outside, at floor or ceiling). This resolves ambiguity (f) and the R11A-10 deferral. `created_at` is required when `amends` is given (`REG-FIELD registration.created_at`). |
| R11A-30 | rev-11 review LOW-7 | **Boundaries pinned.** A seedless record within the bound is a missing item `no_seed`, never `seed_mismatch`, clause not MISSING. R13-8 pinned with unequal repeat counts and unequal arm denominators: Δ̂, arm ratios, BCa interval (item jackknife, z0 with mid-rank ties, same index stream `default_rng(seed).integers(0, n, (B, n))` over complete pairs in registered order), `sd_diff` and power equal an independent implementation to 10 places (num_i, den_i summed over the repeats usable in both arms; d_i with each arm's own mean(den)). `max_unpaired` equality is allowed ("exceed", 05 §3.4) for count and fraction. `is_post_hoc`: an observed result at exactly `amended_at` is not before it (choice 10 now tested). |

**Status reasons added.** MISSING: `malformed_record`, `unknown_item`, `model_hash_mismatch`. Missing-item reasons: `absent`, `out_of_range`, `no_seed`, an error class.

**Choices.** Maintainers' decisions 2026-11-11: (1) reversed (an item outside the set is MISSING `unknown_item`, EXT-1); (2) to (14) accepted: (2) evaluator errors are gate-missing items (EXT-4); (3) model-hash mismatch is MISSING (EXT-11); (4) evaluator identity hash fields (EXT-6); (5) component list and top-level final model not a cut (EXT-13, EXT-14); (6) absent `_name_or_path` refuses (EXT-20); (7) `first_step` null for `stage` edges (EXT-19; `derivation` null is now R13-10); (8) the 5 % boundary is allowed (EXT-29; the binary rule is now a maintainers' decision); (9) a START without a terminator refuses (EXT-28); (10) `training_seed` from the launch record (EXT-32); (11) `records/` copies (EXT-2); (12) adapter name `trl_grpo`; (13) registration key `contamination.allow_exact_overlap` (EXT-21); (14) trainer config `{backend, profile}` (EXT-2). Open, new in this round: `item_meta` carries the response hash in `ext-from-nel` (EXT-35); `EXT-NEL` for an evaluator outside the adapter or registration (EXT-37).

**Ambiguities found in the design (not edited).** (a) 05:179 lists "a seed other than `eval_seeds[repeat]`" as MISSING while 05:181 makes `seed` required, and 05:124 lists only three missing-item reasons: a record with no seed has no stated status (choice 1). (b) 05:166 "validation refuses a null or unresolved hash": "unresolved" is undefined for the prototype, which has no lockfile resolution step; a hash that differs from the resolved one is MISSING at run time (R10A-2), not refused. (c) 05:118 does not name the bootstrap variant for ratio metrics (BCa for means); the tests accept either. (d) 05:166 "a candidate-arm raw-results artifact … created before the amended registration": the registration has no creation time and NeMo Evaluator arms carry no checkpoint identity, so the prototype cannot audit it without new fields (R11A-16). (e) 05:152 defines d_i with "num_i" and "den_i" per item without saying sum or mean over repeats; they coincide only when every item has the same number of paired repeats (the tests use equal repeats). (f) 05:267 refuses a deciding metric whose baseline lies at the floor or ceiling "at registration", but the registration holds no baseline data; the prototype's smoke record (MM-38) is the only place it can be checked.

**Deferred (not in this round).** `model_manifest_hash` of Tier B checkpoints over the deployable subset (08 §3.6) and in the cut manifest (02 §2.4): Tier B evaluates a consolidated Hugging Face export that `tierb-collect` does not receive, and the prototype writes no cut manifest; `base_ref` and `lora_compose` (no adapter checkpoints in the current modules; MM-18); `stage`-precision edges in the impact query (no producer yet; the edge-precision layout is an MM-23 decision); environment parameters as a `DatasetSnapshot` (no environment RL yet; MM-25); learned-scorer validity (C6; no learned scorer yet, MM-GAP-11); floor and ceiling refusal (ambiguity f); `post_hoc` evidence in `tierb-collect` (ambiguity d); `unit: training_seed`; the export's `export.json` field `prompt_set_hash` (an H-1 artifact on the host; `verify-export` must keep reading it).

**Existing tests updated for R11A-23 to R11A-30.** None expected: no test calls `registered_scorer`, and the demo and Tier B registrations have one evaluator whose id equals the spec gate's. **Existing tests changed (R11A-29, accepted 2026-11-11):** the rule "`created_at` is required with `amends`" refused five revision 10 fixtures that set `amends` alone; each now also sets `created_at: "2026-11-11T09:00:00Z"`: `test_r10_registration.TestAmendmentFields.test_amends_accepted` and `test_post_hoc_accepted`, `test_r10_tierb.TestAmendment.mutate`, `TestPostHoc.mutate` (three tests) and `TestLongRunShapedRegression.setUpClass` (`rev10`). The value does not change their results: the gate reads the declared `post_hoc` flag only (post_hoc evidence is not wired, R11A-16).

### 11.17 Design revision 12: constraint clauses and blocking judges (2026-11-11)

**Sources.** `design.md` change table R12-1 to R12-5 and R13-1 to R13-4 (design revision 13, see "Revision 13 edits" below); 05 §3.9 (constraint clauses), §3.1 (`constraint_set` clause field), §3.4 (missingness), §3.6, §3.7 (registration and report contents for constraint clauses), §3.8 (MISSING on a missed canary and on a scorer-hash mismatch), §6.1 (the judge exception, conditions (a) to (e)); 04 §4.2 (constraint gates, rule 2); 06 §9 and B20 (an external judge receiving candidate outputs is an `upload` destination), §12.1 and B26; verification plan §8 VV-GATE-1, SEC-B26, STAT-06. Maintainers' decision: the multimodal decisions, last two bullets. Record format: `MULTIMODAL-SPEC.md` §1a (MM-C1 to MM-C4).

**Dependency on revision 11 (section 11.16).** The tests use the revision 11 names: per-item records with `item`, clause fields `unit: item`, `samples_per_item`, `direction`. Until the rename lands the registration cases also fail on those names; that is expected.

**Scope.** Library level only: a new module `gatedtrain.constraints`, `registration.check_contents` / `check_against_gate`, `gates.combine`, and `report.constraint_report`. The prototype never calls a judge model: it reads recorded verdicts (MM-C1 to MM-C4). Wiring a constraint gate into `run`, `tierb-collect` or `ext-collect`, the CLI text output, and multiplicity (a constraint clause counts in m, 05 §3.5) are not covered here. Error rules added: `REG-CONSTRAINT` (constraint set or constraint clause malformed), `REG-JUDGE` (a judge on a blocking constraint clause fails 05 §6.1 (a)-(e), or canaries are missing), `REG-EGRESS` (an external judge without a recorded egress approval). Each prints the usual `error[RULE] field: message | fix: ...` line and exits 3 from commands that load the registration.

**Prototype choices, to confirm (marked P in the table).** P1 the threshold form is `type: superiority` with `bar` (04 §4.2 maps `gate.threshold` to superiority against `bar`); `margin` is not used there. P2 a constraint clause has no σ plan: `sigma_plan` and `sigma_pilot` are not required (power and n come from the binomial, 05 §3.9). P3 the non-inferiority interval is `clopper_pearson_discordant` (see R12A-6), not the conditional exact McNemar (now the design rule, R13-1). P4 (revised for R13-2) non-inferiority power and n are binomial as in the threshold form, with δ for `bar` and the planning rate of c/n; the design names "a registered planning rate of c/n" without a field, and the prototype reads it as `margin − planning_effect` (the threshold form's construction), so `planning_effect` lies in (0, `margin`] and no clause field is added. P5 the independence inputs (`policy_family`, `training_reward_families`, tier, transfer-test reference) are declared in the registration under `judge_qualification`, because the prototype has no lineage closure to compute them. P6 the validity bounds use the clause's α (now the design rule, R13-3). P7 a judge checker must state `no_tool_reason` (how "programmatic checkers preferred" is checked). P8 a score other than 0 or 1 is a missing item with reason `out_of_range`. P9 `constraint_set_hash` covers the whole `constraint_set` mapping (texts, checkers, judges' scorer fields, canaries); the qualification evidence (`judge_qualification`) is outside it.

#### Requirements

| ID | Source | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| R12A-1 | R12-1, R12-4; 05 §3.1, §3.7, §3.9; 04 §4.2 | **Constraint clause registration.** A clause with `constraint_set: <id>` is a constraint clause. Allowed forms: threshold (`type: superiority` with `bar`, P1) and `non_inferiority` (with `margin`, the acceptable increase of the violation rate); `equivalence` is refused (`REG-CONSTRAINT registration.clause.type`). `direction` must be `lower_is_better` (`… clause.direction`). Threshold: `bar` a number in (0, 1) (`… clause.bar`); `planning_effect` in (0, `bar`], so the planning violation rate `bar − planning_effect` lies in [0, `bar`) (`… clause.planning_effect`). Non-inferiority: `margin` required as for every blocking clause (`REG-MARGIN`), in (0, 1) (`… clause.margin`); `planning_effect` in (0, `margin`], so the planning rate of c/n `margin − planning_effect` lies in [0, `margin`) (`… clause.planning_effect`; R13-2, P4). `interval`: `clopper_pearson` for the threshold form, `clopper_pearson_discordant` for non-inferiority (`… clause.interval`). `sigma_plan` and `sigma_pilot` not required (P2). The registration holds `constraint_set` (a mapping whose `id` equals the clause's; absent or different: `REG-CONSTRAINT registration.constraint_set`; a registered set that no clause names is refused the same way) and `judge_qualification` (required when the set has judges). |
| R12A-2 | R12-1; 05 §3.9 | **Constraint set.** `constraints`: a non-empty list numbered `c1`, `c2`, … in order (`… constraint_set.constraints`); each with a non-empty `text` (`… constraints[i].text`) and one `checker`: `{kind: tool, tool, manifest_hash: sha256:<64 hex>}` or `{kind: judge, judge: <id in judges>, no_tool_reason: <non-empty>}` (`… constraints[i].checker`, `… checker.judge`, `… checker.no_tool_reason`; P7). `judges.<id>`: `model_id`, `model_version` (a version or date), `family` (backbone), `llm_judge`, `endpoint` (`local` or `external`), `version_pinnable`, `prompt_template_sha256`, `decoding`, `parser: {name, sha256}`, `response_cache`; a missing scorer field is `REG-CONSTRAINT registration.constraint_set.judges.<id>.<field>`. `canaries`: `[{id, violates: [cIDs], output_sha256, injection}]`. |
| R12A-3 | R12-1; 05 §3.9, §3.8, §6.1 (d) | **Scorer hash.** `constraints.constraint_set_hash(cs)` = `"sha256:"` + SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the constraint set (P9). It changes with any text, tool hash, judge model version, template, decoding, parser or canary. For a constraint clause `evaluators[0].manifest_hash` must equal it (`REG-CONSTRAINT registration.evaluators[0].manifest_hash`). At run time an arm whose `constraint_set_hash` differs is MISSING with reason `evaluator_hash_mismatch`. |
| R12A-4 | R12-1; 05 §3.9, §3.4, §3.8 | **Violation indicator.** Records are the revision 11 per-item records (MM-C1); `score` 1 means the output violates any constraint. `violation_indicators(records)` maps each item with at least one usable repeat to v_i = max over its usable repeats. A record with an `error` (for example `unparseable_judge_response`) or a score not in {0, 1} (P8, reason `out_of_range`) is a missing item, never a 0; a repeat without a record is missing with reason `absent`. `missing_reasons[arm]` counts reasons; above `max_unpaired` the clause is MISSING (`missing_above_bound`); a seed other than `eval_seeds[repeat]` is MISSING (`seed_mismatch`). `sub_metrics` (violated constraint IDs, rationale) never decide. |
| R12A-5 | R12-2; 05 §3.9, §3.6 | **Threshold form.** One arm (a baseline argument raises `ValueError`). n = items with a usable repeat; k = Σ v_i. `upper_bound` = exact one-sided upper (1 − α) Clopper-Pearson bound, Beta⁻¹(1 − α; k + 1, n − k), 1 when k = n (`clopper_pearson_upper`); zero violations give 1 − α^(1/n), about 3.69/n at α = 0.025. PASS when `upper_bound` < `bar`. `p_value` = P(X ≤ k; n, `bar`), dual to the bound. `threshold_power(n, bar, planning_rate, alpha)` = P(bound < bar) with X ~ Bin(n, planning_rate); `binomial_n_required(bar, planning_rate, alpha, power)` = the smallest such n with power ≥ target (368 for bar 0.01, rate 0, α 0.025; 299 at α 0.05). `achieved_power` at the planning rate; `underpowered` when it is below target or n < `n_required`; a non-rejection is FAIL when powered, else INCONCLUSIVE (`underpowered`). Zero violations set `sd_zero: true` but are **not** a zero-width interval; n < `min_paired` is INCONCLUSIVE (`min_paired`). |
| R12A-6 | R12-2; 05 §3.9, §3.4 | **Non-inferiority form.** Paired on items; v_i per arm over the repeats present in both arms; n = complete pairs. Counts: `violation_count` (candidate), `baseline_violation_count`, `candidate_only` (c), `baseline_only` (b). `delta_hat` = (b − c)/n (oriented), `difference` = (c − b)/n (natural, candidate − baseline). `upper_bound` = `clopper_pearson_upper(c, n, α)`, an exact upper bound of p_cand − p_base = p01 − p10 ≤ p01 (P3). PASS when `upper_bound` < `margin` (R13-1). Power and n by R13-2 and P4: `threshold_power(n, margin, margin − planning_effect, α)` and `binomial_n_required(...)` (436 at δ 0.02, rate 0.005, α 0.025, power 0.8); non-rejection FAIL when powered, else INCONCLUSIVE. No violation in either arm: bound 1 − α^(1/n), positive width, `sd_zero: true`, PASS when below δ. A missing baseline raises `ValueError`. |
| R12A-7 | 05 §3.4, §3.6, §3.8 | **Revision 10 rules carry over.** `evaluate_constraint(..., min_paired=10, max_unpaired=0)`; MISSING takes precedence over INCONCLUSIVE; `status_reasons` as in 11.15 plus `canary_missed`, `canary_absent`, `judge_not_qualified`, `egress_unapproved`. |
| R12A-8 | R12-2; 05 §3.9, §3.5, §3.8; 04 §4.2 | **Combination.** The constraint clause's result and the quality clause's result combine by `gates.combine([...], "and")`: FAIL if any fails, else MISSING, else INCONCLUSIVE, else PASS. |
| R12A-9 | R12-3; 05 §6.1 (a) | **Validity.** `judge_qualification.judges.<id>.validity`: `labelled_violations`, `misses`, `labelled_compliant`, `false_alarms`, `max_miss_rate`, optional `max_false_alarm_rate`, `label_source`. Miss rate = misses / labelled violations, false-alarm rate = false alarms / labelled compliant, each with its Clopper-Pearson upper bound at the clause's α (R13-3, P6). The judge fails (a) when the miss-rate bound exceeds `max_miss_rate`, or the false-alarm bound exceeds a registered `max_false_alarm_rate`, or validity is absent. |
| R12A-10 | R12-3; 05 §6.1 (b), §6.2 | **Independence.** Fails (b) when `tier` is not `T2` or higher, when `transfer_test` is absent, or when the judge's `family` equals `policy_family` or any of `training_reward_families` (P5). |
| R12A-11 | R12-3, R12-5, R13-4; 05 §6.1 (c), §3.8; 06 §12.1, B26 | **Canaries.** Per-batch canaries apply only to a judge that blocks a constraint clause alone, that is a judge on a blocking constraint clause (R13-4). There a set with judges needs a non-empty registered canary set (fails (c); refused `REG-JUDGE registration.constraint_set.canaries`); a non-blocking clause (supporting judge) needs none, and its batch canaries are reported but never make it MISSING. At run time, for a blocking clause, per arm: every judge batch must hold at least one registered canary and every judged record must be in a batch, else MISSING `canary_absent` (`canaries.batches_without_canary`, `canaries.unbatched_records`); a canary with `flagged: false` is MISSING `canary_missed` (`canaries.missed: [{batch, canary}]`). Canaries never enter v_i. A tool-only set needs no batches. |
| R12A-12 | R12-3; 05 §6.1 (d) | **Reproducibility.** Fails (d) when `decoding.temperature` is not 0, when `model_version` is absent, empty or `latest`, or when `response_cache` is not `JudgeResponseCache`. A changed judge changes the hash (R12A-3). |
| R12A-13 | R12-3, R12-5; 05 §6.1 (e); 06 §9, B20 | **External judges.** A judge with `endpoint: external` needs `judge_qualification.judges.<id>.egress_approval: {approval_id, destination: upload, data_tags}` with a non-empty id and tags; otherwise the registration is refused `REG-EGRESS … egress_approval`, blocking or not, and at run time the clause is MISSING `egress_unapproved`. An external judge with `version_pinnable: false` fails (e) (it may only support). The gate reads recorded verdicts only: no socket is opened (tests patch `socket` to fail). |
| R12A-14 | R12-3, R12-5; 05 §6.1; 04 §4.2 rule 2; VV-GATE-1 | **A judge that fails a condition cannot block alone.** `judge_qualification(judge_id, cs, q, alpha)` returns `can_block_alone`, `failed` (codes `a_validity`, `b_independence`, `c_canaries`, `d_reproducibility`, `e_external`) and the four rates and bounds. On a blocking constraint clause a judge checker that fails is refused `REG-JUDGE` with the field of the failing input (`judge_qualification.judges.<id>.validity` or `.tier`, `constraint_set.judges.<id>.family`, `.decoding`, `.model_version`, `.response_cache`, `.version_pinnable`): each constraint has one deciding checker, so the clause would have no qualified blocking evaluator. At run time (second layer) the clause is MISSING `judge_not_qualified`. On a non-blocking clause the judge is supporting: no `REG-JUDGE`, no `judge_not_qualified` (REG-EGRESS still applies). Quality clauses keep the non-LLM rule unchanged. |
| R12A-15 | R12-4; 04 §4.2 | **Spec mapping.** `gate.threshold` compiles to `superiority`; spec `test.constraint_set` maps to `clause.constraint_set`; a differing value is `REG-FIELD registration.clause.constraint_set` (G-4). |
| R12A-16 | R12-1, R12-2; 05 §3.7 | **Report.** `report.constraint_report(result)` returns: `form`, `status`, `status_reasons`, `violation_count`, `n_items`, `upper_bound`, `interval_method`, `bar` or `margin`, `alpha_used`, `p_value`, `achieved_power`, `underpowered`, `n_required`, `sd_zero`, `missing_candidate` (and `missing_baseline`), `missing_reasons`, `constraint_set_id`, `constraint_set_hash`, `constraints: [{id, checker}]`, `judges.<id>: {model_id, model_version, endpoint, tier, family, miss_rate, miss_rate_upper, false_alarm_rate, false_alarm_upper, max_miss_rate, max_false_alarm_rate, can_block_alone, failed, egress_approval_id}`, `canaries: {registered, batches, checked, missed}`; for non-inferiority also `baseline_violation_count`, `candidate_only`, `baseline_only`, `delta_hat`, `difference`. `evaluate_constraint` returns the same statistics, plus `judges` and `canaries` (with `batches_without_canary`, `unbatched_records`). |
| R12A-17 | R12-1; 05 §3.8 | **Recorded verdict format.** `MULTIMODAL-SPEC.md` §1a, MM-C1 to MM-C4. `evaluate_constraint(clause, constraint_set, judge_qualification, candidate, baseline=None, *, min_paired=10, max_unpaired=0)`; an arm is `{records, batches, constraint_set_hash}`. |
| R12A-18 | rev-12 review; 05 §3.4 (expected items = registered item set × K) | **Registered items required.** `evaluate_constraint(..., *, items, ...)`: `items` is a required keyword (no default from the arms' records, which let deleted records vanish from the expected set). Repro: 420 items, the 10 violating items' records and batch entries deleted: MISSING `missing_above_bound`, `missing_reasons.candidate = {absent: 10}`; never PASS at bounds 0, 5 or 0.01. Supersedes the "absent, the items of the arms' records" sentence of R12A-17. |
| R12A-19 | rev-12 review; 05 §3.3, §3.8 (R11-14); R11A-7 | **Evaluated model identity.** Each arm carries `model_manifest_hash`; `evaluate_constraint(..., expected_models=None)` as `gates.evaluate_items`: with `expected_models` given, an arm whose hash differs, is null or absent is MISSING `model_hash_mismatch` (baseline arm too, non-inferiority form); None means no check. MULTIMODAL-SPEC MM-C3, MM-C4 updated. |
| R12A-20 | rev-12 review | **Paths kept apart.** `gates.evaluate_items` raises `ValueError` on a clause with `constraint_set` (it would analyse violations as a mean). The existing `REG-CONSTRAINT registration.clause.constraint_set` refusals of `spec.load_spec` and `tierb.load_registration` get tests (review mutants M31, M32). |
| R12A-21 | rev-12 review; 05 §6.1 (c); MM-C2 | **Batches and canaries.** A batch whose `judge` is not a judge of the registered set, or a record in more than one batch of the same judge, is MISSING `malformed_batch` **(choice: new reason)**. Every judge on a blocking clause must cover every judged record with its own batches holding a registered canary, else MISSING `canary_absent` (two judges, batches from one only). A canary whose `flagged` is anything but `true` (absent key, `"yes"`, 1, null) is missed (`canary_missed`; pins mutant M13). Registration: on a blocking clause, a registered canary that violates no judge-checked constraint is `REG-JUDGE registration.constraint_set.canaries[<i>]`. An egress approval whose `destination` is not `upload` is `REG-EGRESS` at registration and MISSING `egress_unapproved` at run time (pins mutant M17). |
| R12A-22 | rev-12 review; 05 §6.1 (b), (d) | **Judge strings.** `model_version` fails (d) when empty after stripping or equal, case- and whitespace-insensitively, to a floating alias: `latest`, `current`, `stable`, `default`, `newest` (`REG-JUDGE registration.constraint_set.judges.<id>.model_version`; run time `judge_not_qualified`); a version that merely contains an alias (`latest-2025-01-28`) is pinned. `family` is compared with `policy_family` and `training_reward_families` after stripping and case folding (`REG-JUDGE … family`). |
| R12A-23 | rev-12 review; 05 §3.7, §6.2; 06 §9 | **Report and arm approval id.** `constraint_report(...).judges.<id>` adds `transfer_test` (the transfer-test reference). For an external judge each arm records `egress_approval_id` (MM-C3); a value other than the registration's `approval_id`, null or absent is MISSING `egress_unapproved` (either arm). Local judges need no id. |
| R12A-24 | rev-12 review | **Deferred (not in this round).** Binding the clause's `blocking` to the spec gate, and excluding supporting (non-blocking) clauses from `combine`: they come with the `ext-collect` wiring. |
| R12A-25 | re-review (2026-11-11); 05 §3.4, §3.8, §3.9; R11A-23, R11A-28 | **Deferred: wiring constraint clauses through `ext-collect`.** That wiring must: (a) have `constraints.check_registration` take the spec gate's evaluator id and compare the deciding entry's `manifest_hash` (the entry with that id) with `constraint_set_hash(registered constraint set)`, refusing an id that is missing or listed twice (today it reads `evaluators[0]`, R12A-3); (b) pass the registered `constraint_set` to `evaluate_constraint` and require each arm's `constraint_set_hash` to equal the deciding entry's hash (today it is compared with the hash of the set passed in); (c) take `items` from the registered item set and `expected_models` from the run's model manifests; (d) read verdict records with the strict reader (R11A-28: no line skipped, no duplicate overwritten). Until then every command refuses a constraint clause (`REG-CONSTRAINT`, R12A-20). |
| R12A-26 | re-review L-3; R11A-23 | **Evaluator ids are unique.** A registration that lists one evaluator id more than once is refused `REG-FIELD registration.evaluators[<i>].id`, i each later occurrence: the deciding evaluator is found by id, so a repeated id is ambiguous (before, the first entry decided and a scorer on a later duplicate was silently ignored). |
| R12A-27 | re-review L-1, L-4; R11A-23 | **Tier B commands gate on the Tier B evaluator.** `tierb.load_registration` (`merge-eval`, `nel-config`) checks the registration with the deciding evaluator `tierb.EVALUATOR_ID` wherever it is listed (a decoy listed first decides nothing; regression test, the mutant `check_contents(reg)` must fail it), and refuses a registration that does not list it: `REG-FIELD registration.evaluators` (no index-0 fallback). `tierb-collect` already refuses that through the spec gate (G-4). |
| R12A-28 | re-review L-5; R11A-26 | **Extreme ratio parts.** A ratio part too large to convert to a float (`OverflowError`, for example the integer 10**400), or outside the finite range \|numerator\| ≤ 10^12 and 10^-12 ≤ denominator ≤ 10^12 **(choice: `gates.RATIO_PART_LIMIT`; the range keeps every arm sum, ratio, contribution, jackknife power and bootstrap ratio finite)**, is a missing item `out_of_range`, never an exception; the limits themselves are usable. A score too large to convert is `out_of_range` too. Before, 10**400 raised `OverflowError` and one numerator of 1e308 raised `ValueError` in the bootstrap. |

Before implementation the file had 97 tests (revision 12): 7 passed (the 3 guards and 4 that pin behaviour the code already had: `test_noninferiority_margin_required`, `test_non_blocking_clause_allows_supporting_judge`, `TestSpecMapping.test_threshold_gate_maps_to_superiority`, `test_noninferiority_gate_with_threshold_registration_refused`) and 90 failed: 64 with `ModuleNotFoundError: gatedtrain.constraints`, 26 registration cases on missing `REG-CONSTRAINT` / `REG-JUDGE` / `REG-EGRESS` problems (and, for the three valid-registration cases, on unknown fields and the revision 11 names). The three revision 13 tests below came with the implementation; the file now has 100 tests and all pass (2026-11-11).

**Review fixes (R12A-18 to R12A-23), state before the fix (2026-11-11).** `tests/test_r12_fixes.py`, 32 tests. 15 pass on the current code as regressions or pins (the deleted-items repro when `items` is passed, keyword-only `items`, `test_flagged_key_absent_is_not_flagged` and `test_flagged_must_be_true` (M13), `TestEgressDestination` (M17), `TestCommandsRefuseConstraintClause` (M31, M32), and controls). 17 fail: `items` still optional; no `expected_models`; `evaluate_items` accepting a constraint clause (INCONCLUSIVE); batches of an unregistered judge, a record in two batches and a blocking judge without batches all PASS; no canary-coverage registration check; `current`, `stable`, `default`, `newest` accepted and family compared case-sensitively; no `transfer_test` in the judge report; the arm's `egress_approval_id` not compared. Mutation check (2026-11-11, review mutants M01 to M33 on a scratch copy of the live package, this file only): M02, M13, M17, M22, M26, M31, M32 killed; the others are covered by `test_r12_constraints.py`, not targeted here.

**Existing tests updated for R12A-18 to R12A-23.** `test_r12_constraints.evaluate_constraint` (the helper) and the direct `evaluate_constraint` calls must pass `items` (the registered item IDs, `i0000` … for the matrix size); `TestJudgeAtRunTime.test_external_judge_recorded_verdicts_no_network` and any external-judge PASS case must set the arm's `egress_approval_id` to `EGRESS["approval_id"]`.

**Re-review fixes (R11A-28 extended, R12A-26 to R12A-28; L-2), state before the fix (2026-11-11).** `tests/test_r12_rereview_fixes.py`, 24 tests. 7 pass on the current code as pins or controls: `TestConstraintDuplicateItems` (2, L-2), `TestTierbDecidingEvaluator` (3, L-1: the live code already passes the Tier B id when it is listed), `test_blank_lines_merge`, `test_at_limit_is_usable`. 17 fail: merge-eval accepts an unparsable line, a non-object line (exit 1, `AttributeError`), a line without `problem_idx`, a string `problem_idx` and a duplicate (exit 0, clean merged file); duplicate evaluator ids validate; registrations without the Tier B evaluator load and `nel-config` writes configs; extreme ratio parts raise `OverflowError` (10**400) or `ValueError` (1e308), or are used (1e13, 1e-13, 1e-320). After the fix all 24 pass. Mutation check (2026-11-11, scratch copy of the live package, this file only): killed `check_contents(reg)` in `load_registration` (L-1), the dropped not-listed check (L-4), the dropped duplicate-id check (L-3), the dropped range limit and the dropped `OverflowError` catch (L-5), and the dropped duplicate and integer checks of merge-eval (M-1).

**Revision 13 edits (after design revision 13, 2026-11-11).** The tests were written against revision 12 and then aligned with R13-1 to R13-4: (1) R13-2 (power and n of the non-inferiority form are binomial, not the 05 §3.6 normal formula): the non-inferiority fixture clause plans at `planning_effect: 0.015` (planning rate of c/n 0.005) instead of 0.0; `test_underpowered_non_rejection_inconclusive` uses `planning_effect: 0.005` at δ 0.01 and the binomial reference power 0.135 (was the normal formula, 0.37); `test_powered_fail` checks the binomial power; `test_counts_and_bound` checks `n_required` = 436; `test_no_violations_anywhere_is_not_zero_width` and `test_baseline_arm_canary_missed` (δ 0.01) set `planning_effect: 0.01`; new `test_noninferiority_planning_effect`. (2) R13-4 (per-batch canaries only for a judge that blocks alone): new `test_supporting_judge_needs_no_batch_canaries` and `test_supporting_judge_needs_no_canary_set`. (3) R13-1 and R13-3 needed no test change: the tests already used `clopper_pearson_discordant` with the bound of c/n, and the clause's α for the validity bounds. With `gatedtrain.constraints` in place all 100 pass.

### 11.18 External-run adapters, slice 1: generic `ext-collect` and the TRL GRPO adapter (2026-11-11)

**Sources.** `MULTIMODAL-SPEC.md` MM-1 to MM-39 and the multimodal decisions; design revision 13: 05 §3.3, §3.4, §3.8 (per-item record, R11-2, R11-9, R11-14, R13-6), §6.1 (score range R11-11, independence tiers), §11 (`model_manifest_hash` R11-14), §11.2 (precision and derivation R11-18), §11.3 (`stage` edges R11-12); 08 §2.4 item 1 (completeness rule R11-15), §3.6 (deployable components R12-6). MM-GAP-5, -8, -10, -14 are resolved by R11-12, R11-14, R11-19, R11-15; MM-GAP-2, -3 by R11-10, R11-11; MM-GAP-6 by R11-18; MM-GAP-13 by R11-21. Area 1 only: TRL GRPO, Qwen3-0.6B, GSM8K, evaluator `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator_items@0.1`. **Moved (2026-11-17):** the TRL adapter, its rows and its own tests are in `gatedtrain-recipe-trl-grpo-gsm8k`; gatedtrain's tests of this slice run on the synthetic test adapter (`gatedtrain.testing.synthetic`: the same run layout of Hugging Face Trainer cuts with `trainer_state.json` last, `run_args.json` with `model_name_or_path`, `seed`, `profile`, `dry_run`, `max_steps`, `train_file`, `train_file_sha256`, `rows_per_step` and `shuffle`, an optional `samples.jsonl`, `env-freeze.txt` pinning `synthetic-trainer`). Where a requirement below is TRL's (the GRPO batch shape of EXT-48 and EXT-49, the TRL citations, the area-1 README and records), its tests are the recipe's.

**Decision IDs.** Prefix `EXT-`. **(choice)** marks a prototype choice listed under "Choices".

**TRL layout (checked against the pinned clones, 2026-11-11).** trl 1.16.0 (`<mm-root>/src/trl-v1.16.0`) and transformers 5.17.0 (`<mm-root>/src/transformers-v5.17.0`), byte-identical to the area-1 venv; citations in the adapter module and the `tests/_ext.py` docstring. `checkpoint-<global_step>/` written by `Trainer._save_checkpoint` with `trainer_state.json` last (trainer.py:3222, :3229, :3252-3255, :3269; TRL grpo_trainer.py:3389-3390); the cut's tokenizer files are `tokenizer.json`, `tokenizer_config.json`, `chat_template.jinja`; `config.json` never carries `_name_or_path` (configuration_utils.py:1068-1069); `training_args.bin` is a pickle and is never read: the launcher `<mm-root>/area1-trl/train_grpo.py` writes the run's arguments as JSON to `run_args.json` at the top of the output directory, next to `env-freeze.txt`, `env-runtime.json`, `samples.jsonl`, `memory.jsonl`, `attempts.jsonl` and TRL's model card `README.md`. An area-1 smoke run has not yet shown the layout on disk (`checkpoint_completeness.verified` stays false).

**Interfaces (module names are naming choices).**
- `gatedtrain/ext/registry.py`: `TRAINER_ROWS: {adapter: {uses, pins, outputs, components, consumption: {target, fallback}, gpu_h_estimate, evaluators}}`, `EVALUATOR_ROWS: {evaluator_id: {metric, direction, score_range, scorer: {name, version, weights_sha256, code_sha256}, tier, binary, error_classes, score_outcomes, payload_text_field, ...}}`, `adapter(name) -> instance` (KeyError when unknown), `evaluator_identity_hash(evaluator_id)`.
- the adapter (TRL: the recipe's `src/gatedtrain_recipe_trl_grpo_gsm8k/adapter.py`; gatedtrain's tests: `gatedtrain/testing/synthetic.py`): `trainer_id`, `evaluator_ids`, `metric(evaluator_id)`, `checkpoint_completeness: {library, rule, verified}`, `find_cuts(stage_out) -> (complete: [(step, path)], incomplete: [path])`, `identity(cut, base) -> {model_hash, cut_hash, components, base_ref}`, `consumption(stage_out, data, cuts) -> {precision, rows, ...}`, `framework_identity(stage_out, launch)`, `checks(...)`.
- `gatedtrain/ext/launch.py`: `gpu_seconds(log_path, label) -> int` (ValueError when the label has no END, an attempt is unterminated, or the label is absent). `gatedtrain/ext/smoke.py`: `smoke_verdict(record, *, score_range, binary) -> {verdict, reasons}`.
- `gatedtrain ext-from-nel --registration R --adapter NAME --evaluator ID --items DIR --checkpoint DIR --out DIR RUN_0 ... RUN_{K-1}` (EXT-35 to EXT-38).
- CLI: `gatedtrain ext-collect SPEC --adapter NAME --stage-out DIR --launch-record FILE --smoke-record FILE --eval-baseline DIR --eval-candidate DIR [--runs-root R]` (exit 0 PASS, 1 gate not PASS, 3 refused with nothing written); `gatedtrain ext-merge-eval --registration R --out DIR RUN...`; `gatedtrain ext-launch --adapter NAME --profile smoke|full --request REQ.yaml --gpu-log GPU_LOG.md [--smoke-record FILE] [--spec SPEC] [--allow-network] --out-record FILE` (stdout is exactly the command and a newline; `--spec` required when the adapter binds the spec's mounts, EXT-84; `--allow-network`, EXT-81); `gatedtrain ext-launch --adapter NAME --recheck RECORD [--spec SPEC]` (EXT-69: exit 0 and `recheck: ok <label>`, or 3). Host layout (EXT-63, EXT-66, EXT-67, EXT-79): `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_HOST_WORK`, `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_OUTPUT_ROOT`, `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_INPUT_ROOTS` (defaults from the host file, no built-in host path, and the adapter's registry `input_roots`; review round 4); each set variable prints a `warning: ...` line on stderr (EXT-85).
- Error rules added: `EXT-SPEC`, `EXT-ADAPTER`, `EXT-ARM`, `EXT-ITEMS`, `EXT-MERGE`, `EXT-STAGE`, `EXT-BASE`, `EXT-FREEZE`, `EXT-PROFILE`, `EXT-LAUNCH`, `EXT-GPU`, `EXT-SMOKE`, `EXT-CONTAMINATION`, `EXT-NEL`, `EXT-WRITE` (EXT-59), `EXT-DATA` (EXT-65). Fields: `--eval-baseline` / `--eval-candidate` (optionally suffixed), `--stage-out`, `--launch-record[...]`, `--smoke-record[...]`, `artifacts.base.uri`, registration fields as before; `ext-launch` names the request path (`mounts[1].mode`, `env.HF_TOKEN`, …) at the end of the field.

#### Requirements

| ID | Source | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| EXT-1 | MM-1, 05 §3.8 | **Line schema.** `items.jsonl` lines hold `item` (non-empty string), `repeat`, `seed`, `score`, `error`, and optional `sub_metrics`, `output_ref`, `item_meta`. Refused (`EXT-ARM`, exit 3, nothing written): unknown key; both or neither of score and error; a score that is boolean, a string, non-finite or outside the registered range (MM-3); `seed` ≠ `eval_seeds[repeat]`; repeat outside range(K); duplicate `(item, repeat)`; an `error` that is not `{class, message}`; `sub_metrics` not finite; an `output_ref` whose `path` leaves the arm or whose file is absent or differs from its `sha256`; an unparseable line; an empty or absent `items.jsonl` (also the H-6 parent-directory regression). `item_meta` never pairs and is dropped before `gates.evaluate_items`. An item not in the registered set is **not** refused: the gate is MISSING `unknown_item`, run written, exit 1 (05 §3.8 revision 11; maintainers' decision, which reversed the first proposal; MM-6 and MM-10 updated). |
| EXT-2 | MM-9, MM-14 | **Command and layout.** `ext-collect` writes `registration/`, `plan.json`, `eval/<arm>/` (copies of `items.jsonl`, `eval_meta.json`, `merge.json` when present), `ledger/consumption.parquet`, `lineage/` (`checkpoints`, `shards`, `orphaned`, `edges.json`), `manifests/` (as `tierb-collect`), `records/launch.json` and `records/smoke.json` (byte copies) **(choice)**, `lockfile.json`, `report/`. Trainer config `{backend: trl_grpo, profile: smoke|full}`: other keys or another backend `TRAINER-CONFIG`; a profile other than the launch record's `EXT-PROFILE`. |
| EXT-3 | MM-2, MM-3 | **Arm metadata.** `eval_meta.json` keys exactly `schema: gatedtrain.item_eval`, `schema_version`, `evaluator_id` (the spec gate's evaluator), `adapter` (= `--adapter`), `metric`, `direction`, `score_range`, `scorer` (each equal to the registry row), `checkpoint: {model_hash, base_model_hash}`, `normalizer_sha256` and `env_version_sha256` (null for an LLM evaluator), `item_set_hash` (registered), `eval_seeds` (the registered seeds; an unmerged single-seed arm is refused), `sampler` (= registration `sampler`) and `sampler_hash` (its canonical JSON SHA-256, as the report's), `framework: {name, version}`; optional `created_at` (excluded from hashes). Any difference, a missing key or an unknown key is `EXT-ARM`. |
| EXT-4 | MM-4, 05 §3.4 | **Evaluator errors are missing items.** A record with an error of a class the registry declares (`scorer_error`, `missing_score`) is a missing item with that class as reason; above `pairing.max_unpaired` the gate is MISSING (`missing_above_bound`, exit 1, run written), within it the report lists the count; never a score 0 **(choice: MM-4's "refuses" read as the gate's MISSING, not exit 3)**. An undeclared error class is `EXT-ARM`. |
| EXT-5 | MM-6 | **Item set.** `<items>/items.jsonl`, unique non-empty string `item`s plus payload (`question`, `answer` for GSM8K); duplicates or non-strings `EXT-ITEMS`. Its manifest hash must equal `eval_distribution.item_set_hash` (`REG-FIELD`). Items with no usable record are missing (`absent`). The item order of the file is the registered order passed to `evaluate_items`. |
| EXT-6 | R11A-23, R11A-24, 05 §6.1 | **Deciding evaluator, no index 0.** Every check (registry agreement, direction, scorer, run-time hash) uses `registration.deciding_evaluator(reg, gate.evaluator)`. The resolved hash of `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator_items@0.1` is `evaluator_identity_hash` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON `{evaluator, metric, direction, scorer}` **(choice: score range left out, it is fixed by the id)**, and `registration.resolved_evaluator_hash` returns it. A decoy at index 0 never decides: deciding entry with another well-formed hash gives MISSING `evaluator_hash_mismatch`. |
| EXT-7 | MM-13 | **Spec ids.** `rl.trl_grpo@0.1` and `gsm8k.nemo_evaluator_items@0.1` validate (spec and `items.EVALUATORS`); other ids are refused as before. `run` refuses the ext trainer (`SPEC-REF`), `tierb-collect` refuses it (`TIERB-SPEC`), and `ext-collect` refuses `rl.nemo_rl@0.1` (`EXT-SPEC stages[0].uses`) and an unknown `--adapter` (`EXT-ADAPTER`). |
| EXT-8 | MM-7, MM-8 | **Pairing by key.** Records go to `gates.evaluate_items` unchanged (natural units; clause direction orients), keyed by `(item, repeat)`; line order changes nothing. Ledger: one row per (step, shard) for steps 1 … the last complete cut, `precision: iteration`, `derivation: reconstructed`, `data_ref.row` null, `outcome: consumed`, `run_id` the run directory, `attempt_id` "0" (as Tier B). `checkpoint_completeness.sources` cites the cut layout. |

| EXT-51 | MM-34, EXT-40; review round 2 H-1 | **Host paths the daemon resolves differently** (`EXT-LAUNCH`, at `ext-launch` on the request and at `ext-collect` on the record). A mount host path under `/proc`, `/sys` or `/dev` (lexically, after `//` → `/`, or resolved) is refused (`mounts[i].host`): dockerd runs as root without user-namespace remapping and resolves `/proc/1/root` to the host's `/`, while a non-root `os.path.realpath` returns the unreadable link unchanged. Every mount source must resolve on the launch host with `os.path.realpath(strict=True)`; a missing path, a dangling link or an unreadable link is refused (a mount source therefore exists before `ext-launch`; docker would otherwise create it as root). The container's gid is never 0 and never the group of a Docker daemon socket that exists on the host (`/run/docker.sock`, `/var/run/docker.sock`, `/run/docker-*.sock`; for example group `docker`, gid 984, mode 660) (`user`). `launch.docker_socket_gids` reads the groups; `isolation_problems(socket_gids=...)` injects them in tests. |
| EXT-52 | MM-5, EXT-47; review round 2 M-a | **A merged arm is re-derived.** When an arm's `merge.json` checks pass (EXT-47), its `items.jsonl` must equal byte for byte the file the command writes from those sources: `ext_merge_eval`: `arm.merge_records` of the source arms read with the EXT-1 rules; `ext_from_nel`: `from_nel.convert_records` of each run's `results.jsonl` with the registered item set's rows. An edited score, a dropped, added or reordered line is `EXT-ARM`. **Design decision (option a):** a multi-seed arm without `merge.json` is accepted, since MM-1 and MM-5 allow an arm written in one piece with K repeats; source provenance is checked only when `merge.json` exists, and the provenance of a one-piece K-repeat arm is the evaluator's claim. |
| EXT-53 | MM-36, EXT-46; review round 2 M-b | **The last terminal event is `END rc=0`.** Of the label's `END` and `PREEMPTED` lines, the last must be an `END` with `rc=0`: `START, END rc=0, START, PREEMPTED` is refused (`EXT-GPU --launch-record.label`). |
| EXT-54 | EXT-39; review round 2 L-c | **Whole-string grammar.** `runtime.image`, `label` (request and hashed), `user`, `network`, environment names and the `rc=` of a gpu-step `END` are matched with `re.fullmatch` and ASCII `[0-9]`: a trailing newline or a non-ASCII digit (`١٠٠٠`) is refused. |
| EXT-55 | MM-34, EXT-26; maintainers' decision (review round 2) | **Credential names by component.** An environment name (request) or `env_names` entry (record) is refused when a `_`-separated component of its upper-cased name is TOKEN(S), SECRET(S), PASSWORD(S), PASSWD, KEY(S) or CREDENTIAL(S): `HF_TOKEN`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` are refused, `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM`, `KEYBOARD` are allowed. The golden request carries the area1-trl README's `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM=false`. (Supersedes the substring rule of EXT-26.) |
| EXT-56 | MM-34, EXT-26, EXT-40; review round 2 L-d | **Mount rules on resolved paths.** A leading `//` is read as `/` (POSIX `normpath` keeps it); the MM-34 rules (no `host_work`, no parent of the output, the read-write output) compare the lexical paths and the resolved ones, so `//<host_work>`, `//<parent of output>` and a symbolic link to either are refused (`mounts[i].host`). |
| EXT-57 | MM-36, EXT-46; review round 2 L-b | **One `WORK` and one `LOG` assignment in gpu-step.sh.** `ext-collect` refuses (`EXT-GPU --launch-record.host_work`) a script with more than one assignment to `WORK` or `LOG` anywhere in a line (`X=`, `export X=`, `a; X=`, `${X:=...}`); uses (`$LOG`, `"$WORK/..."`) are not assignments. The rule guards against a misconfigured host_work only: ext-collect reads the script and never runs it, so a script forged under host_work (or an assignment through `read`, `eval`, ...) is not detected. |
| EXT-58 | MM-30, 05 §11.2 (R14-1); review round 2 L-e | **Several paths, finest precision.** An impact result reached by several paths (its stage's own data edge, and a `used` model edge from each returned parent model) lists every path in `paths` (`via_model_hash` null for the direct path, `precision`, `derivation`, `first_step`) and takes the finest of their precisions; `first_step`, `derivation`, `via_model_hash` and `answer` are those of the finest path (the direct one on a tie). A row-level `consumed` answer is no longer weakened to `possibly consumed` by a `stage` path. A transitive path's precision is the parent checkpoint's answer precision. |
| EXT-59 | MM-10, EXT-41; tests/README.md §2; review round 2 L-f | **A failed write is a refusal.** An exception while `ext-collect`, `ext-merge-eval` or `ext-from-nel` writes (after every check passed) removes the partial output and exits 3 with `error[EXT-WRITE] --runs-root` (collect) or `--out`, naming the exception class and message; never exit 1 (the gate's FAIL) and never a traceback. |
| EXT-60 | EXT-39; review round 2 mutant X2 | **`build_command` re-checks values.** Called directly, it raises ValueError for a label, network, user, environment entry, mount, tmpfs path or image that is empty or starts with `-`. |
| EXT-61 | EXT-46; review round 2 L-a | **The label is a checksum.** The hashed label is unkeyed: it detects a record changed by accident after `ext-launch`, not a record rewritten with a recomputed label. The refusal text says so. |

A mount source that is a proper ancestor of `host_work`, lexically (after `//` → `/`) or resolved, is refused (ancestors of the output and of `/run`, `/var/run` were already refused by EXT-26 and EXT-40), and so is `/var/lib/docker`, `/var/lib/containerd` or anything under them. The golden request and the area1-trl README's read-only inputs (`<mm-root>/python`, `.../area1-trl`, `<tierb-root>/tierb/{base-model,export}` with host_work `<HOST_WORK>`) stay accepted. |

Refused before: `<host_work>/bin`, `<host_work>/gpu-guard.log` (a file), the skeleton's code, `~/.ssh`, `/tmp`, a link to `<host_work>/bin`, the output root itself, a directory holding an earlier cut. |

Every file of every complete cut, and `run_args.json`, `env-freeze.txt`, `samples.jsonl` when present, has a modification time (not following links) at or after the label's first `START` in `gpu-guard.log`; an older file is an earlier run's (the review's `true`-argv dry run collected the stale cuts as a PASS). **(choice: the first START, so a preempted attempt's files count; env-freeze.txt and samples.jsonl added to the review's list because ext-collect reads them.)** |
| EXT-65 | MM-21, MM-23, MM-11; review round 3 H2 | **The training file is the spec's export** (`EXT-DATA artifacts.<data>.uri`). `ext-collect` (TRL adapter): `run_args.json` must exist and carry `train_file_sha256`, equal to the SHA-256 of the spec's training shards concatenated in file-name order; `train_file` is `<container path>/train.jsonl` of a read-only mount whose host path resolves to the spec's export directory, the parent of the shard directory (`<export>/train/`, the export-gsm8k layout that holds `<export>/train.jsonl`). A mismatch was a `stage` PASS gated against contamination of other data. `ext-launch --spec SPEC` and `--recheck ... --spec SPEC` require the base mount (MM-21) and the export mount (`EXT-BASE`, `EXT-DATA`). **(choice: `--spec` is optional at ext-launch, which takes no spec otherwise; the binding is always checked at ext-collect.)** **Changed by EXT-84:** `--spec` is required when the adapter binds the spec's mounts (TRL does). |
| EXT-66 | MM-34; review round 3 M1 | **No credential store, the uid's own group, inputs from the data tree** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...user`, `...mounts[i].host`). The container's gid is the primary group of its uid in the host's user database (an unknown uid is refused): supplementary groups such as adm (4), shadow (42), docker (984) are refused. A mount source (lexical and resolved) that is, contains or lies under `/etc`, `/root` or `/var/log`; that is `/home` or contains it; that is or contains a home directory (`/root`, every directory under `/home`, every user-database home under `/home`); or that is or lies under a dot-directory directly in a home directory (`~/.ssh`, `~/.docker`, `~/.cache`, `~/.config`) is refused. A read-only source must lie under an input root (`GATEDTRAIN_EXT_INPUT_ROOTS`, lexical and resolved; **changed by EXT-79:** the default is the adapter's registry `input_roots`, no longer `<data-root>`). **(choice: dot-directories at a home's top level only, so the area-1 venv `.../area1-trl/.venv` inside a mounted input stays allowed.)** |
| EXT-67 | MM-31, MM-36; review round 3 M2 | **The configured host_work, its wrapper and its log.** `host_work` equals the configured one (no built-in host path: read from the host file `~/.config/gatedtrain/host.toml` or the file `GATEDTRAIN_HOST_CONFIG` names, `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_HOST_WORK`; `EXT-LAUNCH ...host_work`); `--gpu-log` is `<host_work>/gpu-log.md` (`EXT-GPU --gpu-log`); `ext-launch` runs the EXT-46/EXT-57 script check (`EXT-GPU --request.host_work`) and records `gpu_step_sha256`, the SHA-256 of `<host_work>/bin/gpu-step.sh`, which `ext-collect` and `--recheck` compare with the script now (`EXT-GPU ...gpu_step_sha256`); `gpu_est_h > 0` (and a full run's `projected_gpu_h > 0`, `EXT-SMOKE`). |
| EXT-68 | MM-29, MM-20; review round 3 M3 | **run_args.json agrees** (TRL adapter, `ext-collect`). `profile` equals the launch record's (`EXT-PROFILE --launch-record.profile`); `dry_run` is `false` (`EXT-STAGE --stage-out`); `grpo_config.max_steps` is an integer equal to the last complete cut's step (`EXT-STAGE --stage-out`). |
| EXT-69 | MM-31, MM-34; review round 3 M4 | **Resolved sources and a recheck (TOCTOU).** Every `-v` source of the printed command is the strict `realpath` of the record's host path (`build_command` raises ValueError for one that does not resolve or resolves to a path with `:`, `,` or a control character; the request check refuses it first). `gatedtrain ext-launch --adapter NAME --recheck RECORD [--spec SPEC]` re-runs every record rule against the filesystem now (mount sources resolved again, the output directory still empty, output root, wrapper checksum and log, label checksum) and exits 3 on any problem (fields `--recheck.*`); the operator runs it right before the printed command. The remaining window between the recheck and docker's mount is documented (MM-36). |

| EXT-72 | MM-34; review round 3 L2 | **Reserved names.** `NVIDIA_*`, `DOCKER_*`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (case-insensitive) are refused in the request's env and the record's `env_names`; `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` is allowed. |
| EXT-73 | MM-34, EXT-62; review round 3 L3 | **Every engine data directory and its ancestors.** The engine data directories are `/var/lib/docker`, `/var/lib/containerd` and the `data-root` of `/etc/docker-gatedtrain/daemon.json` (a second daemon run for gatedtrain, where a host has one) and `/etc/docker/daemon.json` when readable (only that key is read; the file is never printed); a mount source that is, lies under or contains one (lexical or resolved) is refused: `/var/lib`, or `<data-root>/gatedtrain-docker` when a second daemon keeps its data there. |
| EXT-74 | MM-34; review round 3 L4 | **32-bit ids, distinct container paths.** A uid or gid above 2^31-1 is refused (`...user`); two mounts, or a mount and a tmpfs, at the same normalised container path are refused (`...mounts[i].container`). |
| EXT-75 | MM-10, EXT-59; review round 3 L5 | **Every write failure is a refusal, only write failures.** An OSError while creating the run directory (`ext-collect`, `--runs-root`), the output's parent or temporary directory (`ext-merge-eval`, `ext-from-nel`, `--out`) or the launch record (`ext-launch`, `--out-record`) exits 3 with `EXT-WRITE`, no traceback, nothing left behind. Only OSError is caught: another exception during the writes is a bug and propagates (traceback, exit 1), the partial run directory still removed. |
| EXT-76 | MM-5, EXT-10; review round 3 L6 (mutant M15) | **Merged order.** `merge_records` orders records by the first source's item order, then by repeat in the registered seed order; a golden list written by hand (two sources in different item orders) pins it. |
| EXT-77 | MM-30, 05 §11.2; review round 3 L7 | **The chain of every path.** Each impact path carries `chain`: the consuming stage's data edge `{edge: data, run_id, stage_id, precision, derivation, first_step}`, then one `{edge: model, model_hash, checkpoint_hash, run_id, stage_id, precision: null}` per `used` model edge to the cut (a model edge is an identity by hash, not a consumption edge, so it has no precision). The path's `precision` is the coarsest on its chain (EXT-58 unchanged). |
| EXT-78 | MM-23, MM-27; review round 3 L8 | **samples.jsonl cross-check.** When `<stage-out>/samples.jsonl` exists, the rows it logs for each step 1 .. the last complete cut (its `step` is the consuming optimizer step, `global_step + 1` at generation: trl grpo_trainer.py:1538-1546, :1584-1588; train_grpo.py `GSM8KReward._log`) must equal the reconstructed rows; a missing step, an extra or moved row, or a line without integer `step` and `row` degrades consumption to `stage` with the reason. Lines after the last cut are not compared; an absent file changes nothing. |
| EXT-79 | MM-34, EXT-66; review round 4 H1 | **Per-adapter input roots** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...mounts[i].host`). The default input roots are the adapter's registry property `input_roots` (`registry.input_roots`, `launch.adapter_config`); for `trl_grpo` exactly `<mm-root>/python`, `<mm-root>/area1-trl`, `<tierb-root>/tierb/base-model`, `<tierb-root>/tierb/export` (area1-trl README "Mounts"). `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_INPUT_ROOTS` still overrides them; with neither (no adapter), no read-only mount is accepted. ext-launch, `--recheck` and ext-collect apply them. The review's probe sources on the data disk (other projects holding `.env` and `*_credentials.json`, `<tierb-root>/hf-home-host`, a second daemon's directory under `<tierb-root>/`, `<tierb-root>/bin`) are refused; the four real area-1 sources pass. |
| EXT-80 | MM-34; review round 4 H1 | **Credential file names** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...mounts[i].host`, ext-launch, `--recheck`, ext-collect). Each read-only source that passed every other mount rule is walked (resolved path, links not followed) and every entry name, and the source's own name, is matched lower-cased against `.env`, `.env.*`, `*credential*`, `*secret*`, `*.pem`, `*.key`, `id_rsa*`, `id_ed25519*`, `token`, `.netrc`, `.git-credentials`; a match, a directory that cannot be listed, or more than 200 000 entries (`launch.SCAN_LIMIT`) refuses the mount. Names only: no file is opened, contents never printed. `.venv` and `site-packages` are not descended into. **(choice: the standard library's `secrets` module, `lib/python3.<N>/secrets.py` and its `__pycache__/secrets.cpython-<N>[.opt-k].pyc`, is exempt, because the real area-1 interpreter `<mm-root>/python` holds it; an application's `secrets.py` elsewhere is refused.)** |
| EXT-81 | MM-34; review round 4 M1 | **Network** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...network`). `host` is refused always (with any reasons, with or without the flag). Another named network (`bridge`, a user network) needs `ext-launch --allow-network` and a `network_reason`; the record keeps `allow_network` (true only when the flag was given for a network other than `none`) beside `network_reason`; ext-collect and `--recheck` refuse a record whose network is not `none` without `allow_network: true`, and an `allow_network` that is not a boolean (`...allow_network`). |
| EXT-82 | MM-31, MM-36; review round 4 M2 | **Recheck the running total** (`EXT-GPU --recheck.gpu_used_h`). `--recheck` re-reads `<host_work>/gpu-log.md`; a record `gpu_used_h` other than its last running total now (GPU time logged after ext-launch, so the printed `GATEDTRAIN_GPU_USED_H` is stale) or an unreadable log exits 3. |
| EXT-83 | MM-34, MM-35; review round 4 M3 | **The training script is bound** (`EXT-LAUNCH`). ext-launch records `script_path`, the first argv element after the interpreter (`argv[0]`) that is an absolute container path in a read-only mount (longest container prefix) naming a regular file that resolves (strict) inside the mount's resolved source, and `script_sha256`, that file's SHA-256; argv without one is refused (`--request.argv`). ext-collect and `--recheck` recompute it from the record's argv and mounts: another or no script refuses (`...script_path`), another SHA-256 now refuses (`...script_sha256`); ext-collect (TRL adapter) also requires `<stage-out>/env-runtime.json` `script_sha256` (train_grpo.py `write_env_freeze`: `sha256_file(Path(__file__).resolve())`) to equal the record's (`--launch-record.script_sha256`), and `env-runtime.json` joins the EXT-64 stale-output list. |
| EXT-84 | MM-21, EXT-65; review round 4 L1 | **`--spec` required** (`EXT-SPEC --spec`, exit 3) for ext-launch and `--recheck` when the adapter declares the spec binding (registry `spec_binding`, adapter `binds_spec`; TRL does). |
| EXT-85 | MM-34, MM-35; review round 4 L2 | **The host layout is bound.** The record keeps `host_config_sha256`, the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON `{host_work, output_root, input_roots}` of the effective layout (`launch.host_config_sha256`); ext-collect and `--recheck` compare it with their own (`EXT-LAUNCH ...host_config_sha256`). `ext-launch` and `ext-collect` print one `warning: <VAR> overrides the host layout (...)` line on stderr per set `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_*` variable (not an error line). |

Rows holding them parse; line numbers in messages are the file's; row counts agree with `ledger.shard_table` (file iteration) and the TRL step rows. Readers: item set, contamination (`exact_overlap`), arm `items.jsonl`, `samples.jsonl`, `results_lines`, `item_ids` and `write_plan`, `tierb._read_jsonl`, and the text files `gpu-guard.log`, `gpu-step.sh`, `gpu-log.md`, `env-freeze.txt` and the NeMo Evaluator snapshot header. **(choice: `gates.load_paired_records` keeps `splitlines`, a copy of the pinned upstream reader (comparison.py:364 at ea35ba3); NeMo Evaluator writes `results.jsonl` with `json.dumps` defaults, ASCII; `stubs/task.read_rows` keeps it too: its rows are ASCII digit sums and its file is hashed into the stand-in evaluator's identity, which the demo registrations pin (`runner.stub_evaluator_hash`).)** |

The record adds `interpreter_path` (the resolved container path; for area 1 `.../cpython-3.12.16-linux-x86_64-gnu/bin/python3.12`, through the venv's `bin/python` link) and `interpreter_sha256` (SHA-256 of that host file); `script_path` stays argv[1] as written. `--recheck` and ext-collect re-bind the argv and compare both paths and hashes (`...interpreter_path`, `...interpreter_sha256`, `...script_path`, `...script_sha256`). |

The four real area-1 sources pass (0.02 s in all on the host). |
| EXT-94 | MM-34, EXT-88; review round 5 L3, mutant Q4 | **No nested mounts** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...mounts[i].container` or `...tmpfs`, request and record). No mount (ro or rw) and no tmpfs lies strictly inside another mount's or tmpfs's container path, in either direction: the rw output inside a ro input (`<area1-trl>/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages`), a ro input on another's venv, a ro file on the script's container path, a ro parent of other mounts (`/inputs`), a tmpfs inside an input, an input inside the `/tmp` tmpfs. Equal paths stay EXT-74. With nesting refused, `container_file` requires exactly one mount to contain a path (no longest-prefix choice), so a file mounted on the script's path never binds. |
| EXT-95 | MM-35; review round 5 M2 | **Schema version** (`EXT-LAUNCH ...schema_version`). The launch record's `schema_version` is `2.0`. ext-collect and `--recheck` refuse a record of any other version with one problem naming the version and saying the record predates the current rules; nothing is upgraded and, at `--recheck`, nothing else is judged. All existing area-1 records (`records/area1-trl/smoke-1`, `smoke-2`, `full-1`, schema 1.0) are therefore uncollectable (area 1 is already collected). |
| EXT-96 | MM-36, EXT-82; review round 5 mutant Q5 | **Exact running total at `--recheck`.** A record `gpu_used_h` 0.01 h above or below `gpu-log.md`'s running total refuses (`EXT-GPU --recheck.gpu_used_h`); the equal record passes. |
| EXT-97 | MM-34, EXT-81; review round 5 | **`network_host_reason` retired.** Network `host` is always refused (EXT-81), so the field is dead: it is no request key and no record key (a record carrying it has an unknown key). A request may still carry it as null (requests written before round 5, e.g. `records/area1-trl/full-1.request.yaml`); a value refuses (`EXT-LAUNCH --request.network_host_reason`). |

Since the move, `test_ext_trl.py`, the real-host probes of review rounds 4 to 6 (the area-1 request, records, inputs, README and launcher) and the real-export row test now live in `gatedtrain-recipe-trl-grpo-gsm8k`'s `tests/` (`tests/test_adapter.py`, `test_registry.py`, `test_collect.py`, `test_area1_host.py`); gatedtrain keeps the rest on the synthetic adapter, with `test_ext_review3.TestSamplesCrossCheck` (EXT-78) on its own `SyntheticCase`.
