**Fixtures.** `fixtures/ext/agentic/p1-a3/`: a cut of the real probe by `_nemo_rl_agentic.cut_probe` (two prompt groups × two members per step covering ok, error and syntax_error tool calls, final with reward 0 and 1, no_answer and the environment's max_turns; calls, positions and idx renumbered; step-log token arrays cut to `input_lengths`, float arrays dropped; profile_settings 2 × 2; the six rows; the cut's 70 MB adapter and the tokenizer, vocabulary and merges files replaced by stand-ins, cut.json re-hashed; provenance and source hashes in SOURCE.json; a test re-cuts the real probe and compares byte for byte). `fixtures/ext/agentic/sliding-puzzle/`: NeMo RL's real SlidingPuzzleEnv through the area's `traj_log.LoggedSlidingPuzzleEnv` and rollout double (`make_sliding.py.txt`, run with the area-4 venv `-I -B`; SOURCE.json pins the code). `fixtures/ext/agentic/runtime/`: byte copies of `agentic_core.py`, `gen_agentic.py`, `exec_protocol.py` and the deployment prompt (never executed). `fixtures/ext/agentic/real-arm/`: the proposed scorer's real run on 20 real items (gen records written by `_nemo_rl_agentic.gen_doc`, one sandbox_failure, one missing), the 20 items, and the runtime scorer's eval_meta.json on the same input.

**Design choices.** (1) The scorer pin is `score_agentic.py` without the `env_id` key in eval_meta.json (MM-2 refuses unknown keys; one-line area change, proposed). (2) The consumption record carries no token counts (05 §12.2 has no field): the totals go into the reason and the report; per-trajectory counts stay in the logs. (3) `--max-steps` 60 .. 100 under the full profile (decision "60-100 steps"); the probe profile is never launched through ext-launch. (4) All three scripts are pinned to the area's image `7a4078e7...` (uid 1000 writes NeMo RL's vLLM patch locks), not the Tier B image. (5) `agentic_core.py`, `gen_agentic.py` and `exec_protocol.py` are pinned in the adapter (training and evaluation must decide turns the same way); a change to them needs a re-pin. (6) The consumption join takes prompts and generations from run_args.json (the fixture cut has 2 × 2); the run checks require the launcher's 16 × 8. (7) Rows follow NeMo RL's drop_last (the area's consumption.py wraps modulo the file; equal for 2,000 rows).

**State before implementation (2026-11-15).** The adapter came first, checked by hand against the real probe (0 problems; 384 trajectories equal to the area's join), then the fixtures and tests; 76 tests, 67 passed at the first run, nine fixed (seven test mutations that did not change what they meant to, one fixture relabel, the docs rows written after).

**Runtime changes (proposed to the area, not made here).** `score_agentic.py`: drop `"env_id": "math_code",` from the `eval_meta.json` it writes (one line; the result is `a750f5be...`, which the registry pins; `gen_meta.json` and the evaluator id still name the environment). Until then no arm of the area's scorer can be filled or collected (MM-2 refuses the unknown key), which `TestRuntimeFacts.test_runtime_scorer_arm_is_refused` shows on a real run of today's file.

**Open points.** (1) Token counts per trajectory have no field in the consumption record (05 §12.2); a maintainers' choice between keeping them in the run's logs (now), an optional record field (a design revision), or a side table in the run directory. (2) The area launcher hard-codes `ENV_ID = "math_code"`; another real environment needs its registration in `run_agentic_nemo.py` (and an `--env` option the adapter would then bind) plus its own evaluator row.

**Mutants** (a mutation script, in-place on a scratch copy, each restored, against `test_ext_nemo_rl_agentic.py`): 20, all killed: `consumption_problems` empty (27 tests fail), the environment's end-reason rule (2) and terminal-reward rule (2), the continuation order (1), `trajectories.jsonl` (1), the float32 reward join (1), the gate's environment binding (2), `lora_config_problem` (6), the cut kind (1), the pinned turn logic (1), `sampler_problems` (5), the record re-derivation (12), the `gen_meta.json` checks (14), the required sidecar (2), `argv_problems` (15), the normalized overlap (1), the LoRA composition (5), the image sidecar's script re-hash (1) and runtime kind (1) in `launch.py`, and collect's `wants_evaluator` hook (1).

### 11.29 Rename to ptc-hub 0.2.0 (2026-11-17)

Version 0.2.0 renamed every persisted identifier of the former `ptc-skel` 0.1.0: schema ids (`ptc_skel.*` to `ptc_hub.*`: `launch_record`, `smoke_record`, `item_eval`, `ext_merge_eval`, `ext_from_nel`, `lockfile`, ...), the ledger `writer_id` (`ptc-hub.ledger@0.2.0`) and `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION` (`ptc-hub 0.2.0`), together with the CLI name, message prefixes and the `PTC_SKEL_*` environment variables (to `PTC_HUB_*`). Version 0.3.0 renamed them again, to `gatedtrain` (section 11.31); the rules below hold for both renames, and module, constant and test names below are today's. Only the new names are read; `gatedtrain.renamed` recognises an old name only to say so in the refusal. `tests/test_rename.py` (23 tests): a registration recording the old analysis code version is refused (REG-FIELD) at `validate` and `ext-collect` with a fix naming the amendment (05 section 3.7: a new registration with `amends`, `created_at`, `post_hoc`). Design revision 17 (R17-1): `registration.check_amendment` finds the prior registration next to the amended one by its hash; when the prior records a pre-rename version (`renamed.PRE_RENAME_VERSIONS`) and the amendment this code's, and nothing but `analysis_code_version`, `amends`, `created_at` and `post_hoc` differs, it is a version-only amendment: not post hoc, it can PASS, and the report says `registration_amendment: version_only` (JSON field, null otherwise; the text line only then). The same amendment with any other field changed and `post_hoc` false or absent is refused (REG-FIELD `registration.post_hoc`); with `post_hoc: true` it collects and cannot PASS; a version-only amendment declaring `post_hoc: true` still cannot PASS; without the prior file next to it nothing is compared and the amendment keeps its declared `post_hoc` (not marked version-only); an amendment still naming the old version, or without `created_at`, is refused. R17-3 (`TestScorerRenameProof`, in process on a temporary evaluator row): `evaluators[*].manifest_hash` may differ in a version-only amendment when the registration's `rename_proof` entry `{evaluator, code_sha256_before, files, composite}` holds (`registration.scorer_rename_problems`): the row's identity with `code_sha256_before` is the prior hash and with its current `code_sha256` the amended one, the pre-rename files (paths relative to the registration's directory) hash to `code_sha256_before` (a file's SHA-256, or for `composite: true` the SHA-256 of the compact sorted JSON {file name: SHA-256}), and the files with `renamed.NAME_MAP` applied give the current `code_sha256`; a single file and a composite pass; a scorer with any other byte change, a missing pre-rename file, a file that is not the registered scorer, another changed evaluator field or a wrong `code_sha256_before` are refused for `post_hoc: false` (accepted, not version-only, with `post_hoc: true`); a launch record, smoke record or arm metadata with an old schema id is refused with a message saying it was written before the rename. The real long run in `fixtures/r11/longrun` keeps its old names byte for byte (it is a copy of real outputs, read by normalization and per-item re-evaluation only), and so does the `gatedtrain.renamed` constant naming the old prefix. The stand-in evaluator's identity hash includes the package name and version (from 0.4.0 the analysis version, section 11.36), so the demo registrations carry the new resolved hash and the demo specs the new registration hashes.

### 11.30 Several blocking clauses, cluster bootstrap, percentile bounds (design revision 18, 2026-11-17)

Design revision 18 (design.md "Revision 18 changes", R18-1 to R18-7; 05 sections 3.1, 3.4 to 3.7; 04 section 4.2), for the TTS recipe's pre-registered gate. A registration may list several blocking clauses in `clauses` (each with the 05 section 3.1 fields plus a unique `name` and its deciding `evaluator`, one evaluator per clause) with `multiplicity: {plan: all_clauses_must_pass}` and `sigma_pilot` (and an optional `baseline_pilot_mean`) keyed by clause name; any clause may set `cluster_key` (an item-set field: clusters of complete pairs resampled together, in sorted value order, leave-one-cluster-out jackknife for BCa, observed design effect for power) and `bootstrap_variant: bca | percentile`. The spec's gate lists one evaluator per clause in clause order; `uses` names the first clause's type. `ext-collect` takes `--eval-baseline`/`--eval-candidate` once per clause, refuses an item set whose items lack a clause's cluster field (`EXT-ITEMS`, `registration.clauses[i].cluster_key`), copies arms to `eval/<arm>/<clause name>/`, and writes per-clause plans, lockfile entries and report entries with the combined status (`gates.combine(..., "and")`: FAIL, then MISSING, then INCONCLUSIVE, else PASS). `run` and `tierb-collect` refuse a registration with `clauses`. A registration with one `clause` and none of the new fields validates, gates and reports exactly as before (the new result keys `resampling_unit`, `cluster_key`, `n_clusters` appear only with `cluster_key`).

`tests/test_r18_clauses.py` (65 tests; helper `tests/_r18.py`, a second synthetic evaluator `synthetic.length@0.1`, registered only inside `_r18.patched()` or in a `python -m _r18` subprocess): the TTS-shaped two-clause registration validates through `check_contents`, and every new field and refusal (`TestTtsRegistration`, `TestClausesRefused`, `TestClusterAndVariantFields`, `TestSpecGate`); `TestGoldenAnalyzeGate` reproduces area 11's `analyze_gate.py` (SHA-256 7b815399...) on synthetic TTS-shaped data, three scenarios: the rounded mean differences and one-sided percentile bounds equal the script's output, and the unrounded bounds equal its replicates' quantiles to 1e-12 (same generator, seed, draw shape and sorted cluster order); `TestClusterBootstrap` (singleton clusters equal the item bootstrap, percentile equals plain quantiles, orientation, cluster order, one cluster is zero width, complete pairs only, ratio of sums); `CollectTwoClauses` (ext-collect end to end: PASS, one clause FAIL, FAIL before MISSING, arm count, arm order, missing cluster field, a smoke record without or with a degenerate second evaluator, ext-launch's per-clause smoke evaluators); `TestSmokeBound` (R18-8, option B: the TTS-shaped 14 zeros and 6 distinct positive WERs PASS, OCR-shaped half zeros on a higher-is-better [0, 1] metric FAIL `degenerate_mode`, all scores at the best bound FAIL `degenerate_bound`, only the best end is exempt, the share stays a share of the whole sample (drones shape), no direction exempts nothing, binary unchanged, per-evaluator problems); `TestRunRefusesClauses`. R18-8 (05 section 6.1, MULTIMODAL-SPEC MM-38; maintainers' decision B): a smoke score exactly on the finite bound at the metric's best end cannot be the value that makes up 50% of the sample (the share stays one of the whole sample; the worst end counts as before); at least a quarter of the scores (and 2) must lie off that bound (`degenerate_bound`); with several clauses the smoke record carries `baseline_scores_by_evaluator` and ext-collect (and ext-launch with `--spec`) check every clause's evaluator. Every existing smoke record (25 files under the data root and the recipes) keeps its verdict under its evaluator's row. The TTS registration's speaker-guard planning comment (n = 63) differs from 05 section 3.6's non-inferiority plan (n = 71 at d = theta + delta = 0.01); the test pins 71.

### 11.31 Rename to gatedtrain 0.3.0 (2026-11-18)

Version 0.3.0 renames the framework from `ptc-hub` to `gatedtrain` before its first publication, by the rules of section 11.29: distribution and import package `gatedtrain` (`src/gatedtrain/`), console script `gatedtrain` and `python -m gatedtrain` (argparse `prog`, message prefix `[gatedtrain]`), adapter entry point group `gatedtrain.adapters`, schema ids `gatedtrain.<x>`, ledger `writer_id` `gatedtrain.ledger@0.3.0`, `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION` `gatedtrain 0.3.0`, and every former `PTC_HUB_<X>` and `PTC_<X>` environment variable as `GATEDTRAIN_<X>` (among them `GATEDTRAIN_HOST_CONFIG`, the `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_*` overrides, and `GATEDTRAIN_GPU_CAP_H`, `GATEDTRAIN_GPU_USED_H`, `GATEDTRAIN_GPU_STEP_EST_H` on the printed gpu-step.sh command, so the operator's wrapper reads the new names). The host file is `~/.config/gatedtrain/host.toml`, the Docker context `gatedtrain`, the second daemon `/etc/docker-gatedtrain/daemon.json` and `/run/docker-gatedtrain.sock`. Rule ids and error codes do not change.

**Old names.** `renamed.OLD_PACKAGES` is (`ptc-skel`, `ptc-hub`); `renamed.OLD_SCHEMA_PREFIXES` maps `ptc_skel.` and `ptc_hub.` to the package that wrote them; `renamed.PRE_RENAME_VERSIONS` is (`ptc-skel 0.1.0`, `ptc-hub 0.2.0`). Exactly as for `ptc-skel` in section 11.29, and now for both: a registration recording either version is refused (REG-FIELD) with the amendment fix; an amendment from either to `gatedtrain 0.3.0` that changes nothing else is version-only (R17-1); a record with either schema prefix is refused, and the note names the package that wrote it. `renamed.NAME_MAP` (R17-3) appends the 0.3.0 pairs to the 0.2.0 pairs, in order `ptc-hub 0.2.0` -> `gatedtrain 0.3.0`, `ptc-hub.ledger@0.2.0` -> `gatedtrain.ledger@0.3.0`, `PTC_HUB_` -> `GATEDTRAIN_`, `PTC_` -> `GATEDTRAIN_`, `ptc_recipe_` -> `gatedtrain_recipe_`, `ptc-recipe-` -> `gatedtrain-recipe-`, `ptc_hub` -> `gatedtrain`, `ptc-hub` -> `gatedtrain`, so a scorer of either earlier version maps to this one; no other name is mapped.

**Host daemons.** The built-in daemon configurations (`launch.DAEMON_CONFIGS`) and sockets (`launch.SOCKET_PATHS`) are the standard ones and the `gatedtrain` ones. A host whose daemons live elsewhere lists them in the host file: `extra_daemon_configs` and `extra_daemon_sockets`, each an optional TOML array of absolute paths (`launch.HOST_FILE_LIST_KEYS`), read by `launch.host_file` (also under the `GATEDTRAIN_EXT_*` overrides, since they only add refusals; any other type is refused, EXT-HOST). Each listed configuration's `data-root` is an engine data directory (EXT-62, EXT-73); each listed socket is never mounted and its group is never a container's (EXT-40, EXT-51). The lists enter `host_config_sha256` only when non-empty, so every existing record's hash is unchanged.

**Post hoc is kept.** Design revision 19 (R19-1): an amendment across the rename keeps a prior `post_hoc: true`; `registration.check_amendment` refuses one that declares `post_hoc` false or leaves it out (REG-FIELD `registration.post_hoc`), so a version-only amendment cannot turn an INCONCLUSIVE gate into a PASS (`test_post_hoc_prior_cannot_be_cleared`); one that keeps `post_hoc: true` is version-only and cannot PASS (`test_post_hoc_prior_kept_is_version_only`).

**Tests.** `tests/test_rename.py` (47 tests): section 11.29's cases for both earlier versions (`TestOldAnalysisCodeVersion` with `ptc-hub 0.2.0`, `TestOldestAnalysisCodeVersion` with `ptc-skel 0.1.0`; `TestScorerRenameProof` and `TestOldestScorerRenameProof` likewise), the published map (`TestNewNames.test_name_map_is_the_published_map`) and old schema ids of both prefixes (subtests). `test_ext_review3.TestHostFileDaemonLists` (4): lists absent (hash as before), valid lists (engine data directory and socket refused, hash includes them, equal to `testing.ext.host_config_sha256`), a listed configuration's engine data refused in a request, and a string, a relative path, a number or a table refused (EXT-HOST). `test_ext_review3.TestEngineData.test_host_daemon` reads the built-in second-daemon configuration or one listed in the operator's default host file, and skips naming both when none is readable. Fixture and demo registrations record `gatedtrain 0.3.0`; the demo registrations carry the new stand-in evaluator hash (`runner.stub_evaluator_hash` includes the package name and version; from 0.4.0 the analysis version, section 11.36) and the demo specs the new registration hashes. Test support: `GATEDTRAIN_DESIGN_DIR`, `GATEDTRAIN_NEMO_EVALUATOR_DIR`, `GATEDTRAIN_NEMO_RL_DIR`, `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_TIERB_ROOT`, `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_MM_ROOT` (section 1; they replace one data root with fixed subdirectory names), `GATEDTRAIN_SKIP_SLOW`, `GATEDTRAIN_KEEP_TMP`; the CLI subprocesses run with the interpreter that runs the suite (`_support.VENV_PYTHON` is `sys.executable`), so a suite run from any virtual environment tests one installation. Suite: 1,304 tests; skips name their reason (no real Tier B root, no copy of a pinned package in the uv cache, no readable second-daemon configuration).

**What keeps old names.** The real long run in `fixtures/r11/longrun` (byte copies of real outputs: `ptc-skel 0.1.0`, `ptc_skel.*`, host paths under `/srv/ptc`), `renamed.py` and `test_rename.py` (the old-name readers), and section 11.29 and this section, which record the renames. Files of a host that ran the recipes are cited as `<tierb-root>/...` and `<mm-root>/...`, schema ids written by a recipe runtime as `<runtime>.<name>`, and a runtime's hooks module by its role.

### 11.32 Python 3.12: lenient path resolution, and CI (2026-11-19)

The first CI dry run on Python 3.12 failed 8 subtests of `test_ext_review2` (`TestHostPathsLaunch`,
`TestHostPathsCollect`): `os.path.realpath` and `Path.resolve()` without `strict` raise `PermissionError` on 3.12
for a symbolic link the user may not read (`/proc/1/root`), so `ext-launch` and `ext-collect` exited 1 instead of
refusing (exit 3). `gatedtrain.paths` (Python 3.13's lenient `realpath`, ported) now resolves every path from a
request, a launch record or a run output (`docs/findings.md`). **Tests.** `tests/test_paths.py` (2): `realpath` and
`resolve` equal the standard library's on 2,000 random paths over a tree of link chains, a loop, a dangling link,
absolute links and `..` (on 3.12 the paths through the loop are left out, where 3.12 stops resolving and 3.13 goes
on); `/proc/1/root`, `/proc/1/root/run`, `//proc/1/root`, `/proc/1/cwd` and a link to `/proc/1/root` are kept as they
are, without raising (skipped when `/proc/1/root` is readable, as for root). `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs the
suite on 3.12 and 3.13 for every push and pull request. Suite: 1,310 tests.

### 11.34 Review of the 3.12 path fix (2026-11-19)

Findings S1 to S6, C1 and T1 to T4 of the review of section 11.32 (`docs/findings.md`, "Review of the 3.12 path
fix"). MM-1 (maintainers' decision 2026-11-19): an `output_ref` path contains no symbolic link (hard links are
allowed); it is opened component by component from the arm without following links (`arm.open_output_ref`), and
`ext-collect` and `ext-merge-eval` copy the bytes they hash (`arm.copy_output_ref`; a file changed or swapped after the
check is refused, EXT-ARM). **Tests.** `tests/test_path_review.py` (18): plain files and hard links accepted; a link
to a file or directory inside or outside the arm, a dangling link and a loop refused (`contains a symbolic link`); the
review's S2 chain (`a`, `a/b` relative 2.5 KB chains, `b/leak` to a host file, which the kernel opens) and S1 (a
relative arm, a path past `PATH_MAX` ending in a link) refused, a regular file past `PATH_MAX` accepted; S5 (absent
past `PATH_MAX`) absent; below a regular file absent; a directory and a FIFO not a regular file (without blocking); a
300-byte component `ENAMETOOLONG`; an unreadable file `EACCES` (skipped as root); `read_meta`, `read_lines` and
`provenance_problems` under an arm path past `PATH_MAX` give `cannot be read (ENAMETOOLONG)`; `copy_output_ref` copies,
and refuses a file swapped for a link or changed (nothing left at the destination); `ext-merge-eval` in process with
the swap between check and copy refused, no output; S3: a spec `file:` URI through a link loop and a spec path that is
a loop refused (on 3.12 `Path.resolve()` raised `RuntimeError`); T4: a loop and `/proc/1/root/x` as the launch record's
`output_dir` (mismatch with `--stage-out`), in `sdk.bound_mounts` (no binding) and in the diffusers family's
`mount_binding_problems` (EXT-BASE, EXT-DATA; an unresolvable extra mount is ignored); S6: an interpreter path through
a loop, missing, or under `/proc/1/root` is in no EXT-113 tree. `test_paths.TestRealpath.test_fixed_values` (T1, T2):
values 3.12 and 3.13 both give for paths through the loop and below a regular file, and a 30 s `SIGALRM` watchdog per
test. `test_r18_clauses.CollectTwoClauses.test_same_arm_through_a_link_refused` (S4): one arm passed again through a
symbolic link is `passed twice`. C1: the launch fixtures run the container as `_ext.container_user()` (the running uid
and its primary group; `<user>` in `fixtures/ext/launch-request.golden.yaml` and `launch-command.golden.txt`), so the
suite runs as any non-root user; it skips those tests as root, for a uid without a user database entry, or when the
primary group owns a Docker socket (with `GATEDTRAIN_REQUIRE_CONTAINER_USER=1`, set in CI, they fail instead).

**Review of PR 2** (`docs/findings.md`, "Review of PR 2"). Arm files are read through `ext/armfs.py`: `items.jsonl`,
`eval_meta.json` and `merge.json` once each without following links (`ArmSnapshot`), every check parses those bytes
and `ext-collect` writes them into the run; outputs and files are opened with `O_PATH` and only a regular file is
reopened for reading; output paths are canonical; a merged arm's sources lie under the sources root (the output root
or `ext-collect --sources-root`, MM-5). `tests/test_arm_reads.py` (19): in-process `ext-collect` with arm files
swapped after the checks (the run holds the checked bytes, no `merge.json` link copied), an output swapped for a link
or rewritten (refused, no run) and an output read error (EXT-ARM, not `--runs-root`); provenance sources under the
root accepted, outside it or with no root refused without a hash in the message, a changed source `differs from the
recorded hash`, a FIFO or linked source file and a source directory through a link refused (under a watchdog), and
`ext-collect` without `--sources-root` refused for sources outside the output root; a linked `eval_meta.json` refused
without its content, FIFO arm files never block, a file swapped for a FIFO after inspection never read, no open
without `O_PATH` returns a descriptor of anything but a directory or regular file, a device node (root only) not a
regular file, a 0o111 directory walked; NUL, lone surrogate, `.` and empty components in an output path refused by
`read_lines` and by `ext-merge-eval` (exit 3, no traceback); `merge.json` records the hashes of the bytes merged; the
`GATEDTRAIN_REQUIRE_CONTAINER_USER` switch. `test_r18_clauses.CollectTwoClauses` (2): one arm passed as `arm` and
`lnk` from a working directory deeper than `PATH_MAX` is passed twice; with two clauses, arm files swapped after the
checks change nothing and an output swapped for a link refuses. CollectCase tests pass `--sources-root` (the test
root). Round 2 (`docs/findings.md`, "Review of PR 2, round 2"): `test_arm_reads.py` (+18):
`TestFromNelReadsOnce` (a FIFO `full_config.yaml` refused under a watchdog, a linked one refused, a config swapped
after the checks not recorded), `TestSourcesRoot` (a relative root, a root that is a link, the fix names
`--sources-root`, the normal-form guard, the `nel_benchmark` and `run_dir` messages; with `TestProvenanceSources`'
tests inherited), `TestSnapshotIsWhatIsChecked` (files changed after the snapshot, the arm replaced before the copy,
the size cap, no descriptor leaked); the provenance refusal tests of `test_ext_hardening` and `test_ext_review2`
assert their reason. Suite: 1,369 tests, 40 skipped in CI (among them the root-only device-node test).

### 11.35 Resumable external runs (EXT-RES-1 to EXT-RES-7; design revision 22, 2026-11-20)

An external NeMo RL run preempted by the scheduler resumes from its last committed cut, and `ext-collect` counts consumption per segment (05 §12.4, linear chains only; 02 §2.1). Old layouts are not re-collected: new adapters accept only the new layout.

A run is the new layout only when the first line of its `attempts.jsonl` (split at `\n`, `\r\n` or `\r`, as the reader splits) names the schema id of the header `{"schema": "gatedtrain.ext.attempts", "version": 1}`; the readers skip the header, and a first line that names the schema id but is not exactly that header (another or a non-integer version, other keys, a byte order mark) refuses (EXT-RES-1). Any other `attempts.jsonl` (the launchers' older formats, for example `{"attempt", "started_at", "argv"}` or start/end lines without a header) or none is the old layout and is collected as before, with attempt id "0".

Run-side `utc` values in `attempts.jsonl` are written as `%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ`; readers also accept ISO 8601 UTC with fractional seconds and `+00:00`, and compare at whole seconds against the guard log (second resolution).

| ID | Subject | Rule | Test file |
|---|---|---|---|
| EXT-RES-1 | Attempt records and guard alignment | Ledger `attempt_id` is `<run_id>.<A>` and `controller_epoch` is A, with parent `(A-1, branch_step)`. The guard events of the run's launch label and the run `start` lines of `attempts.jsonl` align one to one in order; a guard `START` without a `start` line is an empty attempt, allowed only if it ended `PREEMPTED`. Start times do not decrease from one attempt to the next, and each attempt's guard attempt comes after the previous attempt's; otherwise the run refuses. Any guard `END rc!=0` refuses. | `tests/test_ext_attempts.py` |
| EXT-RES-2 | Restore is the newest committed cut | Attempt A+1 restores the newest committed cut of attempts 1..A; any other restore (a rollback) is refused. The restored cut's `cut.json` carries the integer `last_step_included` the `start` line names (design 5.1). | `tests/test_ext_attempts.py` |
| EXT-RES-3 | Non-final attempts preempted, final ok | Every non-final attempt has no `end` line and a guard `PREEMPTED`; the final attempt has `end` with `status: "ok"` and guard `END rc=0` (EXT-53). | `tests/test_ext_attempts.py` |
| EXT-RES-4 | Kept steps exactly once | After records past a non-final attempt's last committed cut are set aside, the kept records cover steps 1..`max_steps` exactly once. The final segment ends at `run_args.json` `max_steps`, or at the last cut without one; a `max_steps` that is not an integer or is below the last cut refuses, and so does a record beyond the final segment's end (never dropped). `ext-collect` checks the adapter's result itself: a kept row lies in its segment's steps, an orphaned row follows a non-final segment's end, the kept rows it writes cover 1..`max_steps` from one attempt each (a `stage`-precision result writes none), `steps_redone` is a non-negative integer or absent, and the collector's `parent_ref` replaces the adapter's. | `tests/test_ext_attempts.py`, `tests/test_ext_resume_collect.py` |
| EXT-RES-5 | Orphaned records in the ledger and impact answers | Records after a non-final attempt's last committed cut stay in the ledger with `status: "orphaned"`; every rollup and impact answer excludes them except the orphaned counts and step ranges. Checkpoints are attributed from kept rows only (a cut covers a kept row when its `last_step_included` is at or after the row's step; kept records after the last cut belong to no checkpoint). The `--json` answer adds `orphaned: {count, attempts: {attempt_id: [[first, last], ...]}}` only for resumed runs: over the selected runs whose ledger holds a row with `status: "orphaned"`, each run-qualified attempt id (`<run_id>.<A>`) lists its orphaned steps of the shard as inclusive contiguous runs, and `count` is the number of orphaned records of the shard in those same runs. A shard consumed only in orphaned steps lists no checkpoint. Skeleton and Tier B records after the last cut (attempt "0", in `lineage/orphaned.parquet` only) stay in `counts` and `ranges` and add no section, so old-layout, single-attempt, Skeleton and Tier B answers keep their keys (fixture `tests/fixtures/ext_resume_old_layout_impact.json`). The text answer prints the section's runs as `orphaned_steps: attempt <id> steps 5-7, 9`; the R11A-19 `orphaned_range` lines stay first-last per attempt and shard. The consumption ledger's `schema_version` is 1.1 (it adds `controller_epoch` and `status`); a 1.0 ledger without them reads as kept rows. | `tests/test_ext_resume_collect.py`, `tests/test_r11_lineage.py` |
| EXT-RES-6 | Optimizer state saved for a resumed run | A recipe's full profiles set `save_optimizer=true` and `save_period` 10 with a cut at every save (smoke: `save_period` 5), so a resumed run restores optimizer state. A recipe adapter enforces it: through its `chain_problems` (or `consumption_problems`) hook, `ext-collect` refuses a run with more than one non-empty attempt whose `run_args.json` shows `save_optimizer` false (the settings apply to every run that may resume). The framework itself does not read these settings. | not tested here: a recipe adapter's own tests (its `chain_problems` / `consumption_problems` and its run-side settings) |
| EXT-RES-7 | Cut repair recorded and valid | At attempt start, a published `step_N` without a complete cut has its cut rebuilt before training and the `start` line records `repaired: true`; the rebuilt cut is valid (`last_step_included` = N; its files match its `cut.json`). An incomplete step directory (no published `step_N`, for example only `tmp_step_N`) is never repaired: it is ignored as NeMo RL ignores it, the restore falls back to the previous committed cut, and no `repaired` flag is written. A recipe adapter enforces the collect-side check: through its `chain_problems` (or `consumption_problems`) hook, `ext-collect` refuses a cut marked `repaired: true` whose step directory is incomplete or whose files do not match its `cut.json`. The framework only reads the `repaired` flag (EXT-RES-1, EXT-RES-2). | not tested here: a recipe adapter's own tests (its `chain_problems` / `consumption_problems` and the repair at attempt start) |

### 11.36 Analysis code version separate from the package version (0.4.0; design revision 24, 2026-11-22)

Every registration records `analysis_code_version` and is refused (REG-FIELD) when it differs from the running code's `gatedtrain.ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION` (05 section 3.7). Up to 0.3.0 that was `gatedtrain <__version__>`, so every package release would have invalidated every existing registration. From 0.4.0 it is `gatedtrain <ANALYSIS_VERSION>`, a separate constant that names the analysis code (gate statistics in `gates.py`, registration semantics in `registration.py`) and changes only when that code's behaviour changes. In 0.4.0 `__version__` is 0.4.0 and `ANALYSIS_VERSION` stays 0.3.0: since v0.3.0 `gates.py` changed one docstring and `registration.py` added the optional `base_model` field and the AI-native evaluator ids' hash resolution, neither of which changes a registration that does not use them. In 0.5.0 `__version__` is 0.5.0 and `ANALYSIS_VERSION` still 0.3.0: neither file changed since v0.4.0.

Identities that a registration pins follow the analysis version: `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION`, and the stand-in evaluator's identity hash (`runner.stub_evaluator_hash`, the Tier A deciding evaluator's `manifest_hash`), so this release leaves the current demo and fixture registrations and the demo specs unchanged. That does not make a Tier A stand-in registration written with 0.3.0 valid: the hash also covers the stand-in code files, and `stubs/model.py` changed after v0.3.0 (oneDNN turned off in the CPU demo), so the demo registration's `manifest_hash` changed from `sha256:e7d57d2f...` (v0.3.0) to `sha256:b09acedc...`, and a 0.3.0 stand-in registration gates MISSING (`evaluator_hash_mismatch`) and must be registered again. Registrations whose evaluators are Tier B, external-run or recipe evaluators keep their resolved hashes and stay valid. Identities that record which package wrote something follow `__version__`: the lockfile backends' `package_version`, the ledger `writer_id` (`gatedtrain.ledger@<__version__>`), the keeper's `hello` `keeper_version`, the `setup keeper` install pin `gatedtrain==<__version__>`, and `--version`. No reader compares these with the running package.

**Tests.** `test_rename.TestAnalysisVersion`: `__version__` is 0.5.0 while `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION` stays `gatedtrain 0.3.0`, and the stand-in evaluator hash does not change with `__version__` but does with `ANALYSIS_VERSION`. `test_rename.TestNewNames.test_identifiers` checks the ledger `writer_id` against `__version__`; `test_e2e_slow` checks the report's `analysis_code_version` against `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION` and the lockfile backends' `package_version` against `__version__`; `test_r18_clauses` builds its registration with `ANALYSIS_CODE_VERSION`; `test_stubs_docs_tierb.test_version` checks `__version__`.

## 12. AI-native step 1 (AN-1 to AN-10)

Design revision 21, `05-evaluation-and-lineage.md` §18. Step 1 runs on a CPU only synthetic pack. The trust backend is
`InProcessTrust` (`sealed_guarantee`: "unsealed (step 1): the operator process can read the sealed items, and every
stage can read and rewrite the trust records and the attempt log") and the alpha ledger is `FixedAlphaLedger` (alpha
0.05, one attempt per project; the alpha is per project in step 1). The keeper daemon and the alpha-investing variant are
step 2 and are not tested here.

| Rule | Behaviour pinned | Test file |
|---|---|---|
| AN-1 | `--json` (before the subcommand) prints one object with `schema` `gatedtrain.cli_result`, `schema_version`, `command`, `ok`, `exit`, `data`, `problems` (`rule`, `field`, `message`, `fix`) and `warnings`; nothing else on stdout, stderr empty; the exit code is the text mode's; no sealed content. The legacy `impact ... --json` flag (after the subcommand) keeps its own shape, the bare impact object on stdout (R10A-9); only the top-level `--json` gives the envelope, whose `data` is that object. EXT-85 override warnings print first on stderr in text mode and stay in `warnings` of an `INTERNAL` envelope | `tests/test_an_json.py` |
| AN-2 | Project store: created once, `project.yaml` fields, schema id on every record, invalid record refused | `tests/test_an_project.py` |
| AN-3 | Stage protocol: declared schemas, `plan` without side effects, `check` problems in the AN-1 form; a stage writes its outputs under its pipeline ref name (`ctx.stage`) and an output under another stage name is refused, so two refs of one stage type keep apart | `tests/test_an_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_an_synthetic_pack.py` |
| AN-4 | Pipeline spec refused when a trust stage is missing, duplicated, replaced or out of order, or when a stage name is not a safe directory name or holds '.' | `tests/test_an_pipeline.py` |
| AN-5 | Same inputs and decision give identical artifacts or a refusal; interrupted stage resumes from records | `tests/test_an_machine.py` |
| AN-6 | `intake_seal`: deterministic split, only the allowed views pass on, unlabelled data refused, small-data warning (planning SD of the paired difference sqrt(0.5) ≈ 0.71 for `exact_match`, the independence bound at p = 0.5; expected sizes computed from it) | `tests/test_an_intake.py` |
| AN-7 | `preregister`: registration fields, metric from the library, nothing trains before confirmation, amendment marks `post_hoc`; a base model reference without a `generator` an installed scenario pack serves is refused before training | `tests/test_an_intake.py` |
| AN-8 | `evaluate_sealed`: records out, no item content out; the stub states its guarantee; one sealed scoring per project (a claim in the trust store bound to the candidate's model reference hash and the registration; once it exists every further scoring is refused, for the same reference too, since a mutable reference can hide new weights; a done stage with unchanged inputs is still reused; the report lists every `evaluate_sealed` attempt) | `tests/test_an_gate.py` |
| AN-9 | `gate` on the sealed records: item-set hash check, `post_hoc`, `FixedAlphaLedger` refuses a second attempt, attempt log | `tests/test_an_gate.py` |
| AN-10 | `report` from records only; a known-limits list (`sealed_guarantee`, the per-project alpha, the `metric_confirmed` flag, that a model reference must content-address its weights, the number of `evaluate_sealed` attempts) in `report.json` and `report.md`; unrecorded numbers refused; spent-alpha fixes ask for new sealed data, not a new project | `tests/test_an_gate.py` |
| all | End to end on the synthetic pack | `tests/test_an_e2e.py`, `tests/test_an_synthetic_pack.py` |

`gatedtrain project` (AN-11). `create <root> --goal --data --pipeline [--gpu-hours]`, `plan`, `run [--decision FILE]`,
`status`, `report`. `run gate` exits 0 on PASS and 1 otherwise; a refusal (an earlier stage not done, MM-38 at
`smoke`, alpha spent) exits 3; `report` exits 0 on PASS, 1 on another status and 3 when the report stage is not
done. `--gpu-hours` (finite, at least 0) is only recorded. `create` copies the pipeline to `<root>/pipeline.yaml` and records its sha256; a changed copy is refused (AN-5). A stage refusal raised in `run` before the stage writes a record, spends alpha or claims a trust record is logged as `refused` and never changes derived state (after any of these it is `failed`); `status` shows the recorded gate result (`gate_recorded`) and the alpha ledger apart from the stage state. The synthetic pack is not registered, so `tests/test_an_e2e.py` runs the CLI
through `tests/fixtures/ainative/run_cli.py`, which injects it like `inject_pack()`; the data is
`tests/fixtures/ainative/toy.jsonl` (800 rows, regenerated by `make_toy.py`). The test file pins: `noise=0` ends in
a PASS report with every stage `done`; `noise=1` is refused at `smoke` (MM-38, exit 3), the gate never runs and
`status` and `report` do not claim PASS; re-running the whole sequence is a no-op (same outputs, no new attempt
lines); a second gate attempt (changed decision) and an amended registration after the gate are refused because the
alpha is spent. Under the top-level `--json` an uncaught exception is the AN-1 object (rule `INTERNAL`, exit 1, the
class name only, no traceback), pinned with the usage-error and `--json` NOT_PASS (exit 1) tests in
`tests/test_an_json.py`.

## 13. AI-native step 2 (AN-12 to AN-19)

Design revision 23, `05-evaluation-and-lineage.md` §18.3-§18.5 and §18.9. Step 2 adds the keeper: a daemon under its
own OS user that alone holds the sealed items, registrations, claims, per-item records, the alpha-investing ledger
(keyed by the sealed set) and an Ed25519 signing key, reached over a Unix socket (Linux only; the keeper tests skip
elsewhere). CI has no sudo and no second user, so every test here runs the keeper as a subprocess of the test user
(`--owner-uid` = the test uid) on a socket in a fresh temporary directory, started through
`tests/fixtures/ainative/run_cli.py keeper serve ...` so the synthetic pack is injected as in step 1; the keeper then
states the same-user guarantee ("... the records are signed, but nothing is sealed (a test or development setup)").
What CI therefore cannot show is the separate-user property itself: that the agent-side user cannot read the state
directory, cannot connect as another uid, and that the root-owned keeper environment cannot be changed from the
user's account. That is checked once by hand on a host with a real keeper user (the manual check of step 2's last
task: `setup keeper` commands run with sudo, a connection as another uid refused with AN-12, a group-writable keeper
environment refused at start with AN-13, an edited project record failing `verify` with AN-14). The step 1 path
(`InProcessTrust`, `FixedAlphaLedger`) keeps its tests in section 12 unchanged.

| Rule | Behaviour pinned | Test file |
|---|---|---|
| AN-6 (extended) | A sealed label may be a list (for `set_f1`); intake under the keeper splits in the keeper and plans power at alpha 0.02375, so the same data need a larger sealed part than at 0.05; data whose split would most likely be INCONCLUSIVE are refused before anything is stored unless the decision sets `accept_inconclusive`, which the driver forwards | `tests/test_an_scorers.py`, `tests/test_an_sealed_eval.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py` |
| AN-8 (extended) | Each sealed evaluation spends alpha first: the claim (candidate model hash, registration hash, alpha_j) is recorded and signed before any item is scored; the keeper scores and keeps the per-item records, the driver receives only the status (PASS, FAIL, INCONCLUSIVE or MISSING) and the alpha spent and earned; an exception from pack code reaches the driver as its class name only, and a refusal after the grant fails the attempt and costs its alpha; so does any other exception after the grant (a failed write of the per-item records), which settles not passed and returns the spend's envelopes, naming its class only; no sealed input reaches any output, project file or the keeper's log; the in-process path keeps one scoring per project | `tests/test_an_sealed_eval.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-9 (extended) | The keeper computes the gate at the attempt's alpha_j and signs the gate record; a granted attempt always settles (crash, MISSING and INCONCLUSIVE are not passed and cost their alpha); an attempt open at a keeper restart is gated if its complete records are stored, else settles not passed; a PASS ends the project's attempts (a later evaluate refused); through the CLI the gate exits 1 on INCONCLUSIVE at 0.02375 and 0 on the rerun's PASS at 0.011586107554417413 | `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-10 (extended) | The keeper builds and signs `report.json` from its own records: headline the PASS attempt's, else the latest attempt's; every attempt listed; key fingerprint; the keeper's known limits (`sealed_guarantee`, original data file, the intake and registration oracles, metric confirmation, model reference, alpha rule with its mFDR guarantee and the assumption that each test is valid given the earlier outcomes, number of sealed evaluations); `report.md` rendered from `report.json` alone; the ledger lists each attempt's alpha, cost and payout | `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-12 | Keeper protocol and peer credentials: one JSON request per line, one response with problems in the AN-1 shape; only the owner uid or the keeper's own uid may connect; a line over the cap, non-JSON, a JSON array, an unknown op or a silent peer gets one problem response or a closed connection and the keeper keeps serving; a second keeper on one state directory refuses to start; the client refuses an unreachable socket without a traceback, and a bound project whose keeper is unreachable is refused, never run on the step 1 path; a client queued behind another client's long evaluation waits with the long-op deadline (`intake`, `evaluate`, `gate` and `report` are long ops) | `tests/test_an_keeper_protocol.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_cli.py` |
| AN-13 | Keeper environment and model file formats: the keeper refuses to start when its code, environment, state directory or socket directory is open to another uid (state directory mode 0755 refused, a socket directory with mode 0777 refused); the environment record lists the distributions it runs with their file hashes; pickle-based or unknown model file formats are refused before any alpha is spent. Known limit: a module imported lazily (first imported after the start-up check) that lies outside gatedtrain, the installed packs and gatedtrain's runtime dependencies is not checked; site-packages is not walked as a whole | `tests/test_an_keeper_env.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_protocol.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py` |
| AN-14 | Signed records, the trust anchor and `verify`: Ed25519 over the canonical JSON of `{kind, key_fingerprint, record}`; a key file with group or other mode bits refused; a wrong schema, kind, fingerprint or signature refused; `report` refuses a bad signature; a project bound to one key refuses a keeper serving another, and a deleted binding never falls back to the step 1 path; `verify` checks against the key the keeper serves (or `--fingerprint`), exits 3 on a replaced `trust/keeper.pub` or binding fingerprint, on another project's report and (with `--keeper`) on a record the keeper never signed or a deleted record, and exits 1 saying it checked consistency only without either anchor, or against a keeper whose `hello` says it runs as the owner's uid (`same_user`; exit 0 when it says otherwise); the driver adds to its receipts every envelope the keeper signed for the project that they lack, after each evaluate, gate and report and on `project report` (an evaluation the client gave up on, then a retrain, still verifies with `--keeper` and `--fingerprint`), and the missing-record message says the record was signed but not received, or deleted; every signed copy edited by one value (the registration, each receipt line, the gate result, `report.json`, `report.md`) fails `verify --keeper` with exit 3; `project run report` on an edited registration refuses (AN-2, or AN-10 against the keeper's copy when the hash pin is rewritten too). Known limit: `verify --fingerprint` (offline) cannot detect a deleted record and says so | `tests/test_an_signing.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_cli.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-15 | Alpha-investing ledger: W0 = 0.05 * (1 - 0.05) = 0.0475, bid alpha_j = W_{j-1} / 2, cost alpha_j / (1 - alpha_j) when not passed, payout 0.05 and no cost on a PASS, refusal when alpha_j < 0.005 (a fourth failing attempt from W0 is refused and nothing is scored), planning alpha 0.02375; a replayed ledger reproduces every stored number and a tampered or re-signed ledger with wrong arithmetic is refused; a bid never costs more than the wealth (alpha_j = min(W / 2, W / (1 + W)); W = 3 bids 0.75, cost 3); concurrent claims on one sealed set are serialised (no double spend); a second project on the same data bids from the shared wealth (0.011586107554417413 after one attempt that did not pass); `project status` reads the keeper's ledger | `tests/test_an_alpha_investing.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_cli.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-16 | Sealed items tracked one by one by the hash of the canonical input: a byte-identical data file reuses its sealed set; other data containing a sealed item (reshuffled, relabelled or with one new row) refused, the message naming a count and no row; inputs released as training are forced into the training part of later splits; after `report` every claim on the sealed set, from any project, is refused, and a new project on the same data is refused at intake. Known limit, stated in the report: opening a project is free and a refused intake stores nothing, so intake refusals answer whether candidate inputs were sealed before (counts, unthrottled), and a registration refused for its scorer names how many sealed labels it cannot take | `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_attempts.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |
| AN-17 | Scorer library (`exact_match`, `numeric_match`, `normalised_match`, `set_f1`, `field_f1`; ids `<name>.ainative@0.1`, higher is better, range [0, 1], planning SD sqrt(0.5)) and pack scorers by reference (unknown name, library-name clash, ext evaluator row id clash, code hash mismatch, bad parameters refused); a scorer with a planning SD above the sealed set's sizing refused; a sealed label the scorer cannot take refused at registration before any spend, naming a count only | `tests/test_an_scorers.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py` |
| AN-18 | Metric confirmation record: `keeper confirm` records the plain sentence, signed, with time and peer uid, only after `yes` (prompt on stderr) or `--yes`; with nothing to read on stdin (EOF before an answer) it refuses with "no answer was read" and names `--yes`; under `--json` it never prompts and refuses without `--yes`; a confirmation from another uid refused; a registration without a matching confirmation (scorer, parameters, direction, margin) refused | `tests/test_an_keeper_intake.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_trust.py`, `tests/test_an_keeper_cli.py` |
| AN-19 | `setup keeper` prints the privileged commands, the systemd unit and the fingerprint command, and runs or writes nothing; an unpinned package, a bad user name, a relative path, or a path holding `%`, `$`, whitespace, a quote or a backslash (rewritten or split by systemd in the unit) is refused | `tests/test_an_keeper_cli.py` |
| all | End to end through `run_cli.py --json` (empty stderr on every call) with a keeper subprocess: a project PASS, `verify --keeper` exit 1 (the test keeper runs as the owner's uid) and `verify --fingerprint` exit 0; two attempts in one project; two projects sharing one sealed set and a third refused after the report; tampering with every signed copy; no sealed input anywhere | `tests/test_an_keeper_e2e.py` |

## 14. AI-native step 3a (AN-20 to AN-25, EXT-263)

Design revision 25, `05-evaluation-and-lineage.md` §18.9 and §18.10. Step 3a runs the user's jobs and the keeper's
sealed evaluation on rootless Docker, detects rootful Docker access by the user's account and downgrades the keeper's
guarantee when it finds it, shares one rootless variant of the launch rules between `ext-launch` and the local
backend, and runs a scenario pack's batch generator in the keeper's inference container on a copy of the model files
the keeper hashed. CI has no rootless Docker and no GPU: the container runtimes are fakes
(`gatedtrain.testing.fake_runtime.FakeRuntime`, injected through `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_BACKEND_RUNTIME` and
`GATEDTRAIN_TEST_KEEPER_RUNTIME` in `tests/fixtures/ainative/run_cli.py`), the GPU probe is a fake, and the host state
the detection reads is an injected `HostView` (a tree under a temporary directory, `GATEDTRAIN_TEST_HOST_VIEW`), so
no test depends on the runner's own Docker sockets or groups (the CI runner user is in the `docker` group). `setup
host` is tested on the printed text only (`bash -n` on a copy; nothing runs). What CI therefore cannot show (the real
rootless daemons, the group removal, the detection on a real host, the owner's containers unable to read the keeper's
state, and a CPU job and the keeper's container on the real daemons) is checked once by hand on a host with a real
keeper user (step 3a's last, manual task). The step 1 and step 2 tests in sections 12 and 13 keep passing; the
rootful launch rules keep their golden commands, configuration hashes and labels.

| Rule | Behaviour pinned | Test file |
|---|---|---|
| EXT-263 | `rootless = true` in the host file (a TOML boolean only, else EXT-HOST): the container user must be `0:0`; the output directory and a sidecar's spool must be owned by the owner; the owner's primary group must not own a rootful Docker socket, and the owner's own socket under `/run/user/<uid>/` is ignored; GPU options `--device nvidia.com/gpu=all` instead of `--runtime=nvidia --gpus all`; the launch record carries `"rootless": true` and a record written under the other setting is refused; the host configuration hash changes only with the key (unchanged golden value without it); `--recheck` refuses, by a stat only, when the owner's socket under `/run/user/<uid>/` is missing, not a socket or owned by another uid; a sidecar under rootless runs as `0:0` with an owner-owned spool | `tests/test_ext_rootless.py` |
